Confirmed this empirically (see discriminating-power verification below) before shipping, and replaced the 4 diagonal-corner checks with 4 edge-MIDPOINT checks instead — `(3,8)`/`(12,8)`/`(8,3)`/`(8,12)`, one immediately outside each of the glyph rect's 4 edges at that edge's own midpoint — which correctly isolate an X-only or Y-only error. All 5 checks (1 inside + 4 edge-midpoint) pass with the exact predicted colors on the first attempt with the corrected check points. **Discriminating power independently verified, and this is exactly what caught the corner-check weakness**: sabotaged `EasyGLSpriteBatchBackend::Draw`'s destination-rect X coordinate by a constant `+3.0f` offset — with the ORIGINAL diagonal-corner checks, all 5 still incorrectly reported PASS (the exact silent-failure mode being guarded against); rebuilt with the CORRECTED edge-midpoint checks and reran the same sabotage — exactly the right-edge check (`(12,8)`) failed as predicted (4/5), the other 4 (including the still-correct top/bottom edge checks, unaffected by an X-only shift) stayed green; restored the sabotage (confirmed clean `git diff` — zero net production-code change) and reconfirmed all 5 pass. That fix already applies to every backend including EasyGL (it lives in shared code), already spot-checked via Task 694's own existing EasyGL `SpriteBatch`/`SpriteFont` unit-test regression (62/62 passed) — but never via a genuine EasyGL PIXEL test verifying the actual rendered output, until now. New `examples/easygl_spritefont_effects_flip_test.cpp`, registered as `EasyGL_SpriteFont_EffectsFlip`: 2 checks, both pass with the exact predicted colors on the first attempt — confirming the shared fix renders correctly through EasyGL's own draw backend, not just SDL_Renderer's. **Discriminating power independently verified via a targeted sabotage of the SHARED code Task 694 already covers on SDL_Renderer**: temporarily hardcoded `SpriteBatch.cpp`'s `const int effIdx = static_cast<int>(effects);` to `const int effIdx = 0;` — forcing the axis-direction lookup tables to always resolve to the unflipped (`None`) case regardless of the actual `effects` parameter, precisely reproducing the original pre-Task-694 bug (the requested flip is silently ignored for placement purposes) — rebuilt and reran, and BOTH checks failed as predicted (0/2, glyphs rendered unflipped and unreordered — `'A'` still left, `'B'` still right); restored (confirmed clean `git diff` — zero net production-code change) and reconfirmed both pass. Since this task shipped zero net production-code change and the sabotaged logic is shared, cross-backend code already independently verified correct on SDL_Renderer by Task 694, no separate cross-backend spot-check was needed beyond the full EasyGL regression below. | 429 | Verify rotation/origin/scale with `DrawString`                                                   | ✅      | **CLOSED — confirmed as the EasyGL counterpart of Task 694's own "Test 2"; no bug found, since this code path was already correct BEFORE Task 694's own flip fix and untouched by it.** Task 694's write-up cross-references Tasks 428/429 as the EasyGL ports of its 2 sub-tests: 428 = "Test 1" (flip, already closed), 429 = "Test 2" (rotation/origin/scale, this row). Unlike Task 428 (which independently re-verifies a SHARED bug Task 694 had to fix), this task's own subject — `DrawString`'s per-glyph `offsetX`/`offsetY` → `localX`/`localY` → `scaledX = localX*scale.X`/`scaledY = localY*scale.Y` → `rotX`/`rotY` (rotation applied via `cosR`/`sinR`) → final `dest` rect formula — was never touched by Task 694's flip fix at all (that fix only affects `baseOffset`/`axisDirX`/`axisDirY`/`axisIsMirroredX`/`axisIsMirroredY`, all upstream of this math and already provably neutral for `effects=None`). Direct EasyGL port of Task 694's "Test 2": single glyph `'A'` (White, 8×8), `origin=(4,4)` (centers the pivot on the unscaled glyph), `scale=(2,2)`, `rotation=0`, `position=(10,10)`. Hand-derived: `localX = curOffset.X(0)+cCrop.X(0)-origin.X(4) = -4`; `scaledX = -4*2 = -8`; `dest.X = round(10-8) = 2`; `dest.Width = round(8*2) = 16` → `dest = (2,2,16,16)`. New `examples/easygl_spritefont_effects_rotation_scale_test.cpp`, registered as `EasyGL_SpriteFont_EffectsRotationScale`: 3 checks — `(10,10)` inside the scaled glyph → White, `(0,0)`/`(20,20)` outside → Black. All 3 pass with the exact predicted colors on the first attempt, confirming this already-correct math also renders correctly through EasyGL's own draw backend, not just SDL_Renderer's. **Discriminating power independently verified via a targeted sabotage of the SHARED code Task 694 already covers on SDL_Renderer**: temporarily dropped the `* scale.X`/`* scale.Y` multiplication from `scaledX`/`scaledY` (`const float scaledX = localX;`/`const float scaledY = localY;`), simulating a bug where the origin-relative offset is never scaled before being applied to the destination position — rebuilt and reran, and exactly the `(20,20)`-outside check failed as predicted (2/3): the sabotaged, unscaled offset shifts the glyph rect from `(2,2,16,16)` to `(6,6,16,16)` (occupying `[6,22)` on both axes), moving it diagonally closer to `(20,20)` until that corner is newly inside the (still correctly-sized, since `dest.Width`/`dest.Height` at lines 469-470 were untouched) glyph rect. **Worth noting explicitly, since Task 424 established the opposite lesson for its own single-axis-only sabotage**: these 2 background checks ARE diagonal corners (the exact shape Task 424 found too weak to catch a *single-axis-only* mis-placement) — but this specific sabotage shifts BOTH axes simultaneously (dropping scale from `scaledX` AND `scaledY` together), so a diagonal corner genuinely is sensitive to it here; this is not a contradiction of Task 424's finding, just a reminder that diagonal-corner checks remain a poor default choice in general (they only catch bugs that happen to move the correct diagonal direction) — restored (confirmed clean `git diff` — zero net production-code change) and reconfirmed all 3 pass. Since this task shipped zero net production-code change and the sabotaged logic is shared, cross-backend code already independently verified correct on SDL_Renderer by Task 694, no separate cross-backend spot-check was needed beyond the full EasyGL regression below. Full regression (full rebuild first, `-k` to skip the known unrelated `cna_demo_xact` Content-copy error): `ctest` 4437/4448 passed, 2 skipped, 3 failed — exactly the established EasyGL baseline (`EasyGL_MRT_TwoAttachments`/`EasyGL_GraphicsDevice_ReferenceStencil`/`easy-gl-resource-smoke-tests`, first documented at Task 902, reconfirmed at Tasks 737/424-428), exact name match, zero new failures; the new `EasyGL_SpriteFont_EffectsRotationScale` test itself passes. **This closes the entire Tasks 424-429 SpriteFont-pixel-verification range on EasyGL, directly mirroring the already-closed Tasks 690-694 range on SDL_Renderer.** |

Final support matrix explicitly notes Vulkan/Bgfx have NOT been given the same dedicated SpriteFont pixel-verification pass as SDL_Renderer/EasyGL (the underlying `DrawString` logic is shared C++, so this is a coverage/confidence gap, not a known bug) — listed as open follow-up work, not closed here (out of this phase's own SDL_Renderer/EasyGL scope). No code/tests changed; no `ctest` regression needed to close a docs-only task (matches this session's own Task 421/725/731 precedent). **This closes Phase 48 in full (Tasks 421-430).** |

## Phase 49 — Model, ModelMesh, and ModelBone correctness

| #   | Task                                                                                   | Status | Notes                     |
| --- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------ | ------------------------- |
| 431 | Audit `Model`, `ModelMesh`, `ModelMeshPart`, `ModelBone`, collections against FNA      | ✅      | **CLOSED — audit complete, starts Phase 49. Property/method surface matches FNA closely across all 4 core types; found several real, previously-undocumented behavioral deviations and one genuine API-completeness gap (deferred to new Task 916).** Compared `Model`/`ModelMesh`/`ModelMeshPart`/`ModelBone`/`ModelBoneCollection`/`ModelMeshCollection`/`ModelMeshPartCollection`/`ModelEffectCollection` line-by-line against FNA's own source files. **`Model`**: `Bones`/`Meshes`/`Root`/`Tag` all match (read-only, correct collection types, no public `Root` setter matching FNA's `internal set`). `CopyAbsoluteBoneTransformsTo`/`CopyBoneTransformsFrom`/`CopyBoneTransformsTo` multiply order and per-bone logic match FNA exactly (`std::vector` size checks replace FNA's null/length exceptions, a reasonable C++ adaptation). **`ModelMeshPart`**: full match — `Effect`'s setter faithfully ports FNA's real, nontrivial add/remove-from-parent's-`Effects`-collection logic (checks whether any sibling part still references the old effect before removing it). **`ModelBone`**: `Name`/`Index`/`Transform`/`Parent` all match; `AddChild` atomically sets both `Parent` and appends to `Children` in one call (FNA's own `AddChild` only appends, `Parent` is set independently by `ModelReader`) — a reasonable consolidation for CNA's hand-build convenience API, not a bug, but worth noting as a behavioral difference from FNA's two-step process. **`ModelEffectCollection`**: the most complete of the 4 collection types — `operator[](int)`/`Count`/`Contains`/`begin()`/`end()`, `Add`/`Remove` correctly `NOXNA` (FNA's are `internal`) — full match. **Real, previously-undocumented deviations found** (added to `CHECKLIST.md`'s acceptable-deviations table rather than silently left undocumented): `Model::Draw()` silently defaults a mesh's bone index to `0` when `ModelMesh::ParentBone` is `nullptr` (FNA's `mesh.ParentBone.Index` would `NullReferenceException`); `ModelMesh::Draw()` silently skips a part whose `Effect` is `nullptr` (FNA's `effect.CurrentTechnique` would also `NullReferenceException` — checking `PrimitiveCount > 0` alone, like FNA, is NOT sufficient in CNA's implementation). **This corrects an incorrect claim in Task 728's own write-up**, which stated the effect-null skip "match[es] FNA's own `ModelMesh.Draw`" — direct FNA source reading confirms it does not; both silent-default behaviors are kept as strictly-safer-than-undefined-behavior C++ adaptations (a null-pointer dereference in C++ is UB, not a catchable exception like C#'s `NullReferenceException`), not bugs needing a fix, but were undocumented until now. Task 728's own test is unaffected by this correction since it uses a real, non-null `Effect`. **Real gaps found, deferred to specific later tasks rather than fixed here** (pure audit task, no code changes): `ModelBone::Children` returns a plain `const std::vector<ModelBone*>&`, not a `ModelBoneCollection` — losing by-name lookup for a bone's children, inconsistent with `Model.Bones` which does get the real collection type — deferred to Task 432, which already covers `ModelBoneCollection` verification and can absorb this finding directly. `ModelBoneCollection`/`ModelMeshCollection` are both missing FNA's `TryGetValue(name, out value)` and `Contains(T)` (inherited from FNA's `ReadOnlyCollection<T>` base); `ModelBoneCollection` additionally has **no `begin()`/`end()` at all** (unlike `ModelMeshCollection`, which has them `NOXNA`-marked) — cannot be used in a range-based `for` loop, a genuine C++-ergonomics regression versus its own sibling collection type — also deferred to Tasks 432/433. **Genuine API-completeness gap, new tracked Task 916** (not guessed at, not blocking — a low-risk, purely-additive fix, not a judgment call): `Model`'s only non-default constructor auto-defaults `Root` to `bones[0]` with no way to specify a different root bone index — FNA's `Model` constructor never sets `Root` at all (it's assigned externally by `ModelReader` from an explicit `rootBoneIndex` field that can name *any* bone, not necessarily index 0), so any hand-built CNA model whose true root bone isn't the first one in `bones` currently cannot be represented correctly. **Minor style finding**: `Model`'s default constructor is not `NOXNA`-marked, inconsistent with its own 3-arg constructor which is. **Test-coverage gap confirmed** (already reflected in Tasks 432-434's own scope, not a new finding): zero test files exist for `Model` itself, `ModelMeshPart`, `ModelMeshCollection`, `ModelMeshPartCollection`, or `ModelEffectCollection` — only `ModelBoneTests.cpp`, `ModelBoneCollectionTests.cpp`, and `ModelMeshTests.cpp` (which never calls `Draw()`) currently exist. `SkinnedModelEXTPartTest`/`ContentManagerSkinnedModelTest` are a separate `NOXNA` extension (skinned-model-part attach/detach), out of scope for this plain-FNA-compliance audit. Docs-only task, no code changed. |

All 3 new tests (7 total in the file) pass on the first attempt — no bug found in CNA's own shipped behavior. **Discriminating power independently verified**: temporarily reversed the per-bone assignment index (`src[count-1-i]` instead of `src[i]`, simulating a bug where source and bone indices don't correspond 1:1) — rebuilt and reran, and exactly the 2 tests that depend on genuine per-bone correctness failed as predicted (5/7, the too-small-throws test and all 4 Task 435 tests stayed green since none of them depend on this specific assignment); restored (confirmed clean `git diff` — zero net production-code change) and reconfirmed all 7 pass. | 439 | Pixel test: model hierarchy transform affects child mesh                               | ✅      | **CLOSED — real, previously-undocumented API gap found and fixed while designing the test (not just a pixel-test task), then the pixel test itself confirms no bug in the fixed behavior.** While designing a fixture with a mesh explicitly parented to a non-root bone, discovered `ModelMesh::parentBone_` (already declared `friend class Model;` in `ModelMesh.hpp` for exactly this purpose) was **never actually assigned by any public construction path** — not even `Model`'s own 3-arg constructor sets it; confirmed via `grep -rn "parentBone_"` across the entire repo, finding only the private member declaration and its own read-only getter, zero write sites anywhere. This meant `ModelMesh::ParentBone` was permanently `nullptr` for every hand-built CNA model, making `Model::Draw`'s own `mesh->getParentBoneProperty() ? mesh->getParentBoneProperty()->getIndexProperty() : 0` ternary's TRUE branch **dead code**, unreachable through any existing public API — a real correctness gap, since FNA's real `ModelReader` always assigns `mesh.ParentBone = bones[meshBoneIndex]` for an arbitrary bone, not just the root (matching FNA's `ParentBone`'s `internal set`, which CNA had no equivalent internal-assignment path for at all). Confirmed by the pre-existing `ModelMeshTest.ParentBoneIsNullBeforeModelAssigns` test's own name, which explicitly documents this was always the intended design, just never implemented. **Fixed via a new, purely-additive `Model` constructor overload** (`Model(GraphicsDevice*, vector<ModelBone*> bones, vector<ModelMesh*> meshes, vector<ModelBone*> meshParentBones)`, `Model.hpp`/`Model.cpp`) that delegates to the existing 3-arg constructor then, via `Model`'s existing friend access to `ModelMesh::parentBone_`, assigns each mesh's `ParentBone` from a parallel per-mesh vector — an empty `meshParentBones` leaves every mesh's `ParentBone` `nullptr` (byte-for-byte identical to the existing 3-arg constructor's behavior, zero regression risk to any existing call site), a non-empty one must match `meshes.size()` exactly or throws `std::out_of_range` (mirroring this file's own established `Copy*`-method bounds-check convention). **3 new unit tests** in `ModelTests.cpp` (`FourArgConstructorSetsMeshParentBone`, `FourArgConstructorEmptyMeshParentBonesLeavesParentBoneNull`, `FourArgConstructorThrowsWhenMeshParentBonesSizeMismatches`) — all pass; discriminating power independently verified by sabotaging the assignment loop to a no-op — exactly the 1 dependent test failed as predicted (12/13), restored and reconfirmed 13/13. **The pixel test itself**: new `examples/easygl_model_hierarchy_child_mesh_test.cpp` (`EasyGL_Model_HierarchyChildMesh`) — a `root` bone (identity) with a `child` bone (`Translate(0.6, 0, 0)`) wired via `ModelBone::AddChild`, and 2 small non-overlapping quads of identical local shape: `meshRoot` (Red, `ParentBone` left null, defaults to bone 0 per Task 431's finding) and `meshChild` (Blue, `ParentBone` explicitly the child bone via the new 4-arg constructor). Samples the backbuffer at NDC `(0,0)` (expect Red, root's identity position) and at NDC `(0.6,0)` (expect Blue, only reachable if `Model::Draw` genuinely resolved `meshChild`'s own parent bone's ABSOLUTE transform — not just always bone 0). Both checks PASS on the first attempt — no bug found in `Model::Draw`'s own bone-resolution logic, once the mesh could actually be parented to a non-root bone at all. **Discriminating power independently verified**: temporarily sabotaged `Model::Draw`'s `boneIdx` computation to hardcode `0` regardless of `ParentBone` — rebuilt and reran, and BOTH checks failed (not just the child one): with depth testing disabled, `meshChild` now rendered unshifted, directly on top of `meshRoot`'s own quad (painted last, so it overdrew the center pixel to Blue instead of Red), and the shifted sample location correctly showed background Green instead of Blue — a stronger, whole-scene discriminating failure than a single isolated check, exactly matching the real-world visual corruption this bug would cause. Restored (confirmed `git diff --stat src/Microsoft/Xna/Framework/Graphics/Model.cpp` showed only the new, permanent 4-arg-constructor code, zero sabotage residue) and reconfirmed both checks pass again. Registered as new EasyGL `ctest` entry `EasyGL_Model_HierarchyChildMesh` in `CMakeLists.txt`, immediately after Task 438's own registration. | 440 | Document model-loading and content pipeline limitations                                | ✅      | **CLOSED — pure documentation, the final task in Phase 49 (mirrors Task 430's `docs/spritefont-support.md` and Task 731's `docs/sdl-renderer-2d-completeness.md` as the closing doc task for its own phase).** Wrote `docs/model-content-pipeline-support.md`. Summarizes Tasks 431-439's own runtime-API audit findings up front (all confirmed FNA-faithful, 1 documented safe deviation, Task 916 still open), then focuses on the piece those tasks never touched: **content loading**. Read FNA's real `ModelReader.cs` line-by-line and compared it against CNA's `ModelTypeReader` (`ContentManager.cpp`). **Key finding: CNA's Model content format is not FNA's binary `.xnb` format at all** — it's an original, NOXNA `.model.json` + raw-binary-sidecar scheme (no real XNA/FNA-produced `.xnb` model asset can ever be loaded). **Real, previously-undocumented gaps found in the loader itself** (documented, not fixed — pure docs task): the loader always synthesizes exactly ONE `ModelBone` regardless of how many the JSON's `"bones"` array actually lists (only the first entry's `"name"` is ever read) — no bone hierarchy support at all, even though the runtime API fully supports it after Tasks 431-439; every mesh is built via the OLD 3-arg constructor path, so `ModelMesh::ParentBone` is never assigned — notably, this is a gap even the just-shipped Task 439 fix doesn't close, since the loader doesn't call the new 4-arg `Model` constructor; `ModelMesh::BoundingSphere` is never populated (stays a degenerate zero-radius default for every loaded mesh); neither `Model::Tag` nor any `ModelMesh::Tag` is ever set; no `VertexBuffer`/`IndexBuffer`/`Effect` resource sharing across meshes (FNA's real `ReadSharedResource` can dedupe, CNA always allocates fresh); no `existingInstance` hot-reload support (acceptable, FNA's own use of it is pipeline-tooling-only). **Confirmed via repo-wide search: zero test or example coverage of `ModelTypeReader`/`ContentManager::Load<Model>()` anywhere** — every one of Tasks 431-439's extensive tests hand-builds `Model`/`ModelMesh`/`ModelBone` directly in C++, never through the content pipeline, which is exactly how Task 439's dead-`ParentBone` gap went unnoticed as long as it did. Cross-referenced `SkinnedModelEXT`/`docs/avatar-real-rendering-ext.md` as a deliberately separate, fully-covered system, to avoid confusing readers into thinking the Avatar pipeline's own test coverage extends to plain `Model` loading too. **This closes Phase 49 in full (Tasks 431-440)**: `Model`'s runtime API is now thoroughly audited and FNA-faithful; its content-loading path has a clearly-documented, honestly-scoped set of gaps versus both FNA and CNA's own now-more-capable runtime API, left as open follow-up work rather than silently glossed over. Docs-only, no code changed. |
| 916 | `Model`'s only non-default constructor auto-defaults `Root` to `bones[0]`, with no way to specify a different root bone index (Task 431 finding) | ⬜ | FNA's `Model` constructor never sets `Root` at all — `ModelReader` assigns it externally afterward, from an explicit `rootBoneIndex` that can name any bone, not necessarily index 0. Any hand-built CNA model whose true root isn't the first bone in `bones` currently can't be represented correctly. Low-risk, purely-additive fix (e.g. an optional `rootBoneIndex` constructor parameter defaulting to `0` to preserve the current default) — not a judgment call, not blocked on anything |
| 917 | Bgfx occlusion queries can't measure true scene-depth visibility — the query-attached submit() shares the SAME view (and depth buffer) as any other same-frame geometry, unlike bgfx's own official "26-occlusion" example, which attaches its occlusion-measurement submit() to a SEPARATE, dedicated view (Task 448 finding) | ⬜ | Task 448 fixed `BgfxOcclusionQueryBackend::Begin()`/`End()` to genuinely attach to a real `bgfx::submit(id, program, occlusionQuery)` call (previously literal no-ops) — a real, verified improvement matching bgfx's own documented API contract exactly. But bgfx's own official example additionally submits its occlusion-measurement pass to a dedicated view (view 1), separate from the "real" visible-scene view (view 0), specifically so the query's own sample count isn't polluted by other geometry drawn earlier in the same view/depth buffer. CNA's fix currently attaches the query to whichever view the game's normal 3D draw already targets (`currentViewId_`), shared with everything else drawn there that frame. Reproducing bgfx's dedicated-view pattern (likely reusing the existing `Detail::AllocateRtViewId()`/`ReleaseRtViewId()` free-list infrastructure already used for render targets, Task 910) would let a query's result reflect ONLY its own geometry's true visibility against the real scene's already-drawn depth, rather than being potentially contaminated by unrelated same-view draws. Not attempted here — a real further architecture addition, and this sandbox's software Mesa GL 2.1 (llvmpipe) renderer couldn't even discriminate whether the CURRENT (shared-view) fix changes observable behavior at all (see Task 448's own write-up), so verifying a fix for this specific gap would need a real GPU-backed environment to test against meaningfully, not this sandbox. |
| 918 | EasyGL has NO real anisotropic filtering support at all — `TextureFilter::Anisotropic` silently falls back to plain trilinear filtering (`LinearMipmapLinear`/`Linear`), with no `GL_EXT_texture_filter_anisotropic`/`glSamplerParameterf(GL_TEXTURE_MAX_ANISOTROPY_EXT, ...)` call anywhere in this backend or the underlying `easy-gl` library (Task 456 finding) | ⬜ | Found while writing Task 456's own EasyGL startup capability dump — confirmed by reading `EasyGLGraphicsBackend`'s own `ApplySamplerState`-equivalent sampler-filter mapping: `case 2: // Anisotropic` maps to the exact same `minF`/`magF` values as ordinary trilinear filtering, with `SamplerState.MaxAnisotropy` never read or forwarded to the GL sampler object at all, on either the EasyGL backend or the vendored `easy-gl` library beneath it (confirmed via a repo-wide `grep` for "Anisotropy"/"ANISOTROPY" finding zero matches in either). Vulkan (Task 454, real `samplerAnisotropy` device-feature-gated support) and Bgfx (`BGFX_SAMPLER_MIN_ANISOTROPIC`/`MAG_ANISOTROPIC` flags) both have genuine anisotropic filtering; EasyGL is the one backend where requesting `TextureFilter::Anisotropic` silently produces plain trilinear results instead, with no error, log (until this task's new capability dump), or documented limitation anywhere before now. Fixing this for real needs adding a genuine `GL_TEXTURE_MAX_ANISOTROPY_EXT` sampler-parameter call (gated behind a `GL_EXT_texture_filter_anisotropic` extension-support check, mirroring the MSAA-clamp-to-`GL_MAX_SAMPLES` pattern this same file already uses elsewhere) — a real, scoped, low-risk feature addition, not a judgment call, not blocked on anything; deferred rather than fixed here since Task 456's own scope is adding a diagnostic log, not implementing a new rendering feature. |

## Phase 50 — OcclusionQuery correctness

| #   | Task                                                                    | Status | Notes                             |
| --- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------ | --------------------------------- |
| 441 | Audit `OcclusionQuery` API against FNA                                  | ✅      | **CLOSED — audit complete, starts Phase 50. Property/method surface matches FNA's real, remarkably minimal shape; found 1 significant, previously-informally-known-but-never-formally-tracked functional gap on EACH of 2 backends (Vulkan, Bgfx), and 1 important reframing needed for the next 3 planned tasks.** Read FNA's real `OcclusionQuery.cs` line-by-line: it is a `GraphicsResource` subclass with exactly 2 properties (`IsComplete`/`PixelCount`, both one-line forwards to `FNA3D_QueryComplete`/`FNA3D_QueryPixelCount`), a constructor forwarding to `FNA3D_CreateQuery`, an overridden `Dispose(bool)` doing an `Interlocked.Exchange` swap-and-null then `FNA3D_AddDisposeQuery`, and exactly 2 public methods `Begin()`/`End()`, themselves pure one-line forwards to `FNA3D_QueryBegin`/`FNA3D_QueryEnd`. **Critical finding: FNA's C# layer has ZERO explicit validation of call sequence** — no guard anywhere against `End()` before `Begin()`, double `Begin()`, or double `End()`; whatever happens for those is entirely up to the native FNA3D library / GPU driver, never surfaced as a .NET exception by FNA itself. **This means Tasks 442-444's own "Match FNA exception" framing is factually incorrect as written** — there is no FNA-level exception to match for any of the 3 invalid sequences; those 3 tasks need reframing (when picked up) to "confirm CNA's own non-validating behavior is a deliberate, correct match to FNA's own lack of validation" rather than expecting a specific throw. CNA's `OcclusionQuery.hpp`/`.cpp` matches this shape closely and correctly: constructor creates an `IOcclusionQueryBackend` via `device.GetBackend().CreateOcclusionQuery()`, `getIsCompleteProperty()`/`getPixelCountProperty()`/`Begin()`/`End()` are all simple forwards with a null-`backend_` guard (returning `false`/`0`/no-op respectively) — CNA also does zero sequence validation, correctly matching FNA's own lack of one rather than inventing stricter behavior FNA never had. `IOcclusionQueryBackend` (`IGraphicsBackend.hpp`) declares exactly the matching 4 pure-virtual methods, no documented invalid-sequence contract either. **Per-backend investigation of what actually happens for the 3 invalid sequences**: **EasyGL** — a thin wrapper over `easygl::Query`'s own raw `glBeginQuery`/`glEndQuery(GL_ANY_SAMPLES_PASSED)` passthrough, zero internal state tracking; all 3 invalid sequences just produce a silent, unchecked `GL_INVALID_OPERATION` in the GL error queue — no crash, no C++ exception, correctly matching FNA's own unvalidated-forwarding shape; disposal is RAII-safe and idempotent (`Query::destroy()` guarded by `is_created()`), double-`Dispose()` safe. **SDL_Renderer** — `CreateOcclusionQuery()` still correctly calls `ThrowNo3D("CreateOcclusionQuery")` (Task 727's fix, confirmed intact and covered by `examples/sdlrenderer_occlusionquery_throws_test.cpp`); since construction itself throws, `Begin()`/`End()` are unreachable here — consistent, no gap. **Vulkan — real, previously-undocumented functional gap, confirmed by reading `VulkanOcclusionQueryBackend` in `VulkanGraphicsBackend.cpp` line-by-line**: `Begin()`/`End()` never inject `vkCmdBeginQuery`/`vkCmdEndQuery` into any command buffer — the implementation's OWN existing code comment already self-documents this ("we cannot inject query begin/end here... the result is always 0... until proper per-draw-call query injection is implemented"), and `End()` unconditionally hardcodes `pixelCount_ = 0` regardless of any real GPU query result. **Occlusion queries on Vulkan currently always report 0 visible pixels / never-visible, regardless of real on-screen visibility** — a query is created and its lifecycle (Begin/End/IsComplete/Dispose) is fully safe and crash-free, but it is functionally inert. **Bgfx — an even more severe, previously-undocumented functional gap, confirmed by reading `BgfxOcclusionQueryBackend` in both `BgfxGraphicsBackend.hpp`/`.cpp`**: `Begin()`/`End()` are literally empty inline no-ops (`{}`), and — confirmed via a targeted grep across the entire Bgfx backend — there is no `bgfx::setCondition()` call anywhere associating the created `bgfx::OcclusionQueryHandle` with any submitted draw call at all. The query handle is created and destroyed safely, but is never wired to any geometry whatsoever; `IsComplete()`/`PixelCount()` call `bgfx::getResult()` against a handle that bgfx itself never receives a real result for (no drawcall was ever conditioned on it), so it can never meaningfully report "visible." **Both gaps map directly onto already-planned, not-yet-picked-up tasks** — Task 447 ("Implement/verify Vulkan occlusion query result synchronization") and Task 448 ("Implement/verify Bgfx occlusion query result behavior") are exactly the right place to fix these; not fixed here, since this is a pure audit task (matches the established Task 431 precedent of auditing-and-deferring to specific already-scoped later tasks rather than fixing everything found in the audit itself). **Test coverage confirmed**: `examples/occlusion_query_test.cpp` (EasyGL) covers construction, `GetTypeName`, one valid Begin/End cycle, and non-crash `IsComplete`/`PixelCount` reads — but zero invalid-sequence tests exist (Tasks 442-444 genuinely unaddressed) and zero pixel-visibility-correctness tests exist (Tasks 445-446 unaddressed; correctly scoped "EasyGL" only in this file already, since Vulkan/Bgfx can't yet produce a real result at all per the gaps just found). `OcclusionQueryDynamicBufferTests.cpp`'s own header comment explicitly documents it deliberately has zero `OcclusionQuery`-specific unit tests, deferring to integration/pixel tests instead. `sdlrenderer_occlusionquery_throws_test.cpp` covers the SDL_Renderer throw path well. **Minor consistency note**: `OcclusionQuery` doesn't override `Dispose(bool)` on the CNA side — confirmed this matches the existing project-wide convention (neither `Texture2D` nor `VertexBuffer` do either), not an `OcclusionQuery`-specific deviation. Docs-only audit task, no code changed; delegated the FNA-vs-4-backend source comparison to a `fork` subagent to keep raw source dumps out of the main context, all synthesis and this write-up done directly. |

| 447 | Implement/verify Vulkan occlusion query result synchronization          | ⛔      | **Genuinely risky architecture decision, not guessed at — see below.** Investigated before writing code (per Task 441's own audit finding that `VulkanOcclusionQueryBackend::Begin()`/`End()` never inject `vkCmdBeginQuery`/`vkCmdEndQuery`, always reporting 0). Read `VulkanGraphicsBackend`'s full command-recording architecture (`RecordCommandBuffer`, `Pending3DDraw`, `activeBatches_`) line-by-line: **this backend defers ALL 3D and 2D draw calls** — `DrawIndexedPrimitives`/`DrawPrimitives`/etc. only ever push a `Pending3DDraw` snapshot (vertex/index bytes, pipeline params, push constants) onto `pending3D_`; the real `vkCmdDraw*`/pipeline-bind calls only happen much later, once per frame, inside `RecordCommandBuffer()` at present time. `OcclusionQuery::Begin()`/`End()`, by contrast, are called synchronously by game code, immediately around a draw call — by the time `RecordCommandBuffer()` actually turns the accumulated `pending3D_` entries into real Vulkan commands, there is currently **no way to know which entries were pushed inside which query's Begin/End span**, since `Pending3DDraw` has no query-association field at all. Implementing this for real requires resolving 3 genuinely non-obvious design questions, none of which have an obviously-correct single answer: **(1)** how to tag `Pending3DDraw` (and, if occlusion queries should also cover `SpriteBatch`/2D draws matching FNA's API-agnostic design, `activeBatches_` too) entries with "recorded inside query X's Begin/End span," so `RecordCommandBuffer` can wrap the corresponding `vkCmdDraw*` calls in `vkCmdBeginQuery`/`vkCmdEndQuery`; **(2)** whether a query may legally span MULTIPLE draw calls (matching real FNA/XNA semantics, but Vulkan requires `vkCmdBeginQuery`/`vkCmdEndQuery` to stay within a single render-pass instance, so a Begin/End spanning multiple RTs/render-passes would need an explicit rejection or splitting policy) or should be restricted to exactly one draw call (simpler to implement, but a real, game-visible deviation from FNA's actual capability); **(3)** how to correctly re-`vkCmdResetQueryPool` a reused `OcclusionQuery` object's query slot before each new frame's Begin/End cycle (currently only reset ONCE, at construction, via a one-time command buffer) — this task's own "avoid stale reads" hint directly names this exact hazard: an idiomatic real-world usage pattern (construct one `OcclusionQuery`, call Begin()/End() every frame to test an object's visibility over time) would either hit a Vulkan validation error (beginning a query slot that was never reset since its last use) or silently read a stale, previous-frame result, unless the reset is correctly re-sequenced into the SAME per-frame command buffer ahead of that frame's `vkCmdBeginQuery`. **Exact question for the project owner**: which of these 3 design choices to make (in particular #1's tagging mechanism and #2's multi-draw-span policy) before implementing — none is a simple, mechanical bug fix like Task 727's `ThrowNo3D` addition; each is a real, load-bearing architectural commitment to this backend's deferred-recording model. Not fixed here. Moving to Task 448 (Bgfx) next, which investigation shows is a substantially simpler, well-scoped fix (bgfx's synchronous, non-deferred submission model plus its own dedicated `bgfx::submit(id, program, occlusionQuery, depth, flags)` overload sidestep this entire class of problem). |
| 448 | Implement/verify Bgfx occlusion query result behavior                   | ✅      | **CLOSED — real production fix shipped, matching bgfx's own documented API contract exactly; a genuinely important discriminating-power limitation found and honestly documented along the way (not swept under the rug); new Task 917 opened for the deeper gap this uncovered.** Investigated before writing code (per Task 441's own audit finding: `BgfxOcclusionQueryBackend::Begin()`/`End()` were literally empty no-ops, no `bgfx::setCondition()`/occlusion-query `submit()` overload used anywhere) — read bgfx's real header docs plus its own official `26-occlusion` example (`examples/26-occlusion/occlusion.cpp`, vendored under `_deps/bgfx_cmake-src`) line-by-line: bgfx has a dedicated `submit(id, program, occlusionQuery, depth, flags)` overload ("Submit primitive with occlusion query for rendering"), used by the example's own measurement pass. **Fixed**: added `activeOcclusionQuery_` (a `bgfx::OcclusionQueryHandle`) to `BgfxGraphicsBackend`, set by the new `BgfxOcclusionQueryBackend::Begin()` (now non-empty) and cleared by `End()` (also non-empty; `BgfxOcclusionQueryBackend` now holds an owner `BgfxGraphicsBackend*`, mirroring `VulkanOcclusionQueryBackend`'s established owner-pointer pattern, plus a destructor safety-clear if the query is destroyed while still marked active). Added a new `SubmitViewProgram(bgfx::ProgramHandle)` helper that routes through the occlusion-query overload whenever a query is active, else the plain overload; mechanically replaced all 12 3D-draw `bgfx::submit(currentViewId_, xProgram_)` call sites (colored3d ×3, textured3d, coloredTextured3d, dualTexture3d, skinned3d, envMap3d, alphaTestColoredTextured3d, alphaTest3d, litTextured3d, instanced3d) with `SubmitViewProgram(xProgram_)` via a scripted regex substitution, confirmed to skip both the unrelated 2D `SpriteBatch` submit site and the new helper's own 2 internal submit calls. Since bgfx submits every 3D draw call synchronously (unlike Vulkan's deferred `RecordCommandBuffer`, Task 447's own BLOCKED reason), no correlation/tagging machinery is needed for the attachment itself — this is a direct, mechanical, low-risk fix, genuinely different in kind from Vulkan's architecture problem. **Critical empirical finding, established via rigorous sabotage-and-revert exactly as this project's discipline requires**: built a real GPU pixel/query test (`examples/bgfx_occlusion_query_test.cpp`, `Bgfx_OcclusionQuery`) drawing a fully-visible quad wrapped in `Begin()`/`End()`, structured as a genuine multi-frame state machine (bgfx's occlusion results are inherently 1+-frame-latent, confirmed via `bgfx::getResult()`'s own doc comment, "Retrieve occlusion query result from previous frame" — a single Draw() call retry-looping internally, as this session's earlier EasyGL Tasks 445/446 used, does NOT work here since it never advances a real `bgfx::frame()`; this test instead relies on `Game::EndDraw()`'s own `Present()`→`bgfx::frame()` call between real per-frame `Draw()` invocations, matching the established `examples/bgfx_render_target_usage_test.cpp` multi-frame convention). **Sabotaging `Begin()`/`End()` back to pure no-ops (the exact pre-fix bug state) and rerunning produced IDENTICAL `IsComplete()`/`PixelCount()` behavior to the fixed version** — on this sandbox's software Mesa GL 2.1 (llvmpipe) renderer, `bgfx::getResult()` returns a non-`NoResult` value even for a query handle that was NEVER submitted anywhere at all, meaning neither `IsComplete()` nor the exact `PixelCount()` value can serve as a discriminating signal in this specific environment — a genuine, empirically-confirmed software-driver/sandbox limitation (this project's own established `Bgfx_RenderTarget2D_MsaaResolve`/Vulkan-DRI3-unavailable precedent for this exact sandbox), not a CNA code defect. Rather than ship a falsely-confident "PASS" or silently drop the test, the test itself was rewritten to assert only what genuinely discriminates in this sandbox — confirmed via its own separate sabotage-and-revert pass on `SubmitViewProgram` (breaking it entirely made the "quad still renders correctly" check fail exactly as predicted, restored and reconfirmed) — while reporting `IsComplete()`/`PixelCount()` informationally only, with the limitation explained directly in the test file's own header comment. **New tracked Task 917** (not blocking, deferred, matching the Task 916 precedent): bgfx's own official example attaches its occlusion-measurement submit specifically to a SEPARATE, dedicated view from the "real" visible scene, precisely so the query's own sample count isn't polluted by other same-view/same-frame geometry — CNA's fix currently shares `currentViewId_` with everything else drawn that frame, a real remaining gap for TRUE scene-depth occlusion correctness, deferred since fixing it needs further architecture (likely reusing the existing `Detail::AllocateRtViewId()`/`ReleaseRtViewId()` free-list, Task 910's own precedent) and can't even be verified in this specific software-rendering sandbox regardless. Registered as new Bgfx `ctest` entry `Bgfx_OcclusionQuery` in `CMakeLists.txt`, immediately after the existing Task 249 `Bgfx_VertexFormatMapping` registration. Full Bgfx regression: `cmake --build cmake-build-bgfx -j4 -- -k` built cleanly (only the known, unrelated `cna_demo_xact` error, skipped via `-k`); full `ctest` run **4413/4414 passed (99%)**, exactly the 1 already-established pre-existing Bgfx failure (`Bgfx_RenderTarget2D_MsaaResolve`, the documented Xvfb-no-DRI3-support environment limitation), zero new failures, and `Bgfx_OcclusionQuery` itself passed under `ctest` specifically. |
| 449 | Verify disposing query while active is safe or throws correctly         | ✅      | **CLOSED — no bug found; confirms already-correct RAII teardown behavior via a repetition-based stress verification (matching Task 719's own established leak-check-style precedent for pure-safety confirmations), not classic sabotage-and-revert (no natural incorrect-vs-correct code branch exists to toggle for this).** Key finding first: `OcclusionQuery` has no override of `Dispose(bool)` of its own — `GraphicsResource::Dispose(bool)`'s base implementation only flips `isDisposed_`/removes the resource-tracking reference, never touches `backend_` at all (confirmed by reading `GraphicsResource.cpp`; matches FNA's own shape closely, since FNA's real `OcclusionQuery.Dispose(bool)` DOES call `FNA3D_AddDisposeQuery` there, a difference already flagged as an established, project-wide, non-`OcclusionQuery`-specific convention by Task 441's own audit — neither `Texture2D` nor `VertexBuffer` override it either). This means the REAL backend teardown (the underlying GL query object) only happens in `~OcclusionQuery()` (default, chains to `backend_`'s own destructor), not the XNA `Dispose()` method — so "disposing a query while active" is really about destroying the C++ object while `Begin()` was called with no matching `End()`. Confirmed EasyGL's own GL query object is owned by an `easygl::Query` member with RAII semantics (`~Query() { destroy(); }`, itself guarded by `is_created()`) — per the GL spec, deleting an active query object is well-defined (deletion is deferred internally by the driver until the query stops being active), so this should already be safe. Extended `examples/occlusion_query_test.cpp` (Tasks 35/442-444's file) with a new check block: 50 iterations of construct→`Begin()`→destroy-with-no-`End()`, confirming no crash/throw across all 50; then, mirroring Task 719's own exact convention, confirmed `GraphicsDevice::GetTrackedResourceCount()` returns to its pre-loop baseline afterward (since `~GraphicsResource()` calls `Dispose(false)`, which DOES call `RemoveResourceReference` even via pure destruction, not just explicit `.Dispose()`); and confirmed a freshly-constructed query afterward still completes a normal Begin/End cycle correctly, proving no corrupted state lingered from the 50 active-disposals. All 3 new checks PASS on the first attempt — no bug found. **Discriminating power**: the core "no crash across 50 active-disposals" check is itself the primary verification (a genuine stress test of the exact scenario this task asks about, not previously exercised by any of Tasks 35/442-444's own checks, which always call a matching `End()` before the query goes out of scope); the `GetTrackedResourceCount()`-returns-to-baseline check reuses a mechanism whose own discriminating power was already independently established by Task 719 (for `Texture2D`/`RenderTarget2D`), so a fresh sabotage-and-revert of that identical shared mechanism here would be redundant rather than new evidence — not repeated. Since this only touches shared EasyGL-backed test code (no production code changed): full EasyGL regression `cmake --build cmake-build-debug -j4 -- -k` built cleanly (only the known, unrelated `cna_demo_xact` error, skipped via `-k`); full `ctest` run **4510/4513 passed (99%)**, exactly the 3 already-established pre-existing EasyGL failures by exact name (`EasyGL_MRT_TwoAttachments`, `EasyGL_GraphicsDevice_ReferenceStencil`, `easy-gl-resource-smoke-tests`), zero new failures, and `EasyGL_OcclusionQuery_Cycle` itself passed under `ctest` specifically. |

Docs-only task, no code changed. |

| 454 | Ensure Vulkan unsupported features throw or document fallback behavior           | ✅      | **CLOSED — no bug found; the underlying `VkPhysicalDeviceFeatures` gating was already correctly implemented, just not previously documented anywhere — documented here per this task's own "throw or document fallback behavior" framing.** Investigated `VulkanGraphicsBackend`'s device-creation code directly, since Vulkan's own device-capability model is genuinely stricter than GL/bgfx's — requesting an unsupported optional feature makes `vkCreateDevice` fail outright, not just silently degrade, so this needed real verification rather than assuming graceful-by-default behavior. Confirmed the ENTIRE optional-feature request surface is exactly 2 features (`fillModeNonSolid` for `FillMode::WireFrame`, `samplerAnisotropy` for anisotropic texture filtering) — no other `VkPhysicalDeviceFeatures` are ever requested at all (no `geometryShader`/`tessellationShader`/`wideLines`/etc.), and both of these 2 are correctly gated behind a real `vkGetPhysicalDeviceFeatures(physicalDevice_, &supported)` query first, each behind its own `if (supported.X)` check before being added to the enabled-feature struct passed to `vkCreateDevice`. Confirmed downstream usage sites correctly respect the resulting support flags rather than assuming the feature is always available: `fillModeWireframe_ = (fillMode == 1) && fillModeNonSolidSupported_` (a device without `fillModeNonSolid` silently falls back to solid fill instead of requesting an invalid pipeline state that would otherwise validation-error or crash); `maxSamplerAnisotropy_` is read from the device's real `VkPhysicalDeviceLimits.maxSamplerAnisotropy` and used to clamp any requested anisotropy level rather than blindly passing through an arbitrary request. Also spot-checked MSAA sample-count selection (`PickSampleCount`) since it's the same class of "device may not support the exact requested value" concern — confirmed it queries the device's real `framebufferColorSampleCounts` limit and picks the best available count ≤ the requested one, not assuming an arbitrary count is always legal. **No gap found across any of these 3 checks** — added a new documented section to Task 451's own `docs/graphics-backend-feature-matrix.md` (not a new separate doc, since this is a small, single-backend addendum to an already-comprehensive matrix) recording this finding, since "already correctly implemented but never written down anywhere" is itself worth fixing even when no code changes. Zero code changes — pure investigation + documentation. |

Investigated via 8 isolated runs of the UNMODIFIED code (`git stash`) before assuming anything — confirmed it fails there too, ~1-in-8 times, with the IDENTICAL failure signature both with and without this task's change (`sample=(255,0,0)` instead of the correct blended `(224,0,32)` — the coarsest mip level evidently hadn't finished auto-generating/blending yet at readback time, a timing race, not a deterministic bug). None of the 4 backends printed anything about actual DEVICE CAPABILITIES (MSAA levels, MRT limits, anisotropic filtering, texture/occlusion-query support) before this task — only "which backend/renderer is active." Added a `std::cout` capability-dump block to each backend's constructor, right after its existing init-log line (Vulkan's placed right after device/queue creation, since it had no prior log site to follow): **EasyGL** — `GL_MAX_SAMPLES` via the file's own existing `metagl::glGetIntegerv(MaxSamples, ...)` MSAA-clamp pattern, MRT (hardcoded 4, FNA's own `MAX_RENDERTARGET_BINDINGS` cap), and anisotropic filtering explicitly reported **NOT supported** — see new Task 918 below. **Vulkan** — real device name (`VkPhysicalDeviceProperties::deviceName`), true max MSAA via the file's own existing `PickSampleCount(physicalDevice_, 64)` helper (queries `framebufferColorSampleCounts`/`framebufferDepthSampleCounts`), and the real `anisotropySupported_`/`fillModeNonSolidSupported_` device-feature-gated flags already established by Task 454's own audit (max anisotropy level included when supported). **Bgfx** — `bgfx::getCaps()` queried directly for `limits.maxFBAttachments` (device's true MRT limit, still clamped to FNA's 4), `BGFX_CAPS_OCCLUSION_QUERY` (Task 448's own subject), and `BGFX_CAPS_TEXTURE_BLIT`\|`READ_BACK` (Task 455's own subject) support bits. **SDL_Renderer** — a concise summary of this backend's already-fully-documented 2D-only scope (no MSAA/MRT/anisotropy; `VertexBuffer`/`IndexBuffer`/`OcclusionQuery` throw at construction; `Texture3D`/`TextureCube` construction succeeds silently with no real backend, BLOCKED Task 725) rather than a raw capability query, since this backend genuinely has none of these capabilities to query. All 4 additions print `SurfaceFormat: Color only (Task 176)`, matching that already-established cross-backend constraint. **3 real bugs caught and fixed while writing/testing these dumps (none shipped)**: (1) Bgfx — `std::min<uint8_t>(caps->limits.maxFBAttachments, 4)` streamed via `operator<<` printed an invisible control character instead of the digit "4", because `uint8_t`/`unsigned char` is treated as a character type by `ostream::operator<<`, not a number; fixed by casting to `int` before both the `min()` call and the stream write. (2) Vulkan — `std::to_string(maxSamplerAnisotropy_)` (a `float`) printed "max 16.000000x"; fixed via `static_cast<int>` first. (3) SDL_Renderer — an early draft of the log text incorrectly claimed `Texture3D`/`TextureCube` "throw at construction"; this contradicts Task 725's own established finding (construction succeeds SILENTLY with a null backend — that silent-success, not a throw, is exactly why Task 725 is BLOCKED rather than already fixed); corrected before building/testing to accurately describe the real behavior. **New Task 918 opened while writing the EasyGL dump**: `TextureFilter::Anisotropic` silently falls back to plain trilinear filtering (`LinearMipmapLinear`/`Linear`) on this backend — confirmed via direct code reading of the sampler-filter mapping switch plus a repo-wide `grep` for "Anisotropy"/"ANISOTROPY" finding zero matches anywhere in `EasyGLGraphicsBackend.cpp` or the vendored `easy-gl` library; `SamplerState.MaxAnisotropy` is never read or forwarded to any GL sampler parameter at all. Vulkan (real `samplerAnisotropy` device feature) and Bgfx (`BGFX_SAMPLER_MIN/MAG_ANISOTROPIC` flags) both have genuine support; EasyGL is the one backend silently downgrading instead of erroring, logging, or documenting the gap before now — deferred as a real, scoped, low-risk feature addition (not a judgment call, not blocked), out of this logging task's own scope. **Full regression, all 4 backends, run sequentially never concurrently, each rebuilt fresh (`-j4 -- -k`, only the known unrelated `cna_demo_xact` Content-copy error each time)**: EasyGL `ctest` 4510/4513 passed (99%), exactly the 3 known baseline failures (`EasyGL_MRT_TwoAttachments`, `EasyGL_GraphicsDevice_ReferenceStencil`, `easy-gl-resource-smoke-tests`), zero new failures. Vulkan `ctest` 4438/4447 passed (99%), exactly the 9 known baseline failures (3 `ContentManagerSkinnedModelTest` segfaults + 5 `BlendState`/Task 868 + 1 `DepthBias` sub-case), zero new failures; new capability log confirmed firing with real hardware info ("device=AMD Radeon 780M (RADV PHOENIX)... MSAA up to 8x... anisotropic filtering: supported, max 16x"). Bgfx `ctest` 4412/4414 passed (99%), exactly the 2 known baseline failures (`Bgfx_RenderTarget2D_MsaaResolve` plus the `Bgfx_RenderTarget2D_MipChain` flaky test discovered and documented in Task 455), zero new failures; capability log confirmed correct with the `uint8_t` bug fix verified working ("MRT up to 4 targets"). SDL_Renderer `ctest` 4410/4421 passed (99%), **11 failed — this matches the TRUE established baseline exactly** (`ContentManagerSkinnedModelTest`×3, `EffectApplyTest`×2, `SkinnedModelEXTPartTest`×6), zero new failures, zero unexplained anomalies. **Baseline reconciliation note**: an earlier self-scheduled wakeup for this task cited an expected baseline of "13" (from `NEXT.md`'s §2 "Test status" section, itself dated to Task 915's own first-ever systematic SDL_Renderer run); that "13" figure was already stale even before this task started — Task 709 (chronologically later than Task 915 despite its lower task number; this project's task numbers reflect discovery order, not execution order) found and fixed a real `SetRenderTargets` bug and, in the same commit, corrected the true baseline down to 11 by special-casing the one test (`SetRenderTargets_FourTargets_DoesNotThrow`) whose expected behavior changed, with every subsequent SDL_Renderer regression from Task 709 onward (710-721 and beyond) consistently confirming 11; `NEXT.md`'s own §2 section was simply never updated to reflect that correction. This task's own 11-failure result is a clean confirmation of the already-correct, already-established baseline, not a new finding — `NEXT.md`'s §2 stale "13" text is corrected as part of this task's own doc updates. |
| 457 | Add automated smoke test selecting every backend available on the machine        | ✅      | **CLOSED — added a `GraphicsSmoke` CTest label to the 4 existing per-backend smoke tests, plus a new local orchestrator script running all 4 sequentially with a combined pass/fail summary; deferred the actual CI-wiring decision as new Task 919.** Investigated existing coverage first (per standing "check before designing something new" discipline): all 4 backends already had their own working 3-frame smoke test (`EasyGL_House3D_SmokeTest`/Task 85, `Vulkan_Demo2D_SmokeTest`/Task 88, `Bgfx_Demo2D_SmokeTest`/Task 89, `SDL_Renderer_Demo2D_SmokeTest`/Task 730), each just running the shared demo binary headlessly (`--smoke 3`) via `CMakeLists.txt`'s `add_test`, but with **no CTest `LABELS` tying them together** (unlike the input suite's own established `LABELS input` convention, `CNA_INPUT_TEST_FILTER`) and **no mechanism to run all 4 backend configurations in one pass** — `CNA_GRAPHICS_BACKEND` is confirmed a single-value, compile-time cache option (one backend per build directory), with no runtime "is backend X available" detection anywhere in the graphics code. Also found a real, exact precedent already merged and running: `.github/workflows/input-ci.yml`, a genuine 4-backend GitHub Actions matrix (EasyGL/SDL_RENDERER/Vulkan/bgfx + an ASan/UBSan EasyGL variant) that configures, builds, and runs `ctest -L input` via `xvfb-run` on `ubuntu-24.04` for every push/PR to `feature/input`/`develop`/`master` — proving this repo's CI is already real and 4-backend-matrix-capable, not merely local/sandboxed. **Scope decision**: creating or modifying a `.github/workflows/*.yml` file is a fundamentally different kind of change from every other artifact this task touches — once merged, it starts running automatically and repeatedly on shared CI infrastructure (consuming Actions minutes on every future push/PR), unlike a one-time source/test/doc change. That falls squarely under this project's own "modifying CI/CD pipelines" category that warrants the project owner's explicit sign-off rather than autonomous action, even under this session's otherwise-broad "commit and push per finished task" authorization (which was granted for ordinary source-code changes, not for authoring new recurring CI automation). Rather than either silently creating the workflow or silently dropping the "CI-friendly" half of this task's own framing, split the work: **done here** — (1) added `LABELS GraphicsSmoke` to all 4 existing `set_tests_properties` blocks (`CMakeLists.txt`, 4 one-line additions, zero behavior change to the tests themselves); (2) added `scripts/run-all-backend-smoke-tests.sh`, a new local orchestrator that iterates the 4 backends against this project's own persistent per-backend build directories (`cmake-build-debug`=EASYGL, `cmake-build-vulkan`, `cmake-build-bgfx`, `cmake-build-sdl`), configuring fresh only if a build dir doesn't already exist, building with the project's own established `-j4 -- -k` convention (tolerating the one already-known, unrelated `cna_demo_xact` Content-copy build error rather than treating it as a false "backend unavailable" signal — an early draft of this script wrongly gated on the aggregate build exit code and reported all 4 backends as unavailable purely because of that one irrelevant target; fixed to let `ctest` itself be the real pass/fail signal instead), then running `ctest -L GraphicsSmoke --output-on-failure` and printing a combined per-backend PASS/FAIL/SKIPPED summary with a non-zero exit if any backend that DID build had a failing smoke test (a backend whose build directory doesn't exist and can't be freshly configured, e.g. missing system Vulkan/GL dependencies, is reported SKIPPED — "not available on this machine" — rather than failing the run, matching this task's own literal "every backend available on the machine" framing). **Deferred, not done here**: actually wiring this into `.github/workflows/` (e.g. a new `graphics-smoke-ci.yml` copying `input-ci.yml`'s exact matrix/dependency-install boilerplate but filtering `ctest -L GraphicsSmoke` instead of `-L input`) — tracked as new Task 919 below, requiring the project owner's explicit go-ahead before creating, given its recurring, shared-resource-consuming nature. **Discriminating power verified via sabotage-and-revert**: temporarily renamed `cmake-build-sdl/cna_demo_2d` out of the way — `ctest -L GraphicsSmoke` correctly reported `SDL_Renderer_Demo2D_SmokeTest` as `Not Run`/`FAILED` (exit code 8), confirming the script's `if ctest ...; then PASS; else FAIL` branch genuinely discriminates; restored the binary and reconfirmed a clean 100% pass. **Full script run, all 4 backends** (build dirs already existed and were already up to date from Task 456's own regression): `EASYGL PASS`, `VULKAN PASS`, `BGFX PASS`, `SDL_RENDERER PASS`, each `ctest -L GraphicsSmoke` showing "100% tests passed, 0 tests failed out of 1" — confirming the label correctly isolates exactly the one relevant smoke test per backend, with zero cross-backend interference (run strictly sequentially, never concurrently, matching this project's own hard testing rule). |

Searched the whole `tests/` tree for `GTEST_SKIP()`: 177 call sites, all unrelated (environment-availability skips — no display, no audio device, no microphone — none backend/GPU-feature-based). Confirmed via `IGraphicsBackend`/`GraphicsDevice` header greps that **no runtime capability-query API exists** (Task 456's new capability dump is `std::cout`-only, not queryable by test code) — and confirmed no test currently needs one, so building one now would be speculative infrastructure with zero consumers, not a real fix. **Real bug found and fixed while verifying Layer 2's 2nd case**: `TextureCubeTests.cpp`'s `#if defined(CNA_BACKEND_EASYGL) \|\| defined(CNA_BACKEND_VULKAN)` exclusion of Bgfx was **stale** — its own comment ("Bgfx: TextureCube::GetData has no GPU readback path at all") predates Task 914's real fix to `BgfxTextureCubeBackend::GetData`, already confirmed working via Task 455/456's own `Bgfx_TextureCube_Mip_RoundTrip`/`Bgfx_TextureCube_PartialRect_RoundTrip` pixel tests, but this specific shared unit test's DDS-decode path had never been re-verified against that fix and was silently skipping real assertion coverage on Bgfx ever since. **Verified empirically, both directions**: temporarily added Bgfx to the `#if` and rebuilt+ran just this test on the Bgfx `CnaTests` binary — genuinely PASSED with correct real per-face colour data read back from the GPU (not a false pass — confirmed real numeric colour values, not skipped); separately temporarily added SDL_Renderer too — genuinely FAILED (buffer stayed zero-initialized), confirming SDL_Renderer's implicit exclusion remains correct and matches Task 725's own documented "construction succeeds silently with a null backend" finding. Fixed by permanently including Bgfx in the assertion (3-way `#if`: EasyGL/Vulkan/Bgfx) and rewriting the comment to explain both the Bgfx fix-history and the still-correct SDL_Renderer exclusion. **Scope decision — no new "skip mechanism" abstraction added**: with only 2 real call sites in the entire test suite, both already clear and well-commented, and both branching *assertions* rather than literally skipping whole tests, extracting a generic macro/helper would be premature abstraction with no discovered need (matches this project's own "three similar lines is better than a premature abstraction" discipline) — the existing idiomatic `#if defined(CNA_BACKEND_X)` pattern *is* the mechanism, now doubly verified correct rather than assumed correct. **Full regression, all 4 backends** (this shared test file is compiled into every backend's `CnaTests` binary): EasyGL 4510/4513 passed (3 known baseline), Vulkan 4438/4447 passed (9 known baseline), Bgfx 4412/4414 passed (2 known baseline, including Task 455's flaky `MipChain`), SDL_Renderer 4410/4421 passed (11 known baseline) — zero new failures anywhere, and the fixed `DDSFromStreamEXTDecodesAllSixFacesWithDistinctColours` test now genuinely exercises Bgfx's real GetData path on every future regression rather than silently skipping it. |

desktop: 1 synchronous immediate context recreate) — real, thought-through code, never exercised against an actual browser. Documented anticipated (not verified) WebGL2/GLES3 capability gaps versus desktop GL (no geometry/tessellation shaders; anisotropic filtering needs `EXT_texture_filter_anisotropic`, currently moot per Task 918's own finding that EasyGL has no real anisotropic support on any platform yet; narrower baseline texture-format support, currently moot per Task 176's `SurfaceFormat::Color`-only constraint; zero WebGL-1 fallback by design). Cross-referenced (not duplicated) `docs/viewport-displaymode-adapter-support.md`'s existing canvas-as-display-model coverage. Docs-only, zero code changed. |

## Phase 52 — Pixel conformance and golden-image testing

| #   | Task                                                                                         | Status | Notes                      |
| --- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------ | -------------------------- |
| 461 | Create common pixel-test helper for window/device creation, draw, present, readback, compare | ✅      | **CLOSED — new, additive, opt-in helper; deliberately did NOT retrofit any of the ~330 existing example test files.** Investigated the actual duplication shape first (read `easygl_textured_quad_test.cpp`, `bgfx_render_target_usage_test.cpp`, `vulkan_instanced_test.cpp`, `sdlrenderer_sample_bouncing_sprite_test.cpp`): window/device creation, the run loop, and present are **already fully centralized** by XNA's own `Game`/`Game::Run()` — there is no ad-hoc window/device boilerplate to extract, since `Game` already is that shared piece. The only genuinely-repeated shape across single-frame pixel tests is a small (~20-25 line) pattern: a `Game` subclass with a one-shot `Draw()` guard, a `GetBackBufferData` readback, an R/G/B compare, a `[PASS]`/`[FAIL]` printf, and a 3-line `main()` — everything else (the actual draw calls, expected colours, regions) is genuinely test-specific, not copy-paste. **Scope decision, matching CLAUDE.md's own "three similar lines is better than a premature abstraction" discipline**: retrofitting ~330 existing, working, already-verified files into a new shared base class would be a large, high-risk, low-value mechanical refactor for purely stylistic deduplication — not attempted. Instead added new, purely-additive infrastructure: `examples/common/PixelTestGame.hpp`, a `Game`-derived base class with a single-shot `RunTest()` override point and an `ExpectPixel(label, region, expectedColor)` helper (readback + R/G/B compare + PASS/FAIL printf + result tracking), plus a `RunPixelTest<TGame>()` one-line `main()` helper. Explicitly scoped to the common single-frame case only — multi-frame state-machine tests (e.g. the Bgfx render-target-usage test) keep hand-rolling their own `Game` subclass, unchanged, since their per-test frame/stage logic doesn't fit this single-shot shape. **Verified genuinely functional, not just written and assumed**: added a new canary test, `examples/easygl_pixeltestgame_smoke_test.cpp` (clears to solid green, checks the centre pixel via the new helper), registered as `EasyGL_PixelTestGame_Smoke`. Built and ran standalone: real window/device created, `[PASS] centre: pixel=(0,255,0,255)`, exit code 0. **Discriminating power verified via sabotage-and-revert**: temporarily changed the expected colour to red — genuinely printed `[FAIL] centre: pixel=(0,255,0,255), expected (255,0,0,*)` and returned exit code 1; reverted and reconfirmed a clean pass. Directly sets up Tasks 462 (tolerance-based comparison) and 463 (golden-image format) to extend this same opt-in helper rather than inventing their own separate infrastructure. Full EasyGL regression (only backend touched — the new files are backend-agnostic XNA-API-only code, but only registered in EasyGL's CMake block so far): `ctest` 4511/4514 passed, exactly the 3 known baseline failures, zero new failures (test count grew by 1 for the new, passing, canary test). |
| 462 | Add tolerance-based pixel comparison for backend differences                                 | ✅      | **CLOSED — extended Task 461's new `PixelTestGame::ExpectPixel()` with a tolerance parameter; confirmed a real, extensive, already-existing need before building anything.** Investigated first: 98 of the ~330 existing `examples/*.cpp` files already independently hand-roll near-identical per-channel `abs(a - b) <= tolerance` comparison logic (named `closeTo`/`colourMatch`/inline, varying only in the actual tolerance value used — 111 call sites use 8, 21 use 10, 34 use 20, 13 use 30, 35 use 40, 14 use 60, driven by how much GPU/driver blending or rounding noise a given scenario is documented to produce, e.g. `avatar_tint_routing_integration_test.cpp`: "Tolerance of 20 absorbs ordinary shading/rounding noise"). This is a much stronger, already-proven duplication case than Task 461's window/device pattern — a real, extensively-repeated need, not hypothetical. No single fixed tolerance fits every case, so it stays a per-call parameter rather than a fixed constant. Extended `ExpectPixel()` with a `tolerance` parameter defaulting to `0` (preserving Task 461's original exact-match behavior unchanged) — per-channel `abs(got.X - want.X) <= tolerance` on R/G/B (alpha still ignored), matching all 98 existing ad-hoc implementations' own convention exactly. The `[FAIL]` message now includes `+/- N` when a non-zero tolerance was used, so a failing tolerance-based check is distinguishable from a failing exact check in test output. **Scope decision matching Task 461's own precedent**: did not retrofit any of the 98 existing ad-hoc implementations to use the new centralized parameter — this is forward-looking infrastructure for future tests, even though the underlying need is empirically proven real by the 98 existing copies; migrating working, already-verified files for pure style remains out of scope. **Verified genuinely functional**: extended the Task 461 canary test (`easygl_pixeltestgame_smoke_test.cpp`) with a 2nd check, `centre-tolerance`, intentionally comparing the real solid-green clear pixel against an expected colour off by 10 on R and B, passing only because of an explicit `tolerance=20` — proving the tolerance path is genuinely exercised, not just present but unused. **Discriminating power verified via sabotage-and-revert**: temporarily tightened the tolerance to 5 (below the real 10-unit diff) — genuinely printed `[FAIL] centre-tolerance: pixel=(0,255,0,255), expected (10,255,10,*) +/- 5` and returned exit code 1; reverted to 20 and reconfirmed both checks pass cleanly. Full EasyGL regression: `ctest` 4511/4514 passed, exactly the 3 known baseline failures, zero new failures. |
| 463 | Add golden image format for small reference outputs                                          | ✅      | **CLOSED — opens Phase 52; a real, solid MVP (not a canary-only stub), since Tasks 464-469 will genuinely build real test cases on top of it.** Investigated first: confirmed zero golden-image mechanism exists anywhere in this repo (only `docs/coverage.md`'s own forward-looking mention and this phase's own header text) and zero reference-image fixture files exist outside `third_party/`/`Content/`. Crucially, **both halves of the mechanism already existed, independently verified, just never wired together as a test pattern**: `Texture2D::SaveAsPng`/`FromStream` (Task 683's own round-trip test, `sdlrenderer_texture2d_saveas_roundtrip_test.cpp`) already correctly save/load CPU-side pixel-cache data as real PNG files — no new image-encode/decode code was written for this task, it's pure reuse. The actual gap was narrower than it first sounds: nothing loaded a *checked-in reference file* and diffed it against a *live render's readback*. Extended `examples/common/PixelTestGame.hpp` (Tasks 461/462's own file) with `CompareGoldenImage(label, region, goldenPath, tolerance=0)`: reads back the region via the existing `GetBackBufferData`, loads the checked-in PNG via the existing `Texture2D::FromStream`, and does a per-pixel per-channel tolerance compare reusing Task 462's own `abs(a-b) <= tolerance` convention exactly (not reinvented) — since exact pixel-perfect cross-backend/driver reproduction is already known unreliable (Task 462's 98-file survey), tolerance defaults to 0 but every real future golden-image test should pass a real value. Added a standard update workflow: setting `CNA_UPDATE_GOLDEN` (any non-empty value) in the environment makes the same call *write* the current live render to `goldenPath` via the existing `Texture2D::CreateFromPixels`+`SaveAsPng` instead of comparing, reporting `[UPDATE]` — the intended path for an intentional rendering change (regenerate, review with `git diff`/an image viewer, commit). New `examples/golden/` directory holds checked-in reference PNGs, kept intentionally small (8×8 in the canary, not full 800×480 frames) to keep repo binary bloat minimal, matching this task's own "small reference outputs" framing. **A genuine, non-obvious infra bug caught and fixed while verifying this for real**: CMake's `add_test()` sets the test's working directory to `CMAKE_CURRENT_BINARY_DIR` (the build directory) by default, not the source tree — a relative `examples/golden/*.png` path would silently resolve to the wrong location (inside `cmake-build-*`) under `ctest`, even though it worked fine when run standalone from the repo root by hand. Fixed by adding an explicit `WORKING_DIRECTORY "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}"` test property — the first test in this project to need one (confirmed via a repo-wide grep: zero prior `WORKING_DIRECTORY` usage anywhere in `CMakeLists.txt`). **Verified genuinely functional end-to-end, not just written and assumed**: new canary test `examples/easygl_goldenimage_smoke_test.cpp` (clears solid blue, compares an 8×8 region) — ran once with `CNA_UPDATE_GOLDEN=1` to generate the real reference PNG, confirmed it re-loads and compares correctly on a normal run (both standalone and via `ctest -R EasyGL_GoldenImage_Smoke`, confirming the `WORKING_DIRECTORY` fix). **Discriminating power verified via sabotage-and-revert**: temporarily changed the live clear colour to red — genuinely printed `[FAIL] solid-blue-8x8: pixel 0 of 8x8 mismatches golden image ...: got=(255,0,0,255) want=(0,0,255,255) +/- 0` and returned exit code 1; reverted to blue and reconfirmed a clean pass both standalone and via `ctest`. **Scope decision matching Tasks 461/462's own precedent**: this is new, additive, opt-in infrastructure — no existing example file was modified to use it; Tasks 464-469's own real feature-coverage golden-image test cases are explicitly separate, later work, not attempted here. Full EasyGL regression: `ctest` 4512/4515 passed, exactly the 3 known baseline failures, zero new failures. |
| 464 | Add BasicEffect golden image cases                                                           | ✅      | **CLOSED — first real (non-canary) golden-image consumer, reusing Phase 42's already-verified BasicEffect capstone scene rather than inventing a new one.** Investigated first: read Phase 42 (Tasks 361-370, full detail in `plan_graphics_20260708.md`) — Task 370, the capstone, renders a 64×64 quad with a real 2×2 multi-texel texture, `VertexColorEnabled=true`, `TextureEnabled=true`, `DiffuseColor`+`EmissiveColor`, `LightingEnabled=false`, sampling all 4 texels via 4 separate constant-UV draws, confirmed byte-identical (modulo 1-unit rounding) across all 3 real backends (EasyGL/Vulkan/Bgfx). This is an ideal, already-verified-correct, cross-backend-consistent scene to reuse — no new scene needed. New `examples/easygl_basiceffect_golden_test.cpp` recreates Task 370's own "top-left texel" case exactly (identical texture data, `VertexColor`, `DiffuseColor`, `EmissiveColor`, constant UV=(0.25,0.25), same `RasterizerState::CullNone`/Task-896 workaround) using the new `PixelTestGame`/`CompareGoldenImage` helper (Tasks 461-463) instead of Task 370's own hand-picked single centre-pixel check — compares an 8×8 region around the centre against a new checked-in reference (`examples/golden/easygl_basiceffect_golden_test.png`), genuinely exercising more of the rendered output than a single point sample. Reused Task 370's own `tolerance=8` exactly (also the single most common value in Task 462's 98-file survey). **Added a deliberate belt-and-suspenders cross-check**: an `ExpectPixel` call against Task 370's own hardcoded expected value `(99,52,23)`, independent of whatever the golden PNG itself contains — this specifically guards against the failure mode where a future `CNA_UPDATE_GOLDEN=1` regeneration accidentally captures a broken render as the new "reference," which would otherwise make the golden-image compare silently "pass" against its own now-wrong file. **Verified genuinely functional and correct, not just written and assumed**: generated the real reference PNG via `CNA_UPDATE_GOLDEN=1`, then independently confirmed via the cross-check that the captured render is EXACTLY `(99,52,23,255)` — Task 370's own documented value, not an accidental blank/black frame (a real, documented risk: Task 370's own test has a "skip blank/black frames" retry loop this new test does not replicate; empirically confirmed unnecessary for this specific single-shot scene in this sandbox). **Discriminating power verified via sabotage-and-revert, on both checks simultaneously**: temporarily changed the sampled UV to `(0.75, 0.75)` (the bottom-right texel) — both the cross-check and the golden-image compare correctly failed, and the sabotaged render's actual value, `(74,78,70)`, exactly matched Task 370's own separately-documented "bottom-right texel" expected value — an independent cross-validation of correctness beyond just "did it fail." Reverted to the top-left UV and reconfirmed both checks pass cleanly, both standalone and via `ctest -R EasyGL_BasicEffect_Golden`. **Scope decision matching Tasks 461-463's own precedent**: new file only — none of Task 370's own 3 existing per-backend combined-test files were modified; single-backend (EasyGL), matching the established canary convention; Tasks 465-469's own real feature-coverage golden-image cases remain separate, later work. Full EasyGL regression: `ctest` 4513/4516 passed, exactly the 3 known baseline failures, zero new failures. |
