## Phase 70 — SDL_Renderer: 2D backend verified perfection

> Runs first, per "Execution order" above. SDL_Renderer is 2D-only by design (see file header),
> so this phase is deliberately smaller than Phases 72–73 — there's no 3D pipeline, blend/depth/
> raster state matrix, or stock-effect shader breadth to cover. "Perfect" here means: every 2D
> XNA feature SDL_Renderer is supposed to support is pixel-verified specifically *through*
> SDL_Renderer (not assumed from EasyGL parity), and every unsupported 3D path throws the
> correct, specific exception rather than a generic one.

### SpriteBatch on SDL_Renderer

| #   | Task | Status | Notes |
| --- | ---- | ------ | ----- |
| 666 | Audit all `SpriteBatch::Draw`/`DrawString` overloads specifically through SDL_Renderer (not inherited from EasyGL test assumptions) | ✅ | **`Draw`'s 9 overloads audited and pixel-verified — all correct, no backend bug found (`DrawString`'s 6 overloads split out, tracked separately under the existing Tasks 690–694 SpriteFont rows).** New `examples/sdlrenderer_spritebatch_overloads_test.cpp` exercises each of the 9 `Draw` overloads exactly once, each drawing a distinct 60×60 tinted square (white 1×1 texture × a unique tint colour, `BlendState::Opaque` so `white*tint == tint` exactly) into its own non-overlapping screen slot, then reads back a real, discriminating pixel per overload. Overloads 4/5 (`Draw(tex,position,color)`/`Draw(tex,position,optSrcRect,color)`) draw at the texture's *native* size (no scale parameter in either signature) — the check reads the exact origin pixel, not the slot centre, unlike overloads 6/7's scaled variants. Overload 3 (the full 8-arg NOXNA overload) is exercised with a real, discriminating `SpriteEffects::FlipHorizontally` against an asymmetric red/green split texture (not the plain white one), so a flip that silently did nothing would read the wrong half's colour — same discriminating-power standard as a bug-fix test, just applied to a from-scratch audit that happened to find the existing implementation already correct. **Caught a real bug in the test itself during development** (not the backend): an early draft mis-placed the read-back point for overloads 4/5 at each slot's centre instead of the native-size sprite's actual origin pixel, reading the background clear colour and failing — confirms the test genuinely discriminates rather than passing vacuously. Requires `PresentationMode::NativeBackBuffer` (Task 915 finding — SDL_Renderer's default `FixedHeightDynamicWidth` mode doesn't map logical pixels 1:1 to physical ones, which `GetBackBufferData`'s underlying `ReadBackbuffer` correctly refuses to trust). Full regression: `CnaTests` (SDL_Renderer, filtered) exact baseline match (4262/4274, 2 skipped, 10 pre-existing/expected 3D-related failures unchanged); `ctest -R SDL_Renderer_SpriteBatch_Overloads` passes. |
| 667 | Pixel test: `SpriteSortMode::Deferred` submission order on SDL_Renderer | ✅ | **CLOSED — no bug found; `Deferred` (the default sort mode) already correctly preserves submission order on SDL_Renderer, ignoring `layerDepth` entirely.** Confirmed by reading `SpriteBatch::flushBatch()` directly: `Deferred` takes the "no sort" fall-through branch (only `BackToFront`/`FrontToBack`/`Texture` actually `std::stable_sort`), so this task's job was to confirm SDL_Renderer's draw-dispatch doesn't reorder anything after that. New `sdlrenderer_spritebatch_deferred_order_test.cpp`: 2 overlapping-sprite pairs, each assigned `layerDepth` values that would flip the overlap outcome if `Deferred` secretly applied `FrontToBack` or `BackToFront` sorting instead (Pair 1: high-depth-then-low-depth submission catches an accidental `FrontToBack`; Pair 2: low-depth-then-high-depth submission catches an accidental `BackToFront`) — both pairs confirm the submitted-last sprite always wins the overlap regardless of depth. Requires `PresentationMode::NativeBackBuffer` (Task 915). All 6 checks pass. Full regression: `ctest` 4275/4290 passed, 2 skipped, 13 failed (same already-documented baseline as Task 669/915 — zero new failures). |
| 668 | Pixel test: `SpriteSortMode::Texture` grouping on SDL_Renderer | ✅ | **CLOSED — no bug found.** FNA/XNA's `SpriteSortMode::Texture` contract sorts by raw texture *pointer* (`flushBatch()`'s `std::stable_sort(..., [](a,b){ return a.texture < b.texture; })`), so which texture group ends up first on screen depends on runtime addresses — already fully covered at the logic level by `SpriteBatchTests.cpp`'s mock-backend `TextureGroupsDrawsByTextureAndPreservesGroupOrder` (Task 414: adjacency + submission-order stability within a group, without depending on pointer order). This task instead pixel-verifies the property a mock recording backend can't: that after `SpriteBatch` reorders the draw sequence by texture, SDL_Renderer's actual draw dispatch still binds the CORRECT texture for each reordered call. New `sdlrenderer_spritebatch_texture_sort_test.cpp`: 4 non-overlapping sprites (so the result doesn't depend on which texture's pointer sorts first), 2 distinct textures, scrambled A/B/A/B submission order — each destination rectangle must show its own assigned colour regardless of internal reordering; a texture/position mismatch would mean a misattributed binding. Requires `PresentationMode::NativeBackBuffer` (Task 915). All 4 checks pass. Full regression: `ctest` 4276/4291 passed, 2 skipped, 13 failed (same already-documented baseline as Task 667/669 — zero new failures). |
| 669 | Pixel test: `SpriteSortMode::FrontToBack` / `BackToFront` ordering on SDL_Renderer | ✅ | **CLOSED — no bug found; both sort modes already work correctly on SDL_Renderer.** `SpriteSortMode` sorting logic lives entirely in the shared, backend-agnostic `SpriteBatch.cpp` (CPU-side sort key in `flushBatch()`, never written into vertex data), already proven correct by Tasks 415/416/420's mock-backend and EasyGL pixel tests — this task's job was to confirm the SDL_Renderer draw-dispatch path doesn't itself reorder or drop anything after that shared sort runs. New `sdlrenderer_spritebatch_layerdepth_test.cpp`: a direct port of Task 420's EasyGL `FrontToBack` scenario (2 overlapping opaque sprites, deliberately submitted in the OPPOSITE order from the correct sorted draw order so the test genuinely discriminates depth-based sorting from raw submission order) plus a NEW `BackToFront` scenario (no existing test in this project, on any backend, previously exercised `BackToFront` with a real pixel verification) — same discriminating methodology, mirrored geometry. Requires `PresentationMode::NativeBackBuffer` per Task 915's finding (SDL_Renderer's default presentation mode doesn't map logical pixels 1:1 to physical ones, breaking exact-pixel `GetBackBufferData` reads). All 6 checks (3 per sort mode) pass. Full regression: `ctest` 4274/4289 passed, 2 skipped, 13 failed (13 pre-existing: the same already-documented "SDL_Renderer does not support 3D" set from Task 915's own baseline — zero new failures). |
| 670 | Pixel test: `SpriteSortMode::Immediate` per-draw flush on SDL_Renderer | ✅ | **CLOSED — no bug found; `Immediate` genuinely flushes per-draw on SDL_Renderer, both at the `SpriteBatch.cpp` dispatch level (already proven by the mock-backend Task 412 tests) and end-to-end on real SDL draw calls.** Confirmed `SdlSpriteBatchBackend::Draw()` calls `SDL_RenderTexture()` synchronously with no backend-internal queue at all (trivially true for this backend), so the only genuinely new thing to verify was whether a plain `GraphicsDevice::Clear()` issued BETWEEN two operations inside one still-open `Immediate` `Begin()`/`End()` session is interleaved into the real draw order exactly where it's called, rather than deferred alongside the sprites until `End()`. New `sdlrenderer_spritebatch_immediate_flush_test.cpp`: draws a Red sprite, then `Clear(Green)` (a plain `GraphicsDevice` call outside `SpriteBatch`'s own queue), then `End()` — under correct per-draw flush the sprite is already real pixels by the time `Clear()` runs, so it gets wiped away (region reads Green); a broken "silently treated like `Deferred`" implementation would still execute `Clear()` immediately (it's not gated by `SpriteBatch` at all) but only draw the sprite later at `End()`, landing on top (region would read Red). Requires `PresentationMode::NativeBackBuffer` (Task 915). **Discriminating power independently verified**: a `Deferred`-only sabotage (`if (false && sortMode_==Immediate)` alone) produced a false negative — it turned into a silent-drop bug (the sprite disappearing entirely, since `End()`'s own `if (sortMode_ != Immediate) flushBatch()` skip meant the now-queued sprite was never flushed at all) rather than "deferred to `End()`", so the test still passed for the wrong reason. Corrected by ALSO making `End()` unconditionally call `flushBatch()` — genuinely reproduces "Immediate silently treated as Deferred", and the test failed exactly as predicted (region read Red); restored both changes and reconfirmed the test passes. Full regression: `ctest` 4277/4292 passed, 2 skipped, 13 failed (same already-documented baseline as Task 667/668/669 — zero new failures). |
| 671 | Pixel test: rotation around origin on SDL_Renderer | ✅ | **Real bug found and fixed — a genuine `SDL_RenderTextureRotated` pivot-offset mismatch, not a test-authoring issue.** Direct port of Task 417's EasyGL rotation test (100×100 texture, Red 20×20 marker top-left, rest Blue; drawn at `destinationRectangle=(200,150,100,100)`, `origin=(100,100)`, rotated `PiOver2`) initially FAILED: the rotated marker landed at the clear colour instead of `(290,60)`. Root cause: XNA's `Draw(destinationRectangle, ..., origin, ...)` contract requires `origin` (source-texture pixel space) to map to exactly `(destinationRectangle.X, destinationRectangle.Y)` on screen, invariant under rotation — matching FNA's real `GenerateVertexInfo` formula (origin subtracted before rotating, `destinationRectangle.X/Y` added after). `SDL_RenderTextureRotated`'s own contract is the opposite: `center` is a pivot *within* `dstrect`'s local space, and `dstrect` is placed unrotated first, so the pivot's actual screen position is `(dstrect.x + center.x, dstrect.y + center.y)` — not `dstrect`'s own `(x,y)`. `SdlSpriteBatchBackend::Draw`'s 8-arg overload passed `destinationRectangle.X/Y` straight through as `dst.x/y`, placing the rotation pivot a full `destinationRectangle.Width/Height` away from where XNA requires it. Fixed by offsetting `dst.x/y` by `-sdlCenter` so the pivot lands exactly on `destinationRectangle.X/Y`. **This single fix corrects rotation for all 9 public `SpriteBatch::Draw` overloads at once** (confirmed by reading `SpriteBatch.cpp`'s `flushSingle()`: every overload converts to a `destRect`/`srcRect`/`origin` representation and funnels through this one backend call — not backend-specific per overload). The rotation *angle* direction itself was already correct (verified algebraically: FNA's `[cos -sin; sin cos]` formula in screen coordinates matches `SDL_RenderTextureRotated`'s documented clockwise convention exactly, no sign flip needed). New `examples/sdlrenderer_spritebatch_rotation_test.cpp` (direct port of the EasyGL original, plus `PresentationMode::NativeBackBuffer` per Task 915). **Discriminating power independently verified**: `git stash`-reverted the backend fix — the marker/interior checks failed exactly as before (reading the clear colour), the "outside sprite" check still passed (unaffected, sanity-check only); restored and reconfirmed all 3 pass. Full regression: `CnaTests` (SDL_Renderer, filtered) exact baseline match, 0 new failures; `SDL_Renderer_SpriteBatch_Overloads` (Task 666, exercises rotation=0 only) unaffected/still passes, confirming no regression to the non-rotated paths. |
| 672 | Pixel test: scalar and `Vector2` scale overloads on SDL_Renderer | ✅ | **CLOSED — no bug found; both scale overloads already work correctly on SDL_Renderer.** `SpriteBatch.cpp` computes the final `destRect.Width/Height = sourceRect.Width/Height * scale` entirely in the shared, backend-agnostic layer before ever reaching the backend's `Draw()` — same shape as the `SpriteSortMode` tasks (667-670), so this task's job was to confirm SDL_Renderer's draw dispatch renders whatever final destination rectangle it's handed without further distortion. New `sdlrenderer_spritebatch_scale_test.cpp`: direct port of Task 418's EasyGL test — a 20x20 solid-Red texture drawn twice (scalar `3.0f` -> 60x60; non-uniform `Vector2(2,4)` -> 40x80), with check points chosen to rule out 2 plausible non-uniform-scale bugs (using `scale.X` for both axes, or `scale.Y` for both axes). Requires `PresentationMode::NativeBackBuffer` (Task 915). All 5 checks pass. Full regression: `ctest` 4278/4293 passed, 2 skipped, 13 failed (same already-documented baseline as Tasks 667-670 — zero new failures). |
| 673 | Pixel test: source rectangle cropping on SDL_Renderer | ✅ | **CLOSED — no bug found; source rectangle cropping already works correctly on SDL_Renderer.** Unlike the `SpriteSortMode`/scale tasks (667-670, 672), this IS genuinely backend-specific: `SdlSpriteBatchBackend::Draw()`'s full 8-arg overload (the one `SpriteBatch::flushSingle()` always calls, regardless of which public `SpriteBatch::Draw()` overload the caller used) passes `sourceRectangle` straight through as `SDL_RenderTextureRotated()`'s own `srcrect` parameter. New `sdlrenderer_spritebatch_sourcerect_test.cpp`: direct port of Task 419's EasyGL test — a 20x20 texture split into a 2x2 grid of 4 distinct solid colours, drawn with a `sourceRectangle` selecting only one cell, stretched into a larger destination; check points near both corners of the destination rule out "whole texture stretched, cropping ignored" (would leak the texture's own corner colours instead of the single selected cell's colour). Requires `PresentationMode::NativeBackBuffer` (Task 915). All 3 checks pass. **Discriminating power independently verified**: a targeted sabotage of `SdlSpriteBatchBackend::Draw`'s `SDL_RenderTextureRotated` call (passing `nullptr` instead of `&src`) reproduced the exact predicted failure (both corner checks read the texture's own uncropped corner colours, Red and Yellow); restored and reconfirmed both pass. (First attempt sabotaged the WRONG overload — the plain, non-rotated `SDL_RenderTexture` 4-arg `Draw()` — since `flushSingle()` always calls the full rotation-aware 8-arg overload regardless of which public overload was used; caught because the sabotage had zero effect on the test result.) Full regression: `ctest` 4279/4294 passed, 2 skipped, 13 failed (same already-documented baseline as Tasks 667-672 — zero new failures). |
| 674 | Pixel test: `SpriteEffects::FlipHorizontally`/`FlipVertically` on SDL_Renderer | ✅ | **CLOSED — no bug found; both flip effects already work correctly on SDL_Renderer.** Genuinely backend-specific (like Task 673's source-rectangle cropping): `SdlSpriteBatchBackend::Draw()` maps `SpriteEffects` to an `SDL_FlipMode` and passes it to `SDL_RenderTextureRotated()`'s own `flip` parameter. New `sdlrenderer_sprite_effects_test.cpp`: direct port of Task 167's EasyGL test — a 400x100 viewport split into 4 sections, each testing `FlipHorizontally`/`FlipVertically` against a 2x1/1x2 two-colour texture with `SamplerState::PointClamp` for exact nearest-neighbour sampling (no bilinear ambiguity). Requires `PresentationMode::NativeBackBuffer` (Task 915). All 8 checks pass. **Discriminating power independently verified**: sabotaging the flip-mode mapping (forcing `flip = SDL_FLIP_NONE` unconditionally) reproduced the exact predicted failure on all 4 flip-dependent checks (the 4 no-flip checks stayed correct, as expected); restored and reconfirmed all 8 pass. Full regression: `ctest` 4280/4295 passed, 2 skipped, 13 failed (same already-documented baseline as Tasks 667-673 — zero new failures). |
| 675 | Pixel test: `transformMatrix` in `SpriteBatch::Begin` on SDL_Renderer | ✅ | **CLOSED — real, previously-undiscovered bug found and fixed: `transformMatrix` was completely silently ignored on SDL_Renderer.** `ISpriteBatchBackend::SetTransformMatrix` defaults to a no-op (`virtual void SetTransformMatrix(const Matrix&) {}`), and `SdlSpriteBatchBackend` never overrode it — confirmed EasyGL is the ONLY backend that does (Vulkan/Bgfx have the identical gap, tracked separately: Bgfx's own equivalent, Task 808, is still open; no dedicated Vulkan row currently exists). Fixed by adding a `transformMatrix` member (defaults to `Matrix::getIdentityProperty()`) + a real `SetTransformMatrix` override, and routing non-Identity transforms through a new code path using `SDL_RenderTextureAffine()` — SDL3's general 3-point (origin/right/down) affine texture-blit API — instead of `SDL_RenderTextureRotated()` (which has no way to accept an arbitrary transform on top of its own rotation/flip). The existing `SDL_RenderTextureRotated()` path is used **unchanged** whenever `transformMatrix` stays Identity (the overwhelmingly common case), so this fix carries zero regression risk for the already-verified rotation/flip/scale/source-rect behavior (Tasks 671/672/673/674) — confirmed by rerunning all of them after landing this fix. The new path computes the sprite's 4 unrotated local quad corners (relative to the origin pivot, using the same `sdlCenterX/Y` scaling math as the existing rotation fix), rotates them by the sprite's own `rotation` exactly like FNA's `GenerateVertexInfo` formula, translates to screen space, then transforms each corner via `Vector2::Transform(corner, transformMatrix)` (world/camera transform applied on top of the sprite's own placement, matching FNA's real vertex-pipeline order). `SpriteEffects` flip is implemented by permuting which of the 4 transformed corners feeds `SDL_RenderTextureAffine`'s `origin`/`right`/`down` parameters (each parameter is pinned to a specific SOURCE corner; flipping changes which SCREEN corner that source corner lands on) rather than via an `SDL_FlipMode`, which this API doesn't accept. New `sdlrenderer_transform_matrix_test.cpp`: direct port of Task 168's EasyGL translation test, PLUS a second check combining `FlipHorizontally` with the same transform (a 2x1 two-colour texture) to exercise the new corner-permutation logic specifically, not just plain translation — positioned in its own screen region to avoid overlapping the first sprite's single-pixel check point (an early draft had them overlapping, causing a spurious failure; caught and fixed by relocating the second sprite). **Discriminating power independently verified via `git stash`**: reverting both backend files reproduced the exact predicted failure on all 4 checks; restored and reconfirmed all 4 pass. Full regression: `ctest` 4281/4296 passed, 2 skipped, 13 failed (same already-documented baseline as Tasks 667-674 — zero new failures, confirming no regression in already-shipped rotation/flip/scale/source-rect behavior). |
| 676 | Decide and document `SpriteBatch::Begin(effect)` custom-`Effect` behavior on SDL_Renderer | ✅ | **CLOSED — decided: throw.** Confirmed `SdlSpriteBatchBackend` never overrode `ISpriteBatchBackend::SetCustomEffect` (the shared no-op default ran, silently ignoring any custom `Effect` passed to `SpriteBatch::Begin` — genuinely a "must not silently misrender" case, since a game relying on a custom shader's visual output would render with the plain built-in sprite blit instead, with no error at all). Since SDL_Renderer has no programmable shader stage whatsoever, there is no way to actually honor a custom `Effect`, so silently ignoring it was never a defensible option. Implemented via a real `SdlSpriteBatchBackend::SetCustomEffect` override that throws `std::runtime_error` whenever a non-null `Effect` is supplied — mirroring this backend's own already-established `ThrowNo3D` convention (genuinely-unsupported-here features throw a clear message rather than silently no-op) rather than introducing a different exception type (`System::NotSupportedException`, used at the XNA-facing layer elsewhere in this project, e.g. `TextureCube`/`SoundEffect`) purely for internal consistency within this one backend file. A null `Effect` (the default "no custom effect" case, used by every other SpriteBatch call in this project including all 8 prior SDL_Renderer tests this session) remains a no-op, since `SpriteBatch::Begin()` calls `SetCustomEffect()` unconditionally on every `Begin()` regardless of whether a custom effect was actually requested. New `sdlrenderer_custom_effect_throws_test.cpp`: constructs a `ShaderEffect` (the minimal concrete `Effect` subclass — its constructor safely no-ops when `CreateEffectBackend()` returns `nullptr`, which SDL_Renderer's default does, so no shader compilation is attempted) and confirms passing it to `Begin()` throws, while a plain `nullptr` effect does not. **Discriminating power independently verified via `git stash`**: reverting the override reproduced the exact predicted failure (no throw); restored and reconfirmed both checks pass. Full regression: `ctest` 4282/4297 passed, 2 skipped, 13 failed (same already-documented baseline as Tasks 667-675 — zero new failures). |
| 677 | Guard tests: `Begin`/`End` sequencing errors throw correctly on SDL_Renderer | ✅ | **CLOSED — no bug found; all 3 sequencing guards (double-`Begin`, `End`-before-`Begin`, `Draw`-before-`Begin`) already work correctly on SDL_Renderer.** All 3 guards live entirely in the shared, backend-agnostic `SpriteBatch.cpp` layer (`begun` flag, checked before any `backend_` call in every case — `Begin()` throws if `begun` is already true, `End()`/`Draw()`/`DrawString()` all throw if `begun` is false), already thoroughly covered by Tasks 161-166's mock-backend unit tests (`SpriteBatchTests.cpp`'s `BeginTwiceWithoutEndThrows`/`EndWithoutBeginThrows`/`DrawXYBeforeBeginThrows` et al., using a no-backend `SpriteBatch` instance). This task's job was to confirm the guards still hold end-to-end with a REAL SDL_Renderer-backed `GraphicsDevice`/`SpriteBatch`/`Texture2D` — i.e. that no real backend call happens (or throws for some unrelated reason) before the sequencing check fires. New `sdlrenderer_spritebatch_begin_end_guard_test.cpp`: 5 checks — `End()` before `Begin()` throws; `Draw()` before `Begin()` throws; a second `Begin()` without an intervening `End()` throws; `Begin()`/`Draw()`/`End()` succeeds (sanity: the real backend engages fine); `Begin()`/`End()`/`Begin()`/`End()` (re-entrant sequencing) succeeds. All 5 pass; no production code changed, so no `git stash`/sabotage verification was needed (matches the established pattern for shared-logic-layer tasks like 667-670/672). Full regression: `ctest` 4283/4298 passed, 2 skipped, 13 failed (same already-documented baseline as Tasks 667-676 — zero new failures). |

### Texture2D on SDL_Renderer

| #   | Task | Status | Notes |
| --- | ---- | ------ | ----- |
| 678 | Audit `Texture2D::SetData`/`GetData` full-array round-trip on SDL_Renderer | ✅ | **CLOSED — no bug found; both the CPU-side round trip and the real GPU texture SDL_Renderer creates from it are correct.** `Texture2D::GetData` is implemented entirely via a CPU-side cache (`cpuPixels_`, maintained in the shared, backend-agnostic `Texture2D.cpp`) — it never queries the GPU texture at all, so a plain `SetData()`-then-`GetData()` round trip is guaranteed correct identically on every backend by construction, not something SDL_Renderer-specific to verify. The genuinely backend-specific question (per this task's own "`SDL_UpdateTexture`/`SDL_LockTexture` path" hint) is whether the REAL GPU texture SDL_Renderer creates from `SetData`'s full-array overload — which calls `GraphicsDevice::GetBackend().CreateTexture()`, constructing a brand-new `SdlTextureBackend` via `SDL_CreateTexture()`+`SDL_UpdateTexture()` — actually contains the correct pixels when drawn, not just whatever the CPU cache reports. New `sdlrenderer_texture2d_setdata_getdata_test.cpp`: a 2x2 texture with 4 distinct solid colours written via the full-array `SetData(Color*, int)` overload; checks (1) `GetData` returns the exact same 4 colours, and (2) drawing the texture (scaled up, `PointClamp` sampling) via `SpriteBatch` and reading back the REAL rendered pixels also shows the correct colour per quadrant. All 8 checks pass. **Discriminating power independently verified**: sabotaging `SdlTextureBackend`'s constructor to upload an all-zero buffer to `SDL_UpdateTexture` (instead of the real pixel data) left the `GetData` checks passing (CPU cache unaffected, confirming it's a genuinely separate code path) while all 4 rendered-pixel checks correctly failed (read back black); restored and reconfirmed all 8 pass. Full regression: `ctest` 4284/4299 passed, 2 skipped, 13 failed (same already-documented baseline as Tasks 667-677 — zero new failures). |
| 679 | Pixel test: `SetData` partial-rectangle region on SDL_Renderer | ✅ | **CLOSED — no bug found; a partial-rectangle `SetData` correctly reaches the real GPU texture on SDL_Renderer.** The existing backend-agnostic `easygl_texture2d_partial_rect_test.cpp` (Task 169) does a pure CPU-side `SetData`-then-`GetData` round trip with no framebuffer readback at all — per Task 678's finding, this is guaranteed correct on every backend by construction, since `GetData` never touches the GPU texture. The genuinely backend-specific question: `Texture2D::SetData`'s partial overload (`SetData(level, rect, data, startIndex, elementCount)`) merges the sub-rect write into the full per-level CPU buffer, then calls `SdlTextureBackend::UpdatePixels()` — which always re-uploads the WHOLE merged buffer via `SDL_UpdateTexture(texture, nullptr, ...)` rather than just the sub-rect region. New `sdlrenderer_texture2d_partial_rect_test.cpp`: a 4x4 texture filled Red, then a 2x2 Blue sub-rect written at (1,1), drawn scaled up via `SpriteBatch` (`PointClamp` sampling) and read back for real — corners must stay Red, the sub-rect must show Blue. All 8 checks pass. **Discriminating power independently verified**: sabotaging `SdlTextureBackend::UpdatePixels` into a no-op reproduced the predicted failure on all 8 checks (the texture's GPU memory is left uninitialized, reading back black); restored and reconfirmed all 8 pass. Full regression: `ctest` 4285/4300 passed, 2 skipped, 13 failed (same already-documented baseline as Tasks 667-678 — zero new failures). |
| 680 | Pixel test: `SetData` `startIndex`/`elementCount` slice on SDL_Renderer | ✅ | **CLOSED — no bug found; a non-zero `startIndex`/`elementCount` slice correctly reaches the real GPU texture on SDL_Renderer.** The existing backend-agnostic `easygl_texture2d_partial_rect_test.cpp` (Task 170) does a pure CPU-side `SetData`-then-`GetData` round trip with no framebuffer readback — per Task 678's finding, this is guaranteed correct on every backend by construction, since `GetData` never touches the GPU texture. The `src = startIndex + row * w + col` slicing arithmetic itself lives entirely in the shared, backend-agnostic `Texture2D::SetData(level, rect, data, startIndex, elementCount)` (`Texture2D.cpp`), so it too is backend-independent; the genuinely backend-specific question, mirroring Task 679: once that slice has been merged into the per-level CPU buffer and handed to `SdlTextureBackend::UpdatePixels()` (which always re-uploads the WHOLE merged buffer via `SDL_UpdateTexture(texture, nullptr, ...)`), does the REAL GPU texture actually contain the correct pixels when drawn. New `sdlrenderer_texture2d_startindex_test.cpp`: direct port of Task 170A's scenario — a 4x1 texture filled Red, then a 6-element source array `[Green, Green, Blue, Blue, Green, Green]` sliced with `startIndex=2`/`elementCount=2` into rect `(1,0,2,1)` (only `data[2]`/`data[3]`, Blue, should be read; the Green elements at both ends of the source array must be skipped), drawn scaled up via `SpriteBatch` (`PointClamp` sampling) and read back from the real framebuffer. All 4 checks pass. **Discriminating power independently verified**: sabotaging the shared slicing arithmetic (`const int src = row * w + col;`, dropping `startIndex`) reproduced the exact predicted failure — the two middle texels read back Green (`data[0]`/`data[1]`) instead of Blue (`data[2]`/`data[3]`), while the two untouched corner texels stayed correctly Red; restored (confirmed clean `git diff`) and reconfirmed all 4 pass. Full regression: `ctest` 4285/4301 passed, 2 skipped, 13 failed (same already-documented baseline as Tasks 667-679 — zero new failures). |
| 681 | Decide and document `Texture2D` mip-level `SetData`/`GetData` on SDL_Renderer | ✅ | **CLOSED — real gap found (silent no-op) and fixed: decided to throw for `level>0` `SetData` on SDL_Renderer.** Confirmed `ITextureBackend::UpdatePixelsLevel` defaults to a silent no-op (`virtual void UpdatePixelsLevel(int, const uint8_t*, int, int) {}`) and `SdlTextureBackend` never overrode it, while `Texture2D::SetData`'s level>0 path (shared, backend-agnostic `Texture2D.cpp`) unconditionally calls it — i.e. a `SetData(level=1, ...)` call previously silently did nothing to the real GPU texture on SDL_Renderer. By contrast, `EasyGLTextureBackend::UpdatePixelsLevel` genuinely implements this via `glTexImage2D` for a real, sampled mip chain (real OpenGL). SDL_Renderer's `SDL_Texture`/2D-blit API has no native mip chain and no per-level LOD sampling at all in its rendering pipeline — even if pixel data were somehow stored per-level, SDL_Renderer could never actually sample from anything but the base level when drawing, so a "single-level-only software fallback" would be equally misleading, just silently so. **Decision: throw**, mirroring this backend's established "throw for what can't be honored" convention (Task 676's `SetCustomEffect`). Implemented via a new `SdlTextureBackend::UpdatePixelsLevel` override throwing `std::runtime_error` unconditionally (this method is only ever reached for `level>0`, per `Texture2D::SetData`'s own branching — level 0 always goes through `UpdatePixels` instead). `level=0` `SetData`/`GetData` (the overwhelmingly common case) and `level>0` `GetData` (a pure CPU-side cache read of `getMipBufferConst(level)`, per Task 678's finding — never touches the GPU) are both completely unaffected. **Known, accepted, documented deviation**: `Texture2D::SetData`'s level>0 path merges the sub-rect into the per-level CPU shadow buffer *before* calling `backend_->UpdatePixelsLevel()`, so the CPU buffer is already updated by the time the exception is thrown — restructuring this ordering would touch the shared layer all 4 backends depend on (EasyGL's real per-level upload also needs the fully-merged buffer, mirroring Task 679's "always re-upload the whole merged buffer" finding), which is out of scope for this single-backend decision task. This is inert in practice: SDL_Renderer never reads mip-level state (CPU or GPU) when rendering, so no rendered pixel is ever affected either way — documented here rather than fixed. New `sdlrenderer_texture2d_miplevel_throws_test.cpp`: a 4x4 `mipMap=true` texture (`levelCount==3`); confirms level=0 `SetData`/`GetData` stay unaffected, level=1 `SetData` throws `std::runtime_error`, and level=1 `GetData` does not throw. All 5 checks pass. **Discriminating power independently verified via `git stash`**: reverting the header+source override reproduced the exact predicted failure (only the "level=1 SetData throws" check failed; the other 4 stayed correct, precisely isolating the fix); restored and reconfirmed all 5 pass. **Note for Vulkan/Bgfx**: both backends also currently rely on the same silent-no-op `UpdatePixelsLevel` default (only EasyGL overrides it) — out of scope here since this task is SDL_Renderer-specific, but already tracked separately (Task 855 for Vulkan, Task 817 for Bgfx — "currently only EasyGL has this depth"). Full regression: `ctest` 4285/4302 passed, 2 skipped, 13 failed (same already-documented baseline as Tasks 667-680 — zero new failures). |
| 682 | Verify `Texture2D::FromStream` (PNG/JPG/BMP/DDS) round-trip renders correctly when drawn via SDL_Renderer | ✅ | **CLOSED — no bug found; `FromStream`'s real GPU texture renders correctly on SDL_Renderer, guaranteed by construction via the exact same code path Task 678 already proved correct.** The existing backend-agnostic `Texture2DTests.cpp` (Task 262) already thoroughly verifies `FromStream`'s DECODE correctness (PNG/JPEG/BMP byte layouts, resize/crop) via `GetData` — a pure CPU-side cache read (Task 678's finding), guaranteed correct on every backend by construction. Traced the full call chain: `Texture2D::FromStream` → `DecodeStreamToImageData` (DDS via `DxtUtil`, everything else via the shared, backend-agnostic `ImageLoader::LoadFromMemory`, built directly on SDL3_image — independent of `CNA_GRAPHICS_BACKEND`, used identically by all 4 backends) → `MakeTextureFromPixels`, which calls `device.GetBackend().CreateTexture(img)` — the **exact same call site** already used by `Texture2D::SetData`'s full-array overload, which Task 678 already proved renders correctly via a real `SpriteBatch` draw + framebuffer readback. New `sdlrenderer_texture2d_fromstream_test.cpp`: a 2x2 source texture with 4 distinct solid colours, encoded to PNG via the existing `SaveAsPng` (lossless), decoded back via `FromStream`, drawn scaled up (`PointClamp`) via `SpriteBatch`, and read back from the real framebuffer. All 5 checks pass (dimensions + 4 quadrant colours). **Discriminating power independently verified**: sabotaging `SdlTextureBackend`'s constructor to upload an all-zero buffer (identical sabotage to Task 678's) reproduced the exact predicted failure — dimensions stayed correct (decode unaffected, confirming it's a genuinely separate code path) while all 4 rendered-pixel checks correctly failed (read back black); restored (confirmed clean `git diff`) and reconfirmed all 5 pass. Full regression: `ctest` 4285/4303 passed, 2 skipped, 13 failed (same already-documented baseline as Tasks 667-681 — zero new failures). |
| 683 | Verify `SaveAsPng`/`SaveAsJpeg` round-trip when the source texture was created/updated through SDL_Renderer | ✅ | **CLOSED — no bug found; `SaveAsPng`/`SaveAsJpeg` correctly round-trip a texture created/updated through the real SDL_Renderer backend, guaranteed by construction.** `SaveAsPng`/`SaveAsJpeg` (shared, backend-agnostic `Texture2D.cpp`) operate entirely on the CPU-side pixel shadow cache (`cpuPixels_`) via SDL3_image (`SDL_CreateSurfaceFrom` + `IMG_SavePNG_IO`/`IMG_SaveJPG_IO`) — neither ever touches `backend_` or the real GPU texture. Confirmed `Texture2D::MaybeFreeCpuPixels()` (which could otherwise free `cpuPixels_` before a save) is gated purely by `GraphicsDevice::contextRecoveryEnabled_`, which defaults to `true` (a `GraphicsDevice`-level flag, not backend-specific) — so under default conditions `cpuPixels_` is always retained and available. Since `cpuPixels_` is populated identically by `SetData` regardless of backend (Tasks 678-680 already proved this true for SDL_Renderer's real backend texture), the save path is correct by construction once `SetData` is. New `sdlrenderer_texture2d_saveas_roundtrip_test.cpp`: a 2x2, 4-distinct-colour texture `SetData`'d through the real SDL_Renderer backend, saved via both `SaveAsPng`/`SaveAsJpeg` to `MemoryStream`s, decoded back via `FromStream` (reusing Task 682's already-proven decode+render path), drawn side-by-side via `SpriteBatch`, and read back from the real framebuffer (PNG: tight tolerance, lossless; JPEG: wider tolerance, lossy — matching `Texture2DTests.cpp`'s own established convention). All 12 checks pass. **Discriminating power independently verified**: sabotaging `SaveAsPng`'s `SDL_CreateSurfaceFrom` call to encode an all-zero buffer instead of `cpuPixels_->data()` reproduced the exact predicted failure — only the 4 PNG-path rendered-pixel checks failed (read back black), while all 4 JPEG-path checks and both dimension/non-empty-output checks stayed correct, precisely isolating the sabotage to the code path actually under test; restored (confirmed clean `git diff`) and reconfirmed all 12 pass. Full regression: `ctest` 4285/4304 passed, 2 skipped, 13 failed (same already-documented baseline as Tasks 667-682 — zero new failures). |

Still genuinely backend-specific to verify with a real render, since SDL_Renderer's internal implementation could in principle mishandle an NPOT upload on a constrained GL profile. | 685 | Pixel test: `TextureAddressMode::Clamp` via `SpriteBatch` on SDL_Renderer | ✅ | **CLOSED — no bug found, but with an important nuance: `TextureAddressMode::Clamp` already produces the correct result on SDL_Renderer, but not because any SamplerState is genuinely honored.** `SpriteBatch.cpp` calls `backend_->SetSamplerAddressMode(addressU, addressV)` once per `Begin()`; `ISpriteBatchBackend::SetSamplerAddressMode` defaults to a silent no-op, and `SdlSpriteBatchBackend` never overrode it. Investigated fixing this via SDL3's real `SDL_SetRenderTextureAddressMode(renderer, uMode, vMode)` API (`SDL_TEXTURE_ADDRESS_AUTO`/`CLAMP`/`WRAP`) — **but its own doc comment scopes it to "used in `SDL_RenderGeometry()`" specifically**; `SdlSpriteBatchBackend::Draw` uses `SDL_RenderTexture`/`SDL_RenderTextureRotated`/`SDL_RenderTextureAffine` instead (texel-space `sourceRectangle`, not raw UV geometry), which are **completely unaffected by it** — confirmed empirically: implementing the override and wiring it up produced byte-for-byte identical results to not calling it at all (verified via `git stash` on the override itself, not just the usual sabotage). Reverted the dead-code override entirely rather than ship unused infrastructure. For this Draw() implementation, `TextureAddressMode` is not actually selectable at all — `SDL_RenderTexture`'s `srcrect` handling has one fixed behavior regardless of requested `SamplerState`, and that fixed behavior already happens to match Clamp semantics (out-of-bounds source-rect sampling clamps to the texture's edge texel). New `sdlrenderer_texture_address_mode_clamp_test.cpp`: direct port of Task 269's EasyGL test (2x1 Red\|Blue texture, `sourceRectangle` twice the texture's width so the sampled region spans past the texture edge, `PointClamp` sample point reads the clamped edge texel — Blue). The test's pass/fail gate is scoped to the `PointClamp` check only (the actual subject); the sibling `PointWrap` check is printed for context but not asserted, since it currently does **not** match XNA (also reads the fixed clamp-like Blue result instead of the correct Wrap-tiled Red) — a real, separate, already-tracked gap for **Task 686** to resolve, which now has empirical confirmation that no native SDL_Renderer fix is available for this Draw() path (native wrap would need a `SDL_RenderGeometry`-based Draw() rewrite, a materially bigger change out of scope here). **Discriminating power independently verified**: sabotaging `SdlTextureBackend`'s upload to an all-zero buffer reproduced the predicted failure (`PointClamp` reads black instead of Blue); restored (confirmed clean `git diff` — zero net production code changes for this task) and reconfirmed pass. Full regression: `ctest` 4285/4306 passed, 2 skipped, 13 failed (same already-documented baseline as Tasks 667-684 — zero new failures). |
| 686 | Decide and document `TextureAddressMode::Wrap` via `SpriteBatch` on SDL_Renderer | ⛔ | **Real architecture decision needed — see question below.** Task 685's investigation established the actual constraint precisely: `SdlSpriteBatchBackend::Draw` uses `SDL_RenderTexture`/`SDL_RenderTextureRotated`/`SDL_RenderTextureAffine`, whose `srcrect` handling has ONE fixed (Clamp-like) edge behavior regardless of requested `SamplerState` — confirmed SDL3's real `SDL_SetRenderTextureAddressMode` API exists but only affects `SDL_RenderGeometry()` calls (per its own doc comment, and verified empirically: wiring it up for this Draw() path changed nothing). A genuine native Wrap fix needs the Draw() path rewritten to build explicit `SDL_Vertex`/UV geometry and call `SDL_RenderGeometry` instead of the texel-rect calls — a materially larger, riskier change touching the exact code already extensively verified correct across Tasks 671-675/685 (rotation, flip, scale, source-rect cropping, transformMatrix, origin placement). **Question for the project owner:** (a) throw `std::runtime_error`/`NotSupportedException` unconditionally whenever `SamplerState.AddressU`/`AddressV == Wrap` is requested via `SpriteBatch::Begin` (safe, small, but risks breaking any existing/future game that passes a Wrap-mode `SamplerState` defensively even when its draws never actually sample past the texture edge, since Wrap vs Clamp only differs then); or (b) invest in the `SDL_RenderGeometry`-based Draw() rewrite for a real, correct fix (bigger, riskier, but behaviorally correct, and would also unlock Mirror in the same pass); or (c) some hybrid (e.g. only throw when the actual `sourceRectangle` passed to a specific `Draw()` call exceeds the texture's own bounds, i.e. detect the only case where Wrap vs Clamp can ever visibly differ, and pass through unchanged otherwise). Not guessing on this per project's own risky-decision policy — leaving BLOCKED until the project owner picks a direction. |

New `sdlrenderer_texture_filter_point_vs_linear_test.cpp`: SpriteBatch-based counterpart to Task 297's 3D `DualTextureEffect` test, scoped to magnification (the natural `SpriteBatch` use case, matching this task's own "via `SDL_ScaleMode`" framing) — a 2x1 (Red\|Green) texture stretched wide via `SpriteBatch`, drawn once with `SamplerState::PointClamp` and once with `LinearClamp`, sampled at the exact texel boundary each time (guaranteed not to coincide with either texel's own center): `PointClamp` reads a pure, unblended colour; `LinearClamp` reads a genuine ~50/50 blend. Both checks pass. **Discriminating power independently verified**: sabotaging `SetSamplerFilter`'s mapping (inverted: `Linear→NEAREST`, else→`LINEAR`) reproduced the exact predicted failure — both checks flipped (Point sample became blended, Linear sample became pure); restored (confirmed clean `git diff`) and reconfirmed both pass. Full regression: `ctest` 4285/4307 passed, 2 skipped, 13 failed (same already-documented baseline as Tasks 667-685 — zero new failures). |
| 689 | Verify `Texture2D::Dispose` releases the underlying `SDL_Texture` exactly once | ✅ | **CLOSED — no bug found; `Dispose()` is safely idempotent and correctly shared-ownership-aware on SDL_Renderer.** `Texture2D::Dispose(bool)` calls `backend_.reset()` unconditionally (`backend_` is a `std::shared_ptr<ITextureBackend>`), THEN `Texture::Dispose(bool)` (which itself guards on the shared `isDisposed_` flag). Since `shared_ptr::reset()` is well-defined and idempotent on an already-null pointer, calling `Dispose()` twice on the same instance cannot double-free the real `SdlTextureBackend`/`SDL_Texture*`, regardless of the `isDisposed_` guard's placement relative to `backend_.reset()` — confirmed this holds via both a normal build AND a throwaway `-DCNA_SANITIZE=address,undefined` instrumented build (this project's existing `CNA_SANITIZE` CMake option, diagnostics-only) for a stronger guarantee than "didn't crash." Also investigated the genuinely CNA-specific (not XNA-standard) wrinkle: `Texture2D` is a copyable VALUE type here (`= default` copy ctor, backed by the shared `backend_`), unlike XNA's reference-type `Texture2D` (a single C# object — "copies" are just aliases, and Disposing it disposes that one shared instance everywhere) — confirmed CNA's translation is still safe: copying creates a second, independent `GraphicsResource` (its own `isDisposed_`), but both copies share the same `backend_`, so Disposing one copy only decrements the backend's refcount; the real `SdlTextureBackend`/`SDL_Texture*` is only actually destroyed when the LAST copy releases it — the surviving copy remains fully usable. New `sdlrenderer_texture2d_dispose_test.cpp`: `tex1`/`tex2` (a copy of `tex1`), draws `tex1` and confirms correct pixels, Disposes `tex1` (confirms `tex1.IsDisposed==true`, `tex2.IsDisposed==false`), draws `tex2` again and confirms STILL correct (the shared native texture wasn't prematurely freed), Disposes `tex1` a second time (no crash), Disposes `tex2` (now genuinely releasing the last reference), Disposes `tex2` a second time (no crash). All 7 checks pass under both the normal build and the ASan/UBSan build. **Discriminating power independently verified two ways**: (1) sabotaging `SdlTextureBackend::~SdlTextureBackend()` to call `SDL_DestroyTexture` twice in a row did NOT trigger an ASan error (SDL3's own `SDL_DestroyTexture` tolerates this internally — not a useful discriminator); (2) sabotaging `Texture2D::Dispose(bool)` to force-destroy the native handle unconditionally via `SDL_DestroyTexture(backend_->GetNativeTexture())` BEFORE `backend_.reset()` (simulating a naive, refcount-ignorant Dispose implementation) reproduced the exact predicted failure — drawing the surviving `tex2` copy crashed with `SDL_SetTextureColorMod failed: Parameter 'texture' is invalid` under both the normal and ASan builds, confirming the test genuinely detects a premature-free bug; restored (confirmed clean `git diff` on both files) and reconfirmed all 7 pass again on both builds. Full regression: `ctest` 4285/4308 passed, 2 skipped, 13 failed (same already-documented baseline as Tasks 667-688 — zero new failures). |

### SpriteFont on SDL_Renderer

| 690 | Pixel test: single glyph at known position on SDL_Renderer | ✅ | **CLOSED — no bug found; `SpriteBatch::DrawString` correctly places a single glyph at the expected position and size on SDL_Renderer.** This row's own "Mirrors Task 424" note could not be literally satisfied — Task 424 (the EasyGL original) is itself still `⬜`/not started (Phase 48 is entirely unimplemented) — so this is a NEW, first-of-its-kind SpriteFont pixel test, designed directly from FNA's real `SpriteBatch.DrawString` contract as traced in the shared, backend-agnostic `SpriteBatch.cpp`: per-glyph destination rect = `position` + accumulated kerning/cropping offset, sized by the glyph's own bounds, sampling the glyph's source rect from the font's atlas texture via the same `pushSprite()`/`backend_->Draw()` path already extensively verified correct on SDL_Renderer across Tasks 671-685. Since CNA has no XNB content pipeline, `SpriteFont` exposes its raw glyph/cropping/kerning tables directly — hand-built a minimal single-character font fixture (matching this project's established "hand-build a minimal fixture when no real asset is available" convention, e.g. Task 663's DDS cube-map fixture): an 8x8 solid-white atlas texture for glyph `'A'`, zero cropping offset, zero left/right kerning bearing, so drawing `"A"` at position `(4,4)` should place it at exactly screen rect `(4,4,8,8)` on a black background. New `sdlrenderer_spritefont_single_glyph_test.cpp`: 1 check inside the glyph rect (White) + 4 checks immediately outside each corner of the glyph rect (Black background) — proving exact position AND exact size, not offset or oversized in any direction. All 5 checks pass. **Discriminating power independently verified**: sabotaging the shared `SpriteBatch::DrawString`'s destination-rect sizing (doubling `cGlyph.Width`/`cGlyph.Height`) reproduced a real predicted failure — the bottom-right corner check fell inside the now-oversized glyph and incorrectly read White; restored (confirmed clean `git diff`) and reconfirmed all 5 pass. Full regression: `ctest` 4285/4309 passed, 2 skipped, 13 failed (same already-documented baseline as Tasks 667-689 — zero new failures). |
| 691 | Pixel test: multiple glyphs with spacing on SDL_Renderer | ✅ | **CLOSED — no bug found; `SpriteBatch::DrawString`'s horizontal-advance math (spacing + kerning) is correct on SDL_Renderer.** Same situation as Task 690 — Task 425 (EasyGL) is itself not yet implemented, so this is a NEW test, building on Task 690's fixture pattern. Traced the shared `SpriteBatch.cpp` advance math: for the 2nd+ glyph in a line, `curOffset.X += spacing + cKern.X` (extra inter-character spacing plus that glyph's own left-bearing), and after each glyph, `curOffset.X += cKern.Y + cKern.Z` (that glyph's width plus right-bearing). Hand-built a two-glyph font: `'A'` (White, 8x8, kerning `(0,8,0)`) and `'B'` (Green, 8x8, kerning `(0,8,0)`), `spacing=4`. Drawing `"AB"` at `(2,2)` computes: `'A'` (first-in-line) → dest `(2,2,8,8)`; after `'A'`, `curOffset.X=8`; `'B'` (not first) → `curOffset.X += 4+0=12` → dest `(14,2,8,8)` — an exact 4px gap at x∈[10,14) between the two glyphs. New `sdlrenderer_spritefont_multiglyph_spacing_test.cpp`: checks inside each glyph (correct, distinct colour per glyph — rules out an index mix-up), the exact gap (background), and both outer edges. All 5 checks pass. **Discriminating power independently verified**: sabotaging the shared advance math to drop `spriteFont.spacing_` entirely reproduced the exact predicted failure — `'B'` shifted left into the gap (now reads Green) while its expected position read background instead; restored (confirmed clean `git diff`) and reconfirmed all 5 pass. Full regression: `ctest` 4285/4310 passed, 2 skipped, 13 failed (same already-documented baseline as Tasks 667-690 — zero new failures). |
| 692 | Pixel test: newline advances by line spacing on SDL_Renderer | ✅ | **CLOSED — no bug found; `\n` correctly advances by `SpriteFont.lineSpacing_` (not glyph height) on SDL_Renderer.** Same situation as Tasks 690/691 — Task 426 (EasyGL) is itself not yet implemented, so this is a NEW test. Traced the shared `SpriteBatch.cpp` newline branch: `curOffset.X = 0; curOffset.Y += spriteFont.lineSpacing_; firstInLine = true;`. Single-glyph font `'A'` (White, 8x8), **`lineSpacing=10` deliberately different from the glyph's own 8px height** so a "advances by glyph height instead of lineSpacing_" bug shape would be caught, not coincidentally pass. Drawing `"A\nA"` at `(2,2)`: 1st `'A'` → dest `(2,2,8,8)`; after `\n`, `curOffset.Y=10`; 2nd `'A'` (first-in-line again) → dest `(2,12,8,8)` — an exact 2px gap (y∈[10,12)) between the two lines. New `sdlrenderer_spritefont_newline_test.cpp`: checks inside each line's glyph (White), the gap (background), and below the 2nd line (background). All 4 checks pass. **Discriminating power independently verified**: sabotaging the shared newline branch to advance by `lineSpacing_ - 5` reproduced the exact predicted failure — the 2nd line shifted up into the gap check point (now White) while its own expected position read background; restored (confirmed clean `git diff`) and reconfirmed all 4 pass. Full regression: `ctest` 4285/4311 passed, 2 skipped, 13 failed (same already-documented baseline as Tasks 667-691 — zero new failures; one incidental full-suite run showed a 14th, unrelated `AudioCategoryTest` failure that passed cleanly in isolation and did not reproduce on a repeat full run — pre-existing flakiness, not a regression from this change). |
| 693 | Pixel test: default character fallback on SDL_Renderer | ✅ | **CLOSED — no bug found; `SpriteBatch::DrawString`'s unknown-character fallback correctly renders the configured `defaultCharacter`'s glyph (not the first/wrong glyph, and does not throw) on SDL_Renderer.** Same situation as Tasks 690-692 — Task 427 (EasyGL) is itself not yet implemented, so this is a NEW test. Traced the shared `SpriteBatch.cpp` fallback branch: an unresolvable character looks up `spriteFont.defaultCharacter_.value()` in `characterIndexMap_` (throws only if no `defaultCharacter_` was configured at all). Two-glyph font: `'A'` (White, the font's only "real" character) and `'?'` (Red, the designated `defaultCharacter`) — drawing `"Z"` (a character in neither the drawn string's other glyphs nor otherwise special-cased, simply absent from the font's character list) must fall back to rendering `'?'`'s glyph (Red), not throw, and not render `'A'`'s glyph by mistake. New `sdlrenderer_spritefont_default_char_test.cpp`: confirms no throw, the fallback glyph reads Red (not White), and surrounding background stays correct. All 4 checks pass. **Discriminating power independently verified**: sabotaging the fallback lookup to hardcode `'A'`'s index instead of resolving the real `defaultCharacter_` reproduced the exact predicted failure — the fallback glyph incorrectly read White; restored (confirmed clean `git diff`) and reconfirmed all 4 pass. (First sabotage attempt used `characterIndexMap_.begin()`, which coincidentally landed on the same entry as the real default via `unordered_map`'s implementation-defined bucket order — caught because it had zero effect on the result; switched to an explicit, deterministic `find(u'A')` instead.) Full regression: `ctest` 4285/4312 passed, 2 skipped, 13 failed (same already-documented baseline as Tasks 667-692 — zero new failures). |
| 694 | Verify `SpriteEffects` flip + rotation/origin/scale with `DrawString` on SDL_Renderer | ✅ | **CLOSED — real, previously-undiscovered bug found and fixed in the SHARED, backend-agnostic `SpriteBatch.cpp` (affects EVERY backend, not just SDL_Renderer — this is the genuinely cross-cutting exception among Tasks 690-693's SDL_Renderer-only findings).** Same situation as Tasks 690-693 — Tasks 428/429 (EasyGL) are themselves not yet implemented, so this is a NEW test. Traced FNA's real `SpriteBatch.DrawString` (FNA source, not assumption): it uses `axisDirectionX`/`axisDirectionY`/`axisIsMirroredX`/`axisIsMirroredY` lookup tables indexed by `(int)effects` — when `effects != None`, it calls `MeasureString(text)` up front and shifts `origin` by the measured size on the mirrored axis (`baseOffset`), and negates the per-glyph accumulated offset on that axis via `axisDir`, so the ENTIRE glyph sequence mirrors (later characters end up earlier on screen) — not just each glyph's own intra-texture pixel flip. **CNA's prior `DrawString` did none of this**: it forwarded `effects` straight to `pushSprite()` per glyph (which only flips that glyph's own texture sampling, already correct per Task 674's regular-`Draw()` verification) but never touched glyph position/order at all — confirmed empirically via a throwaway diagnostic (`git diff` clean before/after, not committed) showing `DrawString("AB", ..., FlipHorizontally)` rendered `'A'` then `'B'` in the exact same unflipped left-to-right positions as no flip at all. **Fix**: re-derived FNA's formula in terms of CNA's own existing `origin`-subtraction sign convention rather than porting FNA's raw internal variables verbatim — algebraically confirmed FNA's own internal `offsetX` equals `-localX` in CNA's terms for the untouched `effects=None` case (verified this substitution reproduces CNA's *exact*, byte-for-byte original formula when `effects=None`, so the non-flip path is provably unchanged), then applied the same substitution for the `FlipHorizontally`/`FlipVertically` cases. Implemented as `axisDirX[3]`/`axisDirY[3]`/`axisIsMirroredX[3]`/`axisIsMirroredY[3]` static tables (only 3 entries — CNA's `SpriteEffects` enum has no combined-flip value, unlike FNA's `[Flags]` enum, so index 3 is unreachable and not worth including), a `baseOffset` computed once before the loop (calling `SpriteFont::MeasureString` only when `effects != None`, avoiding the extra cost for the overwhelmingly common unflipped case), and the per-glyph `offsetX`/`offsetY` formula replacing the old naive `curOffset+cCrop-origin`. New `sdlrenderer_spritefont_effects_test.cpp`: **Test 1** (the fix) — two-glyph font `'A'`(White)/`'B'`(Green), drawing `"AB"` with `FlipHorizontally` at `(4,4)`; derived by hand that `'B'` must now render first (leftmost, `x=8`) and `'A'` second (rightmost, `x=16`) — the exact mirror of the unflipped layout; **Test 2** (rotation/origin/scale, pre-existing code path, untouched by the fix) — single glyph `'A'`, `origin=(4,4)`, `scale=(2,2)`, `rotation=0`, `position=(10,10)`, hand-derived `dest=(2,2,16,16)`. All 5 checks pass. **Discriminating power independently verified via `git stash`**: reverting the `SpriteBatch.cpp` fix reproduced the exact predicted failure — ONLY the 2 flip-related checks failed (rotation/origin/scale stayed correct, confirming that code path was never broken); restored and reconfirmed all 5 pass. **Cross-backend regression, since this touches shared code**: full SDL_Renderer `ctest` 4285/4313 passed, 2 skipped, 13 failed (same already-documented baseline as Tasks 667-693 — zero new failures); additionally ran the full EasyGL `SpriteBatch`/`SpriteFont`-related test subset (62 tests: `SpriteBatchTest`/`SpriteFontTest`/`SpriteBatchSortModeTest`/`SpriteBatchBackendInjectionTest`/`EasyGL_SpriteFont_*`/`EasyGL_SpriteBatch_*`) — 62/62 passed, zero regressions (all existing `DrawString` unit tests use `effects=None`, mathematically proven unchanged by this fix). Vulkan/Bgfx were not separately re-run this task (out of scope for the SDL_Renderer audit phase's own build-dir rotation; the fix is provably `effects=None`-neutral and the EasyGL spot-check already gives strong cross-backend confidence). |

### BlendState on SDL_Renderer

| #   | Task | Status | Notes |
| --- | ---- | ------ | ----- |
| 695 | Audit `BlendState` → `SDL_BlendMode` mapping for all 4 presets | ✅ | **CLOSED — TWO real, previously-undiscovered bugs found and fixed on SDL_Renderer.** **Bug 1 (`SdlGraphicsBackend::ApplyBlendState`):** the previous mapping only special-cased 2 of the 4 presets by their exact `(colorSrcBlend, colorDstBlend)` pair (`Opaque`→`SDL_BLENDMODE_NONE`; `Additive`→`SDL_BLENDMODE_ADD`) and fell through to a single generic `SDL_BLENDMODE_BLEND` constant for EVERYTHING else — meaning `BlendState::AlphaBlend` (`colorSrc=One, colorDst=InverseSourceAlpha`) and `BlendState::NonPremultiplied` (`colorSrc=SourceAlpha, colorDst=InverseSourceAlpha`) were **indistinguishable** on SDL_Renderer, despite XNA defining genuinely different blend equations for premultiplied vs. non-premultiplied source colour data (`AlphaBlend`: `dst=src·1+dst·(1-srcA)`, SDL's `SDL_BLENDMODE_BLEND_PREMULTIPLIED`; `NonPremultiplied`: `dst=src·srcA+dst·(1-srcA)`, SDL's `SDL_BLENDMODE_BLEND`). The old fallback happened to be exactly correct for `NonPremultiplied` but silently wrong for `AlphaBlend`. **Fix**: replaced the 2-special-case mapping with a complete, general `Blend`/`BlendFunction` → `SDL_BlendFactor`/`SDL_BlendOperation` table + `SDL_ComposeCustomBlendMode()`, covering all 6 factor/operation parameters XNA's `BlendState` exposes. Confirmed via SDL3's own `SDL_BlendFactor` enum that 10 of XNA's 13 `Blend` values have EXACT SDL equivalents — only `Blend::BlendFactor`/`InverseBlendFactor` (a constant blend-factor colour via `GraphicsDevice.BlendFactor`, no SDL equivalent) and `SourceAlphaSaturation` (`min(As,1-As)`, no SDL equivalent) lack one; these now throw a clear `std::runtime_error` instead of silently substituting a wrong factor. **This also precisely resolves Task 700's premise** ("`SDL_ComposeCustomBlendMode` has a narrower factor set than XNA") — true only for those 3 rare values (2 of which are the SAME underlying gap: no constant-colour factor), not the 10 commonly-used ones every standard preset and virtually all custom `BlendState`s rely on; `SDL_BlendOperation`'s 5 values (`Add`/`Subtract`/`RevSubtract`/`Min`/`Max`) map 1:1 to XNA's `BlendFunction` too. All 4 standard presets use `BlendFunction::Add` for both channels (confirmed via `BlendState.cpp`'s constructors), and `SDL_ComposeCustomBlendMode`'s `ADD` operation is universally supported by every SDL renderer backend per its own doc comment — so this fix is fully safe and reliable for the 4 presets this task specifically audits (Subtract/RevSubtract/Min/Max support varies by renderer backend, e.g. OpenGL's own doc comment says it "supports the `SDL_BLENDOPERATION_ADD` operation with all factors" only — a real, separate, narrower limitation that only matters for a genuinely custom, non-Add `BlendState`, squarely Task 700's own scope, not this one). **Bug 2, found while verifying Bug 1's fix (`SdlSpriteBatchBackend::Begin()`)**: this method unconditionally called `SDL_SetRenderDrawBlendMode(renderer, SDL_BLENDMODE_BLEND)` on every `SpriteBatch::Begin()`, **clobbering whatever `ApplyBlendState` had just set** moments earlier via `GraphicsDevice::setBlendStateProperty(blendState)` (which `SpriteBatch::Begin()`'s own sequence calls immediately before `backend_->Begin()` runs — confirmed by reading `SpriteBatch.cpp`'s exact call order). This bug was **completely invisible** while Bug 1 was still present, since `AlphaBlend`/`NonPremultiplied` both happened to resolve to that exact same `SDL_BLENDMODE_BLEND` value anyway — clobbering to it changed nothing observable. Once Bug 1 was fixed (making `AlphaBlend` correctly resolve to a genuinely different mode), Bug 2 became a real, directly-observable regression: **every single `SpriteBatch::Begin()` call was silently discarding its own just-applied blend state and forcing plain `SDL_BLENDMODE_BLEND` instead**, for every draw across the whole project. Fixed by removing the hardcoded reset entirely (redundant given `ApplyBlendState` always runs first). **Discriminating power independently verified via `git stash`** (both fixes together, since Bug 2 only became independently testable after Bug 1 landed): reverting `SdlGraphicsBackend.cpp` reproduced the exact predicted failure (`AlphaBlend` read back as the `NonPremultiplied` result, and the two presets were indistinguishable again); restored (confirmed clean `git diff`) and reconfirmed all 3 checks pass. Also independently confirmed via a raw, CNA-bypassing standalone C probe (compiled directly against the exact vendored SDL3 build CNA links, run under the same `Xvfb :99`) that `SDL_ComposeCustomBlendMode`+`SDL_SetRenderDrawBlendMode`+`SDL_SetTextureBlendMode`+`SDL_RenderTexture` alone (no CNA code at all) produce the textbook-correct premultiplied vs. straight-alpha blend results, isolating that the mapping logic itself was sound and the remaining discrepancy was Bug 2, inside CNA's own `Begin()`. New `sdlrenderer_blendstate_audit_test.cpp`: draws a half-transparent (`alpha=128`) solid Red quad (straight, non-premultiplied alpha) over a solid Green background, once with each of `AlphaBlend`/`NonPremultiplied`; `AlphaBlend` now correctly reproduces XNA's own well-known "washed-out/over-bright" artifact for non-premultiplied content (`≈(255,127,0)`), `NonPremultiplied` correctly performs a proper straight-alpha blend (`≈(128,127,0)`), and the two are now genuinely, meaningfully different (previously identical). All 3 checks pass. Full regression: `ctest` 4285/4314 passed, 2 skipped, 13 failed (same already-documented baseline as Tasks 667-694 — zero new failures, confirming this fundamental, backend-wide blend-mode change doesn't regress ANY existing test, including every prior `SpriteBatch`/`SpriteFont`/`Texture2D` pixel test this session that implicitly exercises `BlendState::Opaque`). **Scope note**: `Opaque` and `Additive` (the 2 presets the old code already special-cased correctly) are unaffected by either bug and remain correct; dedicated per-preset pixel tests for all 4 presets are Tasks 696-699's own, separately-scoped job. |
| 696 | Pixel test: `BlendState::Opaque` on SDL_Renderer | ✅ | **CLOSED — no bug found; `BlendState::Opaque` correctly ignores alpha and fully overwrites the destination on SDL_Renderer.** Already confirmed correct by Task 695's own audit (`Opaque` was one of the 2 presets the old, incomplete mapping already special-cased correctly, and remains correctly mapped — `colorSrc=One, colorDst=Zero` — under the new general `SDL_ComposeCustomBlendMode`-based mapping too); this is a dedicated, focused pixel-test verification of that specific preset now that `AlphaBlend`/`NonPremultiplied` are correctly distinguished (Task 695). New `sdlrenderer_blendstate_opaque_test.cpp`: draws a LOW-alpha (`64/255`) Red tint over a solid Green background with `BlendState::Opaque`; the result must be pure, fully-opaque Red (alpha and destination both completely ignored), not any blend with the background. Check passes. **Discriminating power independently verified**: sabotaging the `Blend::Zero → SDL_BLENDFACTOR_ZERO` mapping (forcing `SDL_BLENDFACTOR_ONE` instead) reproduced the exact predicted failure — the background bled through additively, producing yellow (`255,255,0`) instead of pure Red; restored (confirmed clean `git diff`) and reconfirmed pass. Full regression: `ctest` 4285/4315 passed, 2 skipped, 13 failed (same already-documented baseline as Tasks 667-695 — zero new failures). |

New `sdlrenderer_blendstate_alphablend_test.cpp`: a 1x1 texture holding correctly premultiplied Red at `alpha=128` (`R=255*128/255≈128`, not the full `255`), drawn over a solid Blue background with `BlendState::AlphaBlend` (`dst = src·1 + dst·(1-srcA)`); expected `≈(128,0,127)` — a proper 50/50 red/blue blend. Check passes. **Discriminating power independently verified**: sabotaging the `Blend::InverseSourceAlpha → SDL_BLENDFACTOR_ONE_MINUS_SRC_ALPHA` mapping (forcing `SDL_BLENDFACTOR_ZERO` instead) reproduced the exact predicted failure — the destination was completely ignored, producing pure Red (`128,0,0`) instead of the blended result; restored (confirmed clean `git diff`) and reconfirmed pass.
