| 698 | Pixel test: `BlendState::NonPremultiplied` on SDL_Renderer | ✅ | **CLOSED — no bug found; `BlendState::NonPremultiplied` correctly performs the straight-alpha blend equation on SDL_Renderer.** This preset's factors (`SourceAlpha`, `InverseSourceAlpha`) exactly match SDL's plain `SDL_BLENDMODE_BLEND` — the same value the OLD, incomplete mapping already fell back to for anything besides `Opaque`/`Additive` — so `NonPremultiplied` was already correct even BEFORE Task 695's fix (unlike `AlphaBlend`, which needed the fix); this task is a dedicated, focused pixel-test verification of that specific preset now under the new general `SDL_ComposeCustomBlendMode`-based mapping too, confirming no regression. New `sdlrenderer_blendstate_nonpremultiplied_test.cpp`: a half-transparent (`alpha=128`), straight (non-premultiplied) Red quad — full-brightness `R=255` with `alpha=128`, as a typical unprocessed image would store it — drawn over a solid Blue background; expected `dst = src·srcA + dst·(1-srcA) ≈ (128,0,127)` — a proper 50/50 blend. Check passes. **Discriminating power independently verified**: sabotaging the `Blend::SourceAlpha → SDL_BLENDFACTOR_SRC_ALPHA` mapping (forcing `SDL_BLENDFACTOR_ONE` instead) reproduced the exact predicted failure — the source colour was applied at full brightness instead of scaled by its own alpha, producing `(255,0,127)` instead of the correct blended `(128,0,127)`; restored (confirmed clean `git diff`) and reconfirmed pass. Full regression: `ctest` 4302/4317 passed, 2 skipped, 13 failed (same already-documented 13-failure baseline as Tasks 667-697 — zero new failures). |
| 699 | Pixel test: `BlendState::Additive` saturation behavior on SDL_Renderer | ✅ | **CLOSED — no bug found; `BlendState::Additive` correctly adds (alpha-scaled) source colour onto the destination, including correct saturation/clamping at 255 without wraparound, on SDL_Renderer.** Already confirmed correct by Task 695's audit (`Additive` was one of the 2 presets the old, incomplete mapping already special-cased correctly via `SDL_BLENDMODE_ADD`, and remains correctly mapped via the new general `SDL_ComposeCustomBlendMode`-based mapping too); this is a dedicated, focused pixel-test verification. New `sdlrenderer_blendstate_additive_test.cpp`: two checks — (1) non-saturating: half-alpha (`128/255`) dim Red (`100,0,0`) drawn over a dim Red (`50,0,0`) background, `dst=src·srcA+dst·1≈(100,0,0)`, confirming both the `SourceAlpha` colour scaling and the additive combination when the result stays representable; (2) saturating: full-alpha bright Red (`200,0,0`) drawn over an already-bright Red (`200,0,0`) background — mathematically `(400,0,0)`, must clamp to `(255,0,0)` rather than wrap or misbehave, the specific "saturation behavior" this task's own row names. Both checks pass. **Discriminating power independently verified**: sabotaging the `BlendFunction::Add → SDL_BLENDOPERATION_ADD` mapping (forcing `SDL_BLENDOPERATION_SUBTRACT` instead) reproduced the exact predicted failure — both checks read black (`0,0,0`, the subtracted/clamped-negative result) instead of the correct additive sums; restored (confirmed clean `git diff`) and reconfirmed both pass. Full regression: `ctest` 4303/4318 passed, 2 skipped, 13 failed (same already-documented 13-failure baseline as Tasks 667-698 — zero new failures). **This closes the entire dedicated per-preset pixel-test set (696-699); all 4 `BlendState` presets are now individually, empirically verified correct on SDL_Renderer.** |
| 700 | Decide and document custom (non-preset) `BlendState` combinations on SDL_Renderer | ✅ | **CLOSED — decision was already implemented by Task 695's fix; this task's own job was to verify and precisely document the outcome, which surfaced one additional real finding.** Task 695 replaced the old 2-preset-special-case mapping with a general `Blend`/`BlendFunction` → `SDL_BlendFactor`/`SDL_BlendOperation` table + `SDL_ComposeCustomBlendMode()`, which handles ANY custom, non-preset `BlendState` built from the 10 XNA `Blend` values with exact SDL equivalents — confirmed here with a genuinely custom `(colorSrc=DestinationColor, colorDst=Zero)` "modulate"/colour-filter blend (not one of the 4 XNA presets), which composes and applies correctly. **Decision 1 (factors)**: `Blend::BlendFactor`/`InverseBlendFactor` (a constant blend-factor colour via `GraphicsDevice.BlendFactor`, no SDL equivalent) and `Blend::SourceAlphaSaturation` (`min(As,1-As)`, no SDL equivalent) throw `std::runtime_error` rather than silently substituting a wrong factor — this row's own premise ("narrower factor set") is real but precisely narrower than implied: only these 3 of XNA's 13 `Blend` values lack an SDL equivalent, not the whole API surface, and none of the 4 standard presets or any built-in CNA effect uses them. **Decision 2 (operations), the one genuinely NEW finding from this task**: investigated `BlendFunction::Subtract`/`ReverseSubtract`/`Max`/`Min` empirically (not just theoretically) — SDL3's own `SDL_ComposeCustomBlendMode` doc comment explicitly documents only `SDL_BLENDOPERATION_ADD`-with-all-factors support for the `opengl` driver (unlike `direct3d`/`direct3d11`/`opengles2`, which get broader documented support) — confirmed via a real `BlendFunction::Subtract` test on this project's own actual SDL_Renderer/OpenGL sandbox that it DOES compose and apply correctly here (`src-dst`, matching XNA's own `BlendFunction::Subtract` docstring exactly — first test draft had the operands backwards, self-corrected and reconfirmed with `src>dst` for a non-degenerate, non-clamped-to-zero result). **Decision**: no throw is added for non-Add `BlendFunction` values (they DO work on this sandbox), but this is documented as a "confirmed-working-here, not universally-guaranteed-on-every-OpenGL-driver" caveat rather than silently assumed reliable everywhere — matches this task's own "document fallback ... per unsupported combination" framing without overreaching into a throw that would break a genuinely working case. New `sdlrenderer_blendstate_custom_test.cpp`: 4 checks — custom modulate blend, `BlendFactor` throws, `SourceAlphaSaturation` throws, `BlendFunction::Subtract` composes and applies correctly. All pass. **Discriminating power independently verified for all 3 newly-exercised code paths** (the previously-untested `DestinationColor` factor mapping, the throw-on-unsupported-factor default case, and the `Subtract` operation mapping): sabotaging `Blend::DestinationColor→DST_COLOR` to `ONE` broke the modulate check (unmodulated source colour leaked through) and simultaneously sabotaging the throw case to return a valid factor instead broke both throw checks (silently rendered instead of throwing) — all 3 failed exactly as predicted in one combined sabotage pass, `Subtract` unaffected; separately, sabotaging `BlendFunction::Subtract→SUBTRACT` to `ADD` broke only the `Subtract` check (wrong additive-summed result instead of the correct subtraction) while the other 3 stayed correct. Both sabotages restored (confirmed clean `git diff`) and reconfirmed all 4 pass. Full regression: `ctest` 4304/4319 passed, 2 skipped, 13 failed (same already-documented 13-failure baseline as Tasks 667-699 — zero new failures). **This closes the entire BlendState section (695-700) of the SDL_Renderer audit.** |

### SamplerState on SDL_Renderer

| #   | Task | Status | Notes |
| --- | ---- | ------ | ----- |
| 701 | Audit `SamplerState` → `SDL_ScaleMode` + address-mode mapping | ✅ | **CLOSED — real bug found and fixed on SDL_Renderer; also consolidates/cross-references prior findings from Tasks 685/686/687/688.** `TextureFilter` has 9 values encoding separate min/mag/mip filter components (per `TextureFilter.hpp`'s own doc comments: "shrink" = minification, "expand" = magnification) — `Linear`(0, mag=Linear), `Point`(1, mag=Point), `Anisotropic`(2, linear-based), `LinearMipPoint`(3, mag=Linear), `PointMipLinear`(4, mag=Point), `MinLinearMagPointMipLinear`(5, mag=Point), `MinLinearMagPointMipPoint`(6, mag=Point), `MinPointMagLinearMipLinear`(7, mag=Linear), `MinPointMagLinearMipPoint`(8, mag=Linear). The old `SdlSpriteBatchBackend::SetSamplerFilter` mapping only treated `textureFilter==0` as `SDL_SCALEMODE_LINEAR`, silently downgrading `Anisotropic`/`LinearMipPoint`/`MinPointMagLinearMipLinear`/`MinPointMagLinearMipPoint` (4 of 9 values, all with a Linear magnification component) to `SDL_SCALEMODE_NEAREST` instead. Since SDL_Renderer's 2D blit pipeline has no separate min/mag/mip control and no LOD/mipmap-level sampling at all, and `SpriteBatch` draws are near-universally magnification-dominant (sprites scaled up or 1:1, never minified with proper LOD selection on this backend), the magnification ("expand") component is the one that visibly matters — fixed by switching to a `switch` over the 5 mag=Linear values (`{0,2,3,7,8}`) vs. everything else (mag=Point, `{1,4,5,6}`). New `sdlrenderer_samplerstate_filter_audit_test.cpp`: uses `SamplerState::AnisotropicClamp` (a real, existing XNA preset using `TextureFilter::Anisotropic`), mirroring Task 688's exact texel-boundary methodology (2-texel Red\|Green texture stretched wide, sampled at the exact boundary between the two texel centers) — confirms the boundary now reads a genuine blend (LINEAR magnification), not a pure colour (NEAREST). Check passes. **Discriminating power independently verified via `git stash`**: reverting the fix reproduced the exact predicted failure (Anisotropic read back pure, unblended Green instead of a blend); restored (confirmed clean `git diff`) and reconfirmed pass. **Address-mode mapping**: no new work needed here — already thoroughly audited across Tasks 685 (`Clamp` correct by accident of `SDL_RenderTexture`'s fixed edge behavior, not real `SamplerState` honoring), 686/687 (`Wrap`/`Mirror` genuinely broken, BLOCKED pending a project-owner decision on the `SDL_RenderGeometry`-rewrite-vs-throw question). Full regression: `ctest` 4305/4320 passed, 2 skipped, 13 failed (same already-documented 13-failure baseline as Tasks 667-700 — zero new failures). |
| 702 | Verify default sampler state matches FNA's `LinearClamp` on SDL_Renderer | ✅ | **No bug found — the no-arg `SpriteBatch::Begin()` overload genuinely resolves to `SamplerState::LinearClamp`, byte-for-byte, on SDL_Renderer.** `SpriteBatch.cpp`'s own `Begin()` resolves a null/omitted `samplerState` via `samplerState ? *samplerState : SamplerState::LinearClamp` — confirmed here that this isn't just correct plumbing but genuinely observable in the rendered pixels. Methodology note: an earlier draft of this test tried to hand-derive the expected pixel colour at one combined boundary+out-of-bounds sample point (mirroring Tasks 685/688's individual methodologies at once) and got ambiguous partial-blend values that were hard to interpret in isolation at this backbuffer's low resolution (2-texel-wide texture, 64px-wide backbuffer) — rather than keep re-deriving "what should this exact byte value be", the test was redesigned to sidestep that question entirely: render the same draw (2x1 Red\|Blue texture, oversized `sourceRectangle` spanning texel range [0,4] on a 2-texel texture, Task 685's methodology) once via the no-arg `Begin()` and once via `Begin(Deferred, Opaque, &SamplerState::LinearClamp, nullptr, nullptr)`, and compare 8 sample points across the draw (including the texel boundary and the out-of-bounds clamp region) for exact equality — this proves "the default IS LinearClamp" operationally without needing an independent ground truth for what LinearClamp itself should look like at each point. New `sdlrenderer_samplerstate_default_test.cpp`: all 8 sample points byte-identical between the two renders (including non-trivial partial-blend values like `(60,0,195)` at the clamp-region sample — an interesting but out-of-scope observation for a future task is that this isn't a perfectly flat clamped value, suggesting some blend bleed near the clamped edge under `SDL_RenderTexture`'s oversized-srcrect handling combined with `SDL_SCALEMODE_LINEAR`; not investigated further here since both renders reproduce it identically, which is exactly what this task asks to verify). All 8 samples + the overall check pass. **Discriminating power verified**: sabotaging the default-resolution fallback in `SpriteBatch::Begin()` from `SamplerState::LinearClamp` to `SamplerState::PointClamp` made 4 of the 8 sample points diverge from the (unchanged) explicit-`LinearClamp` render exactly as predicted (the point-filtered texel-boundary and clamp-region samples went sharp/pure instead of matching the blended `LinearClamp` values); restored (confirmed clean `git diff`) and reconfirmed all 8 pass. Full regression: `ctest` 4306/4321 passed, 2 skipped, 13 failed (same baseline as Tasks 667-701 — zero new failures). |
| 703 | Verify per-draw sampler state changes take effect on the next `SpriteBatch::Begin` on SDL_Renderer | ✅ | **No bug found — `SamplerState` genuinely switches, in both directions, across consecutive `Begin()`/`End()` cycles on SDL_Renderer.** `SdlSpriteBatchBackend::SetSamplerFilter` (called once per `SpriteBatch::Begin()`, per `SpriteBatch.cpp`'s own `Begin()` sequence) stores its result into a single `scaleMode` member; every `Draw()` overload re-applies it via `SDL_SetTextureScaleMode()` immediately before rendering — read the source first to confirm this really is a live per-Begin write, not a one-time construction-time default, before writing the test. New `sdlrenderer_samplerstate_perdraw_switch_test.cpp`: draws the same 2x1 Red\|Blue texture 3 times in sequence, each via its own `Begin()`/`Draw()`/`End()` cycle, sampling the exact texel boundary each time — (1) `PointClamp` reads a pure colour, (2) `LinearClamp` on the very next `Begin()` now reads a genuine blend, (3) `PointClamp` again reverts to pure — the 3rd check specifically rules out a one-directional/sticky bug where switching TO Linear works but switching back does not. All 3 pass. **Discriminating power verified**: sabotaging `SetSamplerFilter` with a `static bool appliedOnce` guard (so only the FIRST call's filter ever takes effect, simulating a "stuck at construction" bug) made check 2 fail exactly as predicted (stayed pure instead of blending, since the Linear request was silently dropped) while checks 1 and 3 coincidentally still passed (both requested Point, already the stuck value); restored (confirmed clean `git diff`) and reconfirmed all 3 pass. **Closes the entire SamplerState section (701-703).** Full regression: `ctest` 4307/4322 passed, 2 skipped, 13 failed (same baseline as Tasks 667-702 — zero new failures). |

### RenderTarget2D on SDL_Renderer

| #   | Task | Status | Notes |
| --- | ---- | ------ | ----- |
| 704 | Audit `RenderTarget2D` construction on SDL_Renderer | ✅ | **Real bug found and fixed in SHARED code (`GraphicsDevice.cpp`) — affects EVERY backend, not just SDL_Renderer; this is the first SDL_Renderer-specific `RenderTarget2D` test written this session, so it was the first thing to actually exercise this path on this backend.** `GraphicsDevice::SetRenderTarget(RenderTarget2D*)`/`SetRenderTargets(...)` both implement FNA's `RenderTargetUsage::DiscardContents` semantics (the DEFAULT usage for every simple constructor) by auto-clearing on bind — but did so UNCONDITIONALLY via `Clear(ClearOptions::Target | ClearOptions::DepthBuffer, ...)`, regardless of whether the target actually has a depth-stencil buffer at all. `RenderTarget2D`'s own constructor only allocates a depth buffer when `DepthStencilFormat != DepthFormat::None`; SDL_Renderer additionally has NO depth-buffer concept whatsoever (`ClearColorAndDepth` is unconditionally `ThrowNo3D`) — so simply calling `GraphicsDevice::SetRenderTarget(&rt)` on ANY `RenderTarget2D` with the (default!) `DiscardContents` usage crashed immediately with an uncaught `std::runtime_error("SDL_Renderer does not support 3D: ClearColorAndDepth")`, even for a plain `DepthFormat::None` target — meaning basic render-target binding was completely unusable on this backend before this fix, undiscovered until this task wrote the first test to actually call `SetRenderTarget` here. Fixed by checking `renderTarget->getDepthStencilFormatProperty() != DepthFormat::None` before including `ClearOptions::DepthBuffer` in the auto-clear, in both `SetRenderTarget(RenderTarget2D*)` and `SetRenderTargets(...)` — matches real-hardware/FNA semantics too (no conceptual reason to clear a depth buffer that was never allocated). New `sdlrenderer_rendertarget2d_construction_test.cpp`: (1) property-wiring checks across the 2-arg and full 8-arg constructors — `Width`/`Height` match requested; `DepthStencilFormat`/`RenderTargetUsage` echo the requested value verbatim (FNA: plain field-store, no device clamping); `MultiSampleCount` is device-clamped to 0 regardless of what was requested, since `IRenderTargetBackend::GetMultiSampleCount()` defaults to 0 and `SdlRenderTargetBackend` never overrides it (SDL_Renderer's 2D blit pipeline has no MSAA support at all); (2) bind/draw/unbind isolation — a White marker drawn on the backbuffer BEFORE binding a `RenderTarget2D` survives completely unchanged after the target is bound, cleared/drawn into, and unbound (proving `SDL_SetRenderTarget` genuinely redirects drawing to the target's own off-screen `SDL_TEXTUREACCESS_TARGET` texture, not the visible backbuffer), and a draw issued AFTER unbinding correctly lands back on the backbuffer. All 10 checks pass. **Discriminating power via `git stash`**: reverting the `GraphicsDevice.cpp` fix reproduced the exact predicted crash (`SDL_Renderer does not support 3D: ClearColorAndDepth`) the instant `SetRenderTarget(&rt)` was called with the default-usage, no-depth target; restored (confirmed clean `git diff`) and reconfirmed all 10 pass. **Cross-backend regression** (shared-code fix): EasyGL's full `RenderTarget`-related test subset (36 tests — `RenderTarget2D`/`RenderTargetCube` readback, depth-buffer, mip, MSAA-resolve, properties, `SetRenderTargets` validation) — 36/36 passed, zero regressions, confirming the added depth-buffer guard is a no-op for targets that DO have a real depth format (unchanged behavior) and correctly skips the depth-clear only for `DepthFormat::None` targets. Additionally ran EasyGL's full suite (4433 tests) as a broader sanity check on this shared-code change: 3 pre-existing failures (`EasyGL_MRT_TwoAttachments`, `EasyGL_GraphicsDevice_ReferenceStencil`, `easy-gl-resource-smoke-tests`), each independently confirmed via `git stash` to fail identically with the fix reverted — unrelated to this change. SDL_Renderer full regression: `ctest` 4308/4323 passed, 2 skipped, 13 failed (same baseline as Tasks 667-703, PLUS the new `SDL_Renderer_RenderTarget2D_Construction` test passing — zero new failures; 2 extra `AudioCategoryTest`/`CueTest` failures in the full run confirmed flaky, both passed cleanly on isolated rerun). **This is the first task in the RenderTarget2D section (704-709).** |
| 705 | Verify `RenderTarget2D` can be sampled as `Texture2D` after unbinding on SDL_Renderer | ✅ | **Real memory-safety bug found and fixed — undefined behavior in ALL 3 `SdlSpriteBatchBackend::Draw()` overloads, latent since this backend's inception, never before observably manifesting.** `RenderTarget2D`'s backend (`SdlRenderTargetBackend`) is a SIBLING class of `SdlTextureBackend` — both derive independently from `ITextureBackend`, neither derives from the other — but all 3 `Draw()` overloads unconditionally did `auto& sdlTex = static_cast<const SdlTextureBackend&>(texture);` and then accessed `sdlTex.texture`/`sdlTex.width`/`sdlTex.height` as raw data members. Whenever `texture` is actually an `SdlRenderTargetBackend` (i.e. any time a `RenderTarget2D` is drawn via `SpriteBatch` after being unbound — exactly this task's own subject), this downcast is undefined behavior: an unchecked cast between unrelated classes that only share a common base. On THIS project's current compiler/ABI it happened to silently "work" (both classes declare `SDL_Texture* texture` as their first data member after a single vtable pointer, so the field coincidentally lands at the same offset) — meaning a plain pixel-readback test could not distinguish fixed from broken; **built a throwaway `-DCNA_SANITIZE=address,undefined` scratchpad configuration (this project's existing diagnostics-only option, previously used for Task 689) specifically to prove it**, since UBSan's `vptr` check is designed exactly for "downcast to a type the object isn't really an instance of". Fixed by switching to the already-existing, already-safe virtual accessors `ITextureBackend::GetNativeTexture()`/`GetWidth()`/`GetHeight()` (declared for exactly this purpose, per the interface's own `// TODO: SDL dependency should be abstracted later` comment) — eliminates the cast entirely, safe for either concrete backend. New `sdlrenderer_rendertarget2d_sample_test.cpp`: renders a Blue background + Red corner marker into a bound `RenderTarget2D`, unbinds it, then draws the render target itself via `SpriteBatch::Draw` (exactly as a plain `Texture2D`) onto the backbuffer and reads back both regions — both checks pass. **Discriminating power**: the ORDINARY (non-sanitizer) build passes identically with or without the fix (confirming the UB is currently silently benign on this ABI, not that the fix is unnecessary) — so verification used the UBSan scratchpad build instead: with the bug reverted (`git stash`), the exact same test run produced 6 distinct UBSan `vptr` errors (`runtime error: member access within address ... which does not point to an object of type 'SdlTextureBackend' ... object is of type ... SdlRenderTargetBackend`), one per `sdlTex.`-member-access call site; restored (confirmed clean `git diff`) and reconfirmed the identical UBSan build now runs with zero sanitizer errors and both pixel checks still passing. Full regression: `ctest` 4309/4324 passed, 2 skipped, 13 failed (same baseline as Tasks 667-704 — zero new failures; this fix is SDL_Renderer-specific code, `SdlGraphicsBackend.cpp`, not shared, so no cross-backend spot-check was needed). |
| 706 | Verify `RenderTargetUsage::DiscardContents` vs `PreserveContents` on SDL_Renderer | ✅ | **No bug found — the two usages are genuinely, observably different on SDL_Renderer, matching FNA's intent.** `GraphicsDevice::SetRenderTarget(RenderTarget2D*)` auto-clears to `Color(0,0,0,255)` on EVERY bind when the target's `RenderTargetUsage` is `DiscardContents` (Task 704's fix corrected this to skip the depth component for depth-less targets); for `PreserveContents`, no auto-clear runs at all, relying on the fact that an `SDL_TEXTUREACCESS_TARGET` texture's backing GPU storage is a normal, persistent texture whose content survives being unbound/rebound unless something explicitly overwrites it — this needed no dedicated backend code, only correct non-interference. New `sdlrenderer_rendertarget_usage_test.cpp`: creates one `RenderTarget2D` of each usage, draws a distinctive Green into each once, unbinds both, then rebinds each WITHOUT drawing or clearing anything new and immediately unbinds again (isolating the auto-clear-on-bind mechanism itself from any fresh drawing) — sampling afterward shows `DiscardContents`' Green is GONE (auto-cleared to black on the untouched rebind) while `PreserveContents`' Green is STILL there. Both checks pass. **Discriminating power verified**: sabotaging the `DiscardContents` condition to `renderTarget && false && ...` (disabling the auto-clear entirely) made the `DiscardContents` check fail exactly as predicted (Green survived the rebind, indistinguishable from `PreserveContents`); restored (confirmed clean `git diff`) and reconfirmed both pass. Full regression: `ctest` 4310/4325 passed, 2 skipped, 13 failed (same baseline as Tasks 667-705 — one extra `AudioCategoryTest.PauseOnParentCategoryPausesCueInChildCategory` failure in the full run confirmed flaky on isolated retry, unrelated to this change). |
| 707 | Verify `GetBackBufferData` reads correct pixels after `SetRenderTarget(nullptr)` restores the backbuffer on SDL_Renderer | ✅ | **No bug found — `GetBackBufferData` genuinely reads the real backbuffer's own content after `SetRenderTarget(nullptr)`, both via the XNA-level `Viewport` and the actual backend readback.** `SdlGraphicsBackend::ReadBackbuffer` branches on `SDL_GetRenderTarget(renderer) == nullptr` to decide whether to map coordinates through `SDL_GetRenderLogicalPresentationRect()` (the real-backbuffer path) or treat them as direct render-target texture space — so its correctness after unbinding hinges on `SetRenderTarget2D(nullptr)` genuinely calling `SDL_SetRenderTarget(renderer, nullptr)`. Tasks 704-706's tests all used explicit small `Rectangle` regions; this test specifically exercises the NO-RECT, whole-backbuffer `GetBackBufferData(Color*, int)` overload (which resolves its region via `backend_->GetViewportSize()`), deliberately binding a render target SMALLER than the backbuffer in between to probe for any stale-size/stale-content leak. New `sdlrenderer_getbackbufferdata_after_rt_unbind_test.cpp`: (1) control — full no-rect readback is all Magenta before any RT activity; (2) `GraphicsDevice`'s own `Viewport` correctly reverts to the backbuffer's W/H after unbinding a smaller RT; (3) the full no-rect readback (every element, including the tail) is still all Magenta after the smaller RT was bound, cleared to a different colour (Cyan), and unbound. All 3 pass. **Discriminating power verified via 2 separate sabotages**: (a) omitting the XNA-level `Viewport`/`ScissorRectangle` reset on unbind (in `GraphicsDevice::SetRenderTarget`) broke check 2 exactly as predicted — but, interestingly, did NOT affect check 3, since `SdlGraphicsBackend::GetViewportSize()` returns the backend's own fixed logical width/height (tied to the window/backbuffer configuration, never the currently-bound render target's size) rather than reading `GraphicsDevice`'s XNA-level `Viewport` property — a useful clarifying finding: these two "reverts to backbuffer size" mechanisms are independent, not the same code path; (b) separately omitting the actual `SDL_SetRenderTarget(renderer, nullptr)` call in `SdlGraphicsBackend::SetRenderTarget2D`'s unbind branch broke check 3 exactly as predicted (readback showed the stale Cyan render-target content instead of Magenta) while leaving check 2 unaffected. Both sabotages restored (confirmed clean `git diff` on both files) and reconfirmed all 3 pass. Full regression: `ctest` 4311/4326 passed, 2 skipped, 13 failed (same baseline as Tasks 667-706 — zero new failures). |
| 708 | Decide and document render-target depth-buffer behavior on SDL_Renderer | ✅ | **Decision: silently ignore any requested `DepthFormat` rather than throwing — plus a real bug found and fixed in SHARED code while verifying it.** `SdlGraphicsBackend::CreateRenderTarget2D` never allocates real depth-stencil storage regardless of what was requested (Task 704's own finding); `RenderTarget2D::DepthStencilFormat` still echoes back whatever was requested verbatim, matching FNA's own plain field-store semantics (`RenderTarget2D.cs`'s constructor never clamps `DepthStencilFormat` against device capability either — only `MultiSampleCount` gets that treatment, via `FNA3D_GetMaxMultiSampleCount`). **Rationale for ignoring rather than throwing**: SDL_Renderer's 2D sprite pipeline never performs real depth testing under ANY circumstance regardless of which render target is bound (`SpriteBatch` never depth-tests on any backend, and this backend's `SetDepthTestEnabled`/`ClearDepth`/`ClearColorAndDepth` already throw unconditionally the instant anything actually TRIES to use depth testing) — so a caller requesting a depth format purely for portability with the other 3 depth-capable backends, without ever issuing an actual depth-testing draw call, hits no functional difference on this backend and should not be penalized with a throw for an inert, unactionable request. **Real bug found while verifying this decision**: Task 704's own fix decided whether to include `ClearOptions::DepthBuffer` in the render-target auto-clear-on-bind by checking the XNA-level REQUESTED `DepthStencilFormat` property — which reflects what the CALLER asked for, not whether the backend actually allocated real storage. Since SDL_Renderer never allocates one regardless of request, simply constructing a `RenderTarget2D` with a real depth format (e.g. `Depth24Stencil8`) and binding it with the default `DiscardContents` usage crashed immediately via `ClearColorAndDepth`'s unconditional `ThrowNo3D` — the exact decision this task was supposed to resolve, still unresolved by Task 704's narrower fix. Fixed by adding `IRenderTargetBackend::HasRealDepthBuffer(bool depthFormatWasRequested)` to the shared backend interface (`IGraphicsBackend.hpp`), defaulting to `return depthFormatWasRequested;` — an exact behavioral mirror of Task 704's original check, so EasyGL/Vulkan/Bgfx (which DO honor requested depth formats) need zero code changes — with `SdlRenderTargetBackend` overriding it to always return `false`. `GraphicsDevice::SetRenderTarget`/`SetRenderTargets` now query the backend via this method instead of trusting the XNA-level property directly. New `sdlrenderer_rendertarget_depth_decision_test.cpp`: (1) `DepthStencilFormat` echoes `Depth24Stencil8` verbatim; (2) binding a real-depth-format `RenderTarget2D` no longer throws; (3) the full draw/unbind cycle completes without incident; (4) decision boundary — explicitly requesting a depth-buffer clear on the real backbuffer still throws, confirming ignoring `RenderTarget2D`'s `DepthFormat` doesn't silently unlock depth-testing functionality that this backend genuinely doesn't have. All 4 pass. **Discriminating power via `git stash`** (SDL-specific override only): reverting reproduced the exact predicted crash (`SDL_Renderer does not support 3D: ClearColorAndDepth`) on check 2 specifically, other 3 checks unaffected; restored (confirmed clean `git diff`) and reconfirmed all 4 pass. **Cross-backend verification** (shared interface + `GraphicsDevice.cpp` change): EasyGL's full `RenderTarget` test subset (36 tests) — 36/36 passed, zero regressions (confirms the default `HasRealDepthBuffer` mirrors old behavior exactly); Vulkan and Bgfx's `CNA` target both recompiled cleanly against the new virtual method. Full regression: `ctest` 4312/4327 passed, 2 skipped, 13 failed (same baseline as Tasks 667-707 — zero new failures). |
| 709 | Verify MRT (`SetRenderTargets` with 2+ bindings) throws clearly on SDL_Renderer | ✅ | **Real bug found and fixed — `SetRenderTargets` silently bound only the first target and ignored the rest, plus a significant process-methodology finding that corrects the SDL_Renderer known-failure baseline for Tasks 704-708.** `SdlGraphicsBackend` never overrode `IGraphicsBackend::SetRenderTargets`, so the shared default (`SetRenderTarget2D(count > 0 ? rts[0] : nullptr)`) ran unmodified — for `count > 1`, this silently binds ONLY the first target and drops the rest with no error, meaning a game submitting genuine MRT draws (intending separate content per target) would either lose data silently or have every draw land on the same texture. Fixed by overriding `SdlGraphicsBackend::SetRenderTargets` to throw a clear `std::runtime_error` whenever `count > 1`, matching this backend's single-active-render-target reality. **Process finding, discovered while investigating this task**: `GraphicsDeviceValidationTest.SetRenderTargets_FourTargets_DoesNotThrow`/`_OneTarget_DoesNotThrow` — 2 of the "13 known SDL_Renderer baseline failures" carried forward unchanged in every task write-up since Task 667 — were ALREADY PASSING again by the time this task started, because Tasks 704 and 708's `GraphicsDevice.cpp` fixes had already resolved their actual failure cause (an unconditional `ClearColorAndDepth` call on render-target bind). This had gone unnoticed across Tasks 704-708's own regression write-ups because only the touched example-test target(s) and the `CNA` static library were rebuilt before each `ctest` run, never the separate `CnaTests` unit-test binary — `ctest` never builds anything itself, so it silently ran a STALE `CnaTests` executable each time. **The TRUE SDL_Renderer known-failure baseline from Task 704 onward should have read 11, not 13** (confirmed by rebuilding `CnaTests` fresh and re-running: both tests now pass). This is corrected here, and going forward every full regression in this stretch does a full `cmake --build cmake-build-sdl` (not just the touched targets) before `ctest`. **New conflict resolved by this task's own fix**: making `SetRenderTargets` throw for `count > 1` now makes `SetRenderTargets_FourTargets_DoesNotThrow` throw again on SDL_Renderer specifically — correctly so, since that test's own name/history (Task 881) refers to the backend-agnostic `MAX_RENDERTARGET_BINDINGS=4` cap-check, not SDL_Renderer's own MRT capability; updated with a `#if defined(CNA_BACKEND_SDL_RENDERER)` conditional (matching the established pattern already used in `TextureCubeTests.cpp`) so it now correctly expects a throw on SDL_Renderer while still expecting no-throw on the 3 real-MRT-capable backends. New `sdlrenderer_rendertargets_mrt_throws_test.cpp`: (1) `SetRenderTargets` with 2 bindings throws clearly; (2) `GraphicsDevice` remains fully usable afterward (a normal backbuffer draw succeeds), proving the throw doesn't leave the device in a corrupted half-bound state. Both pass. **Discriminating power via `git stash`**: reverting the SDL-specific override reproduced the exact predicted silent-no-throw behavior on check 1 only, check 2 unaffected; restored (confirmed clean `git diff`) and reconfirmed both pass. **Cross-backend verification**: EasyGL's `GraphicsDeviceValidationTest` suite (4 tests, including the now-conditional one) — 4/4 passed, confirming the `#else` branch (no-throw expectation) is untouched for real-MRT-capable backends. Full regression (fresh full rebuild first, per the corrected process): `ctest` 4316/4328 passed, 3 skipped, 12 failed (TRUE baseline now 11 known failures, not 13, plus 1 flaky `AudioCategoryTest` confirmed via 3x isolated rerun — zero new failures). **This closes the entire RenderTarget2D section (704-709).** |

### Viewport / PresentationParameters / GraphicsDeviceManager on SDL_Renderer

| #   | Task | Status | Notes |
| --- | ---- | ------ | ----- |
| 710 | Verify `Viewport` get/set round-trip and `Project`/`Unproject` math on SDL_Renderer | ✅ | **No bug found — this is the first task in the Viewport/PresentationParameters section (710-715).** `Viewport::Project`/`Unproject` are pure, backend-agnostic math with no GPU dependency at all — already exhaustively unit-tested in `ViewportTests.cpp` (identity/non-identity matrices, round-trips, inverted depth, equal min/max depth, etc.), so this task's genuinely NEW, SDL_Renderer-specific contribution is an INTEGRATION check: tying that already-verified math to the REAL, LIVE `GraphicsDevice::Viewport` this backend actually reports, using a genuine 2D orthographic projection (`Matrix::CreateOrthographicOffCenter(0, W, H, 0, 0, 1)`, the screen-space Y-down convention a 2D game would actually use for e.g. mouse-coordinate transforms) — this row's own "2D orthographic case" hint. Also specifically exercises a NON-ZERO `Viewport` X/Y offset, which every existing `ViewportTests.cpp` `Project`/`Unproject` case omits (all use `Viewport(0, 0, w, h)`) — a genuinely uncovered combination before this test. New `sdlrenderer_viewport_project_unproject_test.cpp`: (1) the initial `GraphicsDevice::Viewport` matches the configured backbuffer size with `X=Y=0`; (2) a custom `Viewport(8, 4, 32, 16)` (non-zero offset) round-trips exactly through `setViewportProperty`/`getViewportProperty`; (3)-(5) world `(0,0)`/`(W,H)`/`(W/2,H/2)` project to the live viewport's own top-left/bottom-right/centre respectively, offset correctly included; (6)-(7) `Unproject(Project(...))` round-trips back to the original world points. All 7 pass. **Discriminating power verified**: sabotaging `Viewport::Project` to drop the `+X_`/`+Y_` offset terms broke exactly the 5 offset-dependent checks (3 through 7) while leaving the 2 offset-independent get/set checks unaffected, precisely as predicted; restored (confirmed clean `git diff`, zero net change to the shared `Viewport.cpp`) and reconfirmed all 7 pass. Since this task's own fix (the sabotage-and-revert) left zero net change to shared code — only a new, purely additive SDL_Renderer-specific test file was added — no cross-backend spot-check was needed. Full regression (full rebuild first): `ctest` 4318/4329 passed, 2 skipped, 11 failed (exactly the true baseline, zero flaky tests this run, zero new failures). |
| 711 | Verify backbuffer resize through `PresentationParameters` on SDL_Renderer | ✅ | **No bug found, plus a real methodology finding about resize timing.** `GraphicsDevice::Reset(presentationParameters)` stores the new `PresentationParameters`, calls `applyPresentationParametersToWindow()` (shared code — calls the real `SDL_SetWindowSize()`), then `backend_->SetVirtualResolution(w,h)` to reconfigure logical presentation, then `UpdateViewportFromWindow()`. First test draft called `GetBackBufferData` immediately after `Reset()`, in the SAME `Draw()` call — this threw `"ReadBackbuffer: physical/logical size mismatch"`, since `SDL_SetWindowSize` is ASYNCHRONOUS under X11: the real window resize only actually lands and gets detected by `SdlGraphicsBackend::Present()`'s own output-size re-check (`SDL_GetRenderOutputSize` compared against `lastOutputW_`/`lastOutputH_`) on a LATER frame, not synchronously inside `Reset()` itself. This is correct, deliberate behavior (matches `ReadBackbuffer`'s own documented "no silent wrong data" design), not a bug — a real game would typically call `Reset()`/`ApplyChanges()` from `Update()`, with the resize settling before the next `Draw()`. Fixed the TEST (not production code) by deferring the post-resize pixel checks to the following `Draw()` call, giving the game loop's own natural per-frame `Present()` cycle a chance to detect and apply the real resize first — confirmed via the log's own two-stage `"Output size changed"` progression (`0x0→32x16`, then `32x16→64x32`). New `sdlrenderer_presentationparameters_resize_test.cpp`: initial `PresentationParameters`/`Viewport` match the configured 32x16 backbuffer; a full-viewport fill confirms the original edge; `Reset()` resizes to 64x32; `PresentationParameters`/`Viewport` report the new size immediately (XNA-level state updates synchronously); a fresh fill on the NEXT frame reads back correctly near the NEW, larger edge (well past the old 32x16 bounds), confirming the real rendering surface genuinely grew. All 6 pass. **Discriminating power verified**: sabotaging `applyPresentationParametersToWindow` to skip the real `SDL_SetWindowSize` call reproduced the exact predicted failure — the physical window never actually grows, so the deferred frame's readback still throws the same physical/logical mismatch (confirming the real window genuinely wasn't resized, not just a timing artifact); restored (confirmed clean `git diff`) and reconfirmed all 6 pass. Since this task shipped zero net change to shared code (sabotage-and-revert only; the actual fix was to the TEST's own call pattern, not production code), no cross-backend spot-check was needed. Full regression (full rebuild first): `ctest` 4319/4330 passed, 3 skipped, 11 failed (exactly the true baseline, zero new failures). |
| 712 | Verify fullscreen toggle on SDL_Renderer | ✅ | **No bug found — verified the XNA-level contract this project can actually guarantee under Xvfb.** `GraphicsDevice::applyPresentationParametersToWindow()` calls `SDL_SetWindowFullscreen(window_, fullScreen)`; Task 902's own comment on that exact call already documents that fullscreen switching may not actually be available in headless/virtual-display test environments (Xvfb) — a backend that can't really switch fullscreen still ends up with the correct stored `PresentationParameters` state (`SDL_ClearError()` swallows the OS-level failure non-fatally). `GraphicsDevice::GetWindowInternal()` (the only accessor to the real `SDL_Window*`) is `private`, so this test cannot directly inspect the real `SDL_WINDOW_FULLSCREEN` flag — scoped accordingly to what's actually verifiable: `PresentationParameters.IsFullScreen` round-trips correctly through `Reset()` in both directions, the toggle never throws, and the device remains fully functional (a normal draw+readback still works) after toggling fullscreen on and back off. New `sdlrenderer_fullscreen_toggle_test.cpp`: 6 checks, all pass; confirmed the real output size stays unchanged throughout (32x16 the whole run per the log), consistent with Xvfb not actually honoring the fullscreen request. **Discriminating power verified**: sabotaging `GraphicsDevice::Reset` to force `presentationParameters_.setIsFullScreenProperty(false)` regardless of what was requested broke exactly the one check verifying the property reports `true` after toggling on, leaving the other 5 checks (including the windowed-toggle-back check, which coincidentally also expects `false`) unaffected; restored (confirmed clean `git diff`) and reconfirmed all 6 pass. Since this task shipped zero net code change (sabotage-and-revert only), no cross-backend spot-check was needed. Full regression (full rebuild first): `ctest` 4319/4331 passed, 3 skipped, 12 failed (true baseline 11, plus 1 flaky `AudioCategoryTest` confirmed via 3x isolated rerun — zero new failures). |
| 713 | Verify `PresentInterval` (vsync) mapping to `SDL_SetRenderVSync` on SDL_Renderer | ✅ | **Real bug found and fixed on SDL_Renderer.** `GraphicsDevice.cpp`'s own `toSwapInterval()` maps `PresentInterval` to an int: `Immediate`→0, `Default`/`One`→1, `Two`→2. `SdlGraphicsBackend::SetSwapInterval` previously collapsed ANY positive value down to 1 via `interval > 0 ? 1 : 0` — silently discarding `PresentInterval::Two`'s own documented semantics (`PresentInterval.hpp`: "Wait for two vertical retrace periods before updating; frame rate is capped to half the refresh rate"). SDL3's own `SDL_SetRenderVSync` doc comment confirms passing `2` directly means exactly that ("synchronize present with every second vertical refresh") — an exact match for XNA's semantics. Fixed by passing `interval` straight through instead of collapsing it, with a fallback to `1` if the driver rejects the requested value outright (SDL's own doc: "Not every value is supported by every driver"). **Empirical finding via a standalone, non-shipped raw SDL3 probe** (matching Task 695's precedent for isolating SDL/GL mechanism questions from CNA's own code): confirmed `SDL_SetRenderVSync(renderer, 2)` genuinely exists and has the documented semantics, but THIS project's own sandbox OpenGL driver rejects it (`ok=false`, readback stays at the previous value, no crash) — meaning this fix's core behavioral improvement (honoring `Two` distinctly from `One`) is real and correct per the documented API contract, but not independently pixel/behavior-observable in THIS specific test environment (both old and new code end up at vsync=1 here, since the new code's own graceful fallback also lands on 1). New `sdlrenderer_presentinterval_test.cpp`: cycles through all 4 `PresentInterval` values via `GraphicsDevice::Reset()` — confirms none throw and `PresentationParameters.PresentationInterval` round-trips exactly for each; confirms the device remains fully functional afterward. All 9 checks pass. **Discriminating power**: honestly scoped to what's actually testable — `GraphicsDevice::GetRendererInternal()`/`GetWindowInternal()` are both `private` (this project's own established convention against leaking backend-specific types through the XNA-facing API), so there is no public accessor to query the real, live SDL vsync state from a test, and (as established above) this sandbox's own driver can't distinguish old-vs-new behavior anyway; the round-trip/no-throw/device-functional checks are backend-independent by design and would pass identically with or without this fix — this is documented honestly rather than claiming discriminating power the test doesn't have. Since the fix is SDL_Renderer-specific code (`SdlGraphicsBackend.cpp`), not shared, no cross-backend spot-check was needed. Full regression (full rebuild first): `ctest` 4319/4332 passed, 3 skipped, 13 failed (true baseline 11, plus 2 flaky `AudioCategoryTest` failures confirmed via 3x isolated rerun — zero new failures). |
| 714 | Decide and document `MultiSampleCount` behavior on SDL_Renderer | ✅ | **Decision: accept-and-ignore-with-log — matches Task 708's `DepthFormat` decision shape exactly.** SDL_Renderer's 2D blit pipeline has no MSAA control at all; the shared `IGraphicsBackend::ApplyMultiSampleCount`/`GetMultiSampleCount` defaults already return 0 unconditionally (SDL_Renderer never overrode either), so `GraphicsDevice::Reset()`'s existing FNA-matching write-back (`presentationParameters_.setMultiSampleCountProperty(backend_->ApplyMultiSampleCount(requested))`) was ALREADY correctly reporting 0 back regardless of what was requested — no throw, no crash, already correct by construction via the shared default. **Rationale for accepting rather than throwing**: `SpriteBatch`'s 2D draws have no anti-aliasing seams to smooth over in the first place (unlike 3D geometry edges), so a caller requesting MSAA purely for portability with the other 3 MSAA-capable backends, without depending on any particular sample count being honored, hits no functional difference here and shouldn't be penalized with a throw for an inert, unactionable request. Added `SdlGraphicsBackend::ApplyMultiSampleCount` as an explicit override (previously relying on the shared default silently) — same 0 return value, but now logs once per non-zero request so a game that expects MSAA and doesn't see it has a diagnostic trail, matching this project's established `SDL_Log` conventions elsewhere in this file. New `sdlrenderer_multisamplecount_decision_test.cpp`: default `MultiSampleCount` is 0; requesting 4 via `Reset()` doesn't throw; the value is clamped back to 0 afterward (matches FNA's device-clamped write-back semantics); the device remains fully functional afterward. All 4 pass. **Discriminating power — honestly scoped**: reverting the new `ApplyMultiSampleCount` override (`git stash`) left all 4 checks passing IDENTICALLY, since the shared default ALREADY returned 0 without throwing — the override's only observable difference is the new diagnostic `SDL_Log` line (confirmed present with the override, absent without it), which this test does not assert on (matching this session's established convention of not scraping stdout for pass/fail); documented honestly rather than claiming discriminating power the pass/fail checks don't have. Since the fix is SDL_Renderer-specific code (`SdlGraphicsBackend.cpp`/`.hpp`), not shared, no cross-backend spot-check was needed. Full regression (full rebuild first): `ctest` 4320/4333 passed, 3 skipped, 12 failed (true baseline 11, plus 1 flaky `AudioCategoryTest` confirmed via 3x isolated rerun — zero new failures). |
| 715 | Verify `DeviceResetting`/`DeviceReset` events fire correctly on SDL_Renderer backbuffer resize | ✅ | **No bug found — this closes the entire Viewport/PresentationParameters section (710-715).** `GraphicsDevice::Reset()` (shared, backend-agnostic code) raises `DeviceResetting` BEFORE storing the new `PresentationParameters`, and `DeviceReset` AFTER the whole reconfiguration (backend resolution update, viewport refresh) completes. Exercised here specifically against a REAL SDL_Renderer backbuffer resize (Task 711's own scenario) to confirm what's genuinely NEW beyond the shared event mechanism's own correctness (already implicit elsewhere): both events fire exactly once per `Reset()` call, in the correct order, and — the crux of this task — `DeviceResetting`'s handler observes the OLD (pre-resize) `PresentationParameters.BackBufferWidth` while `DeviceReset`'s handler observes the NEW (post-resize) value, not both reflecting the same stale-or-already-updated state. New `sdlrenderer_devicereset_events_test.cpp`: subscribes to both events, resizes 32x16→64x32 via `Reset()`, confirms exactly-once firing, correct ordering, and the OLD/NEW width split. All 6 pass. **Discriminating power verified**: sabotaging `Reset()` to fire `DeviceResetting` right before `DeviceReset` (after all state mutation, instead of before) broke exactly the one check verifying `DeviceResetting` sees the OLD width (now saw the NEW width instead), leaving the other 5 checks unaffected (including the "fires before" ordering check, which still coincidentally held since both events were still raised in the same relative order, just both too late); restored (confirmed clean `git diff`) and reconfirmed all 6 pass. Since this task shipped zero net code change (sabotage-and-revert only, in shared `GraphicsDevice.cpp`), no cross-backend spot-check was needed. Full regression (full rebuild first): `ctest` 4321/4334 passed, 2 skipped, 13 failed (true baseline 11, plus 2 flaky `AudioCategoryTest` failures confirmed via 3x isolated rerun — zero new failures). |

### GraphicsDevice / resource lifecycle on SDL_Renderer

| 716 | Verify `GraphicsDevice::Clear` (all `ClearOptions` combinations) on SDL_Renderer | ✅ | **No bug found — first task in the GraphicsDevice-lifecycle section (716-719); reconfirms Task 871's already-tracked, cross-backend `Stencil` gap in its concrete SDL_Renderer manifestation, but does not fix it (separately scoped).** `ClearOptions` is a 3-bit flags enum (`Target=1`, `DepthBuffer=2`, `Stencil=4`, 8 combinations including "none"); `GraphicsDevice::Clear(ClearOptions, ...)` only ever branches on `Target`/`DepthBuffer` — its `stencil` parameter is explicitly discarded (`(void)stencil;`), already listed in `NEXT.md`'s §5 as "Confirmed, universal, not fixed" (Task 871). On SDL_Renderer specifically, ANY combination including `DepthBuffer` routes to `ClearDepth`/`ClearColorAndDepth`, both unconditional `ThrowNo3D` (this backend has no depth buffer at all) — so the only non-throwing combinations are `Target` alone, `Stencil` alone (a genuine no-op per the Task 871 gap), and `Target\|Stencil` (Target clears normally, Stencil silently ignored). New `sdlrenderer_clearoptions_audit_test.cpp`: systematically exercises all of these — `Target` alone clears the color target correctly; `DepthBuffer` alone, `Target\|DepthBuffer`, `DepthBuffer\|Stencil`, and `Target\|DepthBuffer\|Stencil` all throw cleanly; `Stencil` alone doesn't throw and is a genuine no-op (a prior Green fill survives completely untouched); `Target\|Stencil` clears the color target with `Stencil` silently ignored. All 10 checks pass. **Discriminating power verified**: sabotaging the `clearTarget` flag computation to always report `true` regardless of the requested `ClearOptions` broke exactly the one check verifying `Stencil` alone is a genuine no-op (now incorrectly also cleared the target), leaving all 9 other checks unaffected (the `DepthBuffer`-throw checks' internal branching also shifted under the sabotage, but their OBSERVABLE behavior — throwing — stayed identical either way, since both `ClearDepth` and `ClearColorAndDepth` throw unconditionally on this backend); restored (confirmed clean `git diff`) and reconfirmed all 10 pass. Since this task shipped zero net code change (sabotage-and-revert only, in shared `GraphicsDevice.cpp`), no cross-backend spot-check was needed. Full regression (full rebuild first): `ctest` 4324/4335 passed, 2 skipped, 11 failed (exactly the true baseline, zero flaky tests this run, zero new failures). |
| 717 | Verify disposed-resource guards throw `ObjectDisposedException` consistently on SDL_Renderer | ✅ | **THREE real bugs found and fixed in SHARED code — two genuine crash-on-disposed-resource-use scenarios, plus a missing consistency guard.** Researched CNA's actual disposed-guard policy first (matches FNA's own deliberately narrow one: guards live at `GraphicsDevice` CONSUMPTION points — `SetRenderTarget`/`SetRenderTargets`, `TextureCollection`'s slot assignment — not on every instance method of `BlendState`/`SamplerState`/`SpriteBatch`, which FNA itself never guards either). Writing the systematic test surfaced two independent, previously-undiscovered memory-safety bugs: **Bug 1** — `SpriteBatch::pushSprite` (the single funnel point for every `Draw`/`DrawString` overload) had NO guard at all for the texture being drawn; a fully-disposed `Texture2D` has its `backend_` (a `shared_ptr<ITextureBackend>`) reset to null, and `Texture2D::GetBackend()` unconditionally dereferences it (`return *backend_;`) with no null check — a GUARANTEED crash (virtual call through a reference to address 0) the instant a disposed texture reached `flushSingle`/`flushBatch`, worse than even FNA's own leniency (whose managed runtime fails more safely). **Bug 2** — `GraphicsDevice::SetRenderTargets` (the plural/MRT overload) was missing the disposed-check its sibling `SetRenderTarget` (singular) already has, letting a disposed `RenderTarget2D` reach `GetRenderTargetBackend()`. **Bug 3**, found while diagnosing Bug 2 — `RenderTarget2D::GetRenderTargetBackend()` returns `rtBackend_`, a raw non-owning pointer CACHED ONCE at construction time into the object owned by `Texture2D::backend_`; `RenderTarget2D::Dispose()` never cleared it, so `GetRenderTargetBackend()` returned a genuinely DANGLING pointer post-disposal (a use-after-free the instant anything dereferenced it) — Bug 2 was the immediately-reachable trigger, but Bug 3 is a deeper, independently-real defense-in-depth gap (any future code path calling `GetRenderTargetBackend()` on a disposed target would hit the same dangling pointer, not just `SetRenderTargets`). Fixed: (1) `SpriteBatch::pushSprite` now throws `ObjectDisposedException` for a disposed texture before ever touching its backend; (2) `SetRenderTargets` now checks each bound `RenderTarget2D` for disposal, matching `SetRenderTarget`'s existing pattern, throwing before ever reaching `GetRenderTargetBackend()`; (3) `RenderTarget2D::Dispose()` now clears `rtBackend_` to `nullptr`, matching `Texture2D::Dispose()`'s own "backend becomes null on dispose" consistency. New `sdlrenderer_disposed_guards_test.cpp`: `SetRenderTarget`/`SetRenderTargets` both throw for a disposed `RenderTarget2D`; `SpriteBatch::Draw` now throws for a disposed `Texture2D` (previously crashed); `setBlendStateProperty`/`SpriteBatch::Begin` with a disposed `BlendState`/`SamplerState` correctly do NOT throw (matches FNA's own lack of guard there); a disposed `SpriteBatch`'s `Begin`/`Draw`/`End` still work normally (CNA's `Dispose()` doesn't tear down anything they depend on, matching FNA's own lack of self-guard); the device remains fully functional throughout. All 7 checks pass. **Discriminating power verified via 3 separate, precisely-isolated sabotages**: reverting the `SpriteBatch.cpp` fix alone reproduced the exact predicted crash at check 3's `End()` (Deferred sort mode just queues on `Draw()`, so the crash surfaces at flush time, not immediately); reverting BOTH `GraphicsDevice.cpp` and `RenderTarget2D.cpp` fixes together reproduced the original crash at check 2; restoring ONLY the `GraphicsDevice.cpp`/`SetRenderTargets` check (keeping `RenderTarget2D.cpp`'s `rtBackend_` fix sabotaged) made all 7 checks pass anyway — proving the `SetRenderTargets` guard alone is sufficient to prevent THIS specific crash by short-circuiting before ever reaching the dangling pointer, while the `RenderTarget2D::Dispose()` fix remains independently justified as defense-in-depth for any other path to `GetRenderTargetBackend()`. All 3 fixes restored (confirmed clean `git diff`) and reconfirmed all 7 pass. **Cross-backend verification** (all 3 fixes touch shared code): EasyGL's `RenderTarget`/`SpriteBatch`/`GraphicsDeviceValidationTest`/`Texture2DTest` subset (109 tests) — 109/109 passed; Vulkan and Bgfx's `CNA` targets both recompiled cleanly. Full regression (full rebuild first): `ctest` 4325/4336 passed, 3 skipped, 11 failed (exactly the true baseline, zero flaky tests this run, zero new failures). |
| 718 | Verify double-`Dispose` is safe for every SDL_Renderer-backed resource type | ✅ | **No bug found.** `Texture2D::Dispose()`'s idempotency (and its shared-ownership/copy semantics) was already thoroughly covered by Task 689; this task sweeps the remaining resource types Task 717's own disposed-guard audit touched — `RenderTarget2D`, `BlendState`, `SamplerState`, `SpriteBatch` — confirming a SECOND `Dispose()` call on each is always safe (no crash, no double-free, no corrupted device state), and that each type's already-established Task 717 post-disposal behavior still holds after the second call too. Particular attention to `RenderTarget2D`: Task 717's own fix (clearing the cached `rtBackend_` raw pointer to `nullptr` on `Dispose()`, previously left dangling) needed to be confirmed idempotent too — `shared_ptr::reset()` on an already-null pointer and re-assigning `nullptr` to an already-null raw pointer are both trivially safe, but worth confirming the WHOLE `Dispose()` call chain (including the base `GraphicsResource`'s own `isDisposed_` guard skipping the "still bound" check on the second call) behaves correctly end-to-end. New `sdlrenderer_double_dispose_test.cpp`: `RenderTarget2D` reports disposed and keeps `GetRenderTargetBackend()` safely null after a double-`Dispose()`; a double-disposed `BlendState`/`SamplerState` can still be applied/used via `setBlendStateProperty`/`SpriteBatch::Begin` without throwing (matching Task 717's own "no guard, matches FNA" finding, now reconfirmed post-double-disposal); a double-disposed `SpriteBatch`'s `Begin`/`Draw`/`End` still work normally; the device remains fully functional throughout. All 6 checks pass. **Discriminating power verified**: re-sabotaging Task 717's `rtBackend_ = nullptr;` fix (commenting it out again) broke exactly the one check verifying `GetRenderTargetBackend()` stays null post-double-disposal — failing cleanly this time rather than crashing, since comparing a dangling pointer's VALUE against `nullptr` is safe (only dereferencing it would be UB), a cleaner test signal than Task 717's own crash-based discriminating-power proof for the same fix; restored (confirmed clean `git diff`) and reconfirmed all 6 pass. Since this task shipped zero net code change (only a new test file; the sabotage-and-revert touched an already-committed fix with zero net diff), no cross-backend spot-check was needed. Full regression (full rebuild first): `ctest` 4322/4337 passed, 3 skipped, 12 failed (true baseline 11, plus 1 flaky `AudioCategoryTest.PauseOnParentCategoryPausesCueInChildCategory` failure confirmed via 3x isolated rerun — zero new failures). |
| 719 | Add leak-check test: create/dispose 80 SDL_Renderer textures/render-targets, verify `GetTrackedResourceCount` returns to baseline | ✅ | **No bug found — this closes the entire GraphicsDevice-lifecycle section (716-719).** Mirrors Task 219's EasyGL leak-check (`easygl_resource_leak_test.cpp`), which exercises `GraphicsDevice`'s shared, backend-agnostic resource-tracking infrastructure: `GraphicsResource`'s constructor/`Dispose(bool)` call `GraphicsDevice::AddResourceReference`/`RemoveResourceReference` and fire `ResourceCreated`/`ResourceDestroyed`, feeding the `resources_` vector that `GetTrackedResourceCount()` exposes (NOXNA, `GraphicsDevice.hpp`). Scoped to the two resource types this task's title names — `Texture2D` and `RenderTarget2D` — since `VertexBuffer`/`IndexBuffer` are already covered by Task 219's own (shared-code) test. New `sdlrenderer_resource_leak_test.cpp`: creates 40 of each (80 total) via `GraphicsDeviceManager`+a real SDL_Renderer `GraphicsDevice`, checks `ResourceCreated` fires exactly 80 times and the tracking list grows by exactly 80; disposes all 80, checks `ResourceDestroyed` fires exactly 80 times, the tracking list returns to its pre-creation count, and every `HasBackend()` is `false` (no leaked `SDL_Texture` handle); clears the `unique_ptr`s (no crash); repeats a tiny 2-resource batch to catch one-time-initialization artifacts; confirms the device remains fully functional (`Clear`+`GetBackBufferData` readback) after all the churn. All 8 checks pass on the first run (no crash, unlike some of Task 717's early false starts). **Discriminating power verified**: sabotaged `Texture2D::Dispose(bool)` by commenting out its `backend_.reset();` call (the exact same idempotency-relevant line examined in Tasks 717/718, but here in the un-disposed, first-`Dispose`-call path) — rebuilt and reran, and the "No SDL_Texture handles remain" check failed cleanly (7/8 pass) while every other check (including the tracking-list-returns-to-baseline check, since `GraphicsResource::Dispose` still calls `RemoveResourceReference` regardless of backend cleanup) stayed green, precisely isolating what this specific check catches; restored (confirmed clean `git diff`) and reconfirmed all 8 pass. Since this task shipped zero net production-code change (only a new test file + `CMakeLists.txt` registration; the sabotage-and-revert touched already-shipped code with zero net diff), no cross-backend spot-check was needed — Task 219's own EasyGL test already covers this shared mechanism independently. | 720 | Verify `DrawPrimitives`/`DrawIndexedPrimitives`/`DrawInstancedPrimitives` throw the correct exception type+message on SDL_Renderer | ✅ | **No bug found — start of a new section verifying 3D-drawing-call exception behavior on this 2D-only backend.** Traced the full call chain: `SdlGraphicsBackend::CreateVertexBuffer`/`CreateIndexBuffer16` (and, by default delegation, `CreateIndexBuffer32`) both throw `std::runtime_error("SDL_Renderer does not support 3D: ...")` unconditionally — and this happens INSIDE `VertexBuffer`'s/`IndexBuffer`'s own constructor (their member-initializer list calls `device.GetBackend().CreateVertexBuffer`/`CreateIndexBuffer16` directly, before the constructor body ever runs), meaning a valid `VertexBuffer`/`IndexBuffer` object can **never exist** bound to an SDL_Renderer-backed `GraphicsDevice` in the first place. Consequently `GraphicsDevice::DrawPrimitives`/`DrawIndexedPrimitives`/`DrawInstancedPrimitives` — each of which checks `currentVertexBuffer_ == nullptr` BEFORE ever reaching the backend's own 3D-draw entry points (`DrawPrimitivesEx`/`DrawIndexedPrimitivesEx`/`DrawInstancedPrimitivesEx` → `DrawColoredPrimitives`/`DrawIndexedColoredPrimitives`, which is where SDL_Renderer's OWN `ThrowNo3D` lives) — always throw at that first, SHARED (not SDL_Renderer-specific) check, since `currentVertexBuffer_` can never be non-null on this backend. New `sdlrenderer_drawprimitives_throws_test.cpp` confirms this chain end to end with a helper (`ThrowsExactRuntimeError`) that verifies both the EXACT exception type (`std::runtime_error`, not just any `std::exception`) and the EXACT message text (not just "throws something", per this task's own title): `DrawPrimitives`/`DrawIndexedPrimitives`/`DrawInstancedPrimitives` each throw `"GraphicsDevice::DrawXxx: no vertex buffer is bound."` with the correct method name substituted; constructing a `VertexBuffer` throws `"SDL_Renderer does not support 3D: CreateVertexBuffer"`; constructing an `IndexBuffer` (2-arg ctor, defaults to 16-bit) throws `"SDL_Renderer does not support 3D: CreateIndexBuffer16"`; the device remains fully functional (`Clear`+`GetBackBufferData` readback) after all 5 throws. All 6 checks pass. **Discriminating power verified**: sabotaged `GraphicsDevice::DrawPrimitives`'s message text (`"no vertex buffer is bound."` → `"no vertex buffer bound."`, dropping "is") — rebuilt and reran, and exactly that one check failed (5/6) while the other 5 (including the other two draw entry points' own distinct messages) stayed green, precisely confirming the test's exact-message assertion actually discriminates wording, not just presence of a throw; restored (confirmed clean `git diff`) and reconfirmed all 6 pass. Since this task shipped zero net production-code change (only a new test file + `CMakeLists.txt` registration; the sabotage-and-revert touched already-shipped code with zero net diff), no cross-backend spot-check was needed. | 721 | Verify all 5 `DrawUserPrimitives` typed + `VertexDeclaration` overloads throw correctly on SDL_Renderer | ✅ | **No bug found.** Unlike Task 720's `DrawPrimitives`/`DrawIndexedPrimitives`/`DrawInstancedPrimitives` (which require an ALREADY-bound `VertexBuffer` that, as Task 720 established, can never exist on this backend), `DrawUserPrimitives` builds its OWN transient vertex buffer from caller-supplied data on every call — so each of the 5 overloads (`VertexPositionColor`, `VertexPositionColorTexture`, `VertexPositionTexture`, `VertexPositionNormalTexture`, and the raw-pointer + explicit `VertexDeclaration` overload) checks "has an `Effect` been applied" FIRST, a SHARED (not SDL_Renderer-specific) check — meaning this task exercises TWO distinct, independently-reachable exception paths per overload, not just one. **Path 1 (no effect applied)**: all 5 throw `std::runtime_error("GraphicsDevice::DrawUserPrimitives: no effect has been applied.")`, identical across every backend. **Path 2 (effect applied)**: `BasicEffect::Apply()` itself does NOT throw on SDL_Renderer (effects are backend-agnostic data objects until actually used to draw — matches Task 726's own finding), so `currentEffect_` becomes non-null and each overload proceeds to `backend_->CreateVertexBuffer(n)`, which DOES throw SDL_Renderer's own `std::runtime_error("SDL_Renderer does not support 3D: CreateVertexBuffer")` — the SDL_Renderer-specific throw path that Task 720's draw calls could never actually reach. New `sdlrenderer_drawuserprimitives_throws_test.cpp` exercises both paths for all 5 overloads using the same `ThrowsExactRuntimeError` exact-type+exact-message helper as Task 720 (constructing dummy `VertexPositionColor`/`VertexPositionColorTexture` arrays required explicit `Color::White` initializers, since `Color` has no default constructor — `VertexPositionTexture`/`VertexPositionNormalTexture` have no `Color` member and default-construct fine). All 12 checks pass (5 "no effect" throws + `BasicEffect::Apply()` no-throw + 5 "3D unsupported" throws + device-functional-after). **Discriminating power verified**: sabotaged the `VertexPositionColorTexture` overload's "no effect" message text (dropped "has been" → "no effect applied.") — rebuilt and reran, and exactly that one check failed (11/12) while the other 4 overloads' own distinct per-type checks (and both Path-2 checks for the SAME overload) stayed green, precisely isolating per-overload message wording; restored (confirmed clean `git diff`) and reconfirmed all 12 pass.
