Full regression (full rebuild first): `ctest` 4325/4340 passed, 3 skipped, 12 failed (true baseline 11, plus 1 flaky `AudioCategoryTest.PauseOnParentCategoryPausesCueInChildCategory` failure confirmed via 3x isolated rerun — zero new failures). |
| 722 | Verify all 10 `DrawUserIndexedPrimitives` typed + `VertexDeclaration` overloads throw correctly on SDL_Renderer | ✅ | **No bug found.** All 10 overloads (`VertexPositionColor`/`VertexPositionColorTexture`/`VertexPositionTexture`/`VertexPositionNormalTexture` × {16-bit, 32-bit index}, plus the raw-pointer + explicit `VertexDeclaration` overload × {16-bit, 32-bit index}) follow the exact same two-path shape Task 721 established for `DrawUserPrimitives`: **Path 1 (no effect applied)** — all 10 throw the shared `std::runtime_error("GraphicsDevice::DrawUserIndexedPrimitives: no effect has been applied.")`. **Path 2 (effect applied)** — a finding worth confirming explicitly rather than assuming: EVERY one of the 10 implementations calls `backend_->CreateVertexBuffer(numVertices)` BEFORE `backend_->CreateIndexBuffer16/32(indexCount)` — so `CreateVertexBuffer`'s own `std::runtime_error("SDL_Renderer does not support 3D: CreateVertexBuffer")` always fires FIRST, for BOTH the 16-bit and 32-bit index overloads alike. This means `CreateIndexBuffer16`/`CreateIndexBuffer32`'s own `ThrowNo3D` messages are NEVER actually reachable through any `DrawUserIndexedPrimitives` call on this backend — the vertex buffer creation always throws first, regardless of index width. New `sdlrenderer_drawuserindexedprimitives_throws_test.cpp`: 22 checks (10 "no effect" throws + `BasicEffect::Apply()` no-throw + 10 "3D unsupported" throws + device-functional-after), all using the same exact-type+exact-message `ThrowsExactRuntimeError` helper as Tasks 720/721. All 22 checks pass. **Discriminating power verified**: sabotaged the `VertexPositionNormalTexture` (16-bit index) overload's "no effect" message text (dropped "has been" → "no effect applied.") — rebuilt and reran, and exactly that one check failed (21/22) while the other 9 overloads' own distinct per-type/per-width checks (and both Path-2 checks for the SAME overload) stayed green; restored (confirmed clean `git diff`) and reconfirmed all 22 pass. Since this task shipped zero net production-code change (only a new test file + `CMakeLists.txt` registration; the sabotage-and-revert touched already-shipped code with zero net diff), no cross-backend spot-check was needed. Full regression (full rebuild first): `ctest` 4328/4341 passed, 2 skipped, 11 failed (exactly the true baseline, zero flaky tests this run, zero new failures). |
| 723 | Re-verify `CreateVertexBuffer`/`CreateIndexBuffer`/Dynamic variants throw correctly on SDL_Renderer with Task-261-style rigor | ✅ | **No bug found.** An earlier phase covered this at a lighter pass (Tasks 720-722's own construction-throw checks used only the 2-arg `VertexBuffer(device, count)`/`IndexBuffer(device, count)` overloads); this task re-verifies with genuine Task-261-style rigor by exercising EVERY construction overload and variant: `VertexBuffer(device, count)`, `VertexBuffer(device, VertexDeclaration, count, BufferUsage)`, `DynamicVertexBuffer(device, VertexDeclaration, count, BufferUsage)`, `IndexBuffer(device, count)` [16-bit default], `IndexBuffer(device, SixteenBits, count, BufferUsage)`, `IndexBuffer(device, ThirtyTwoBits, count, BufferUsage)`, `DynamicIndexBuffer(SixteenBits, ...)`, `DynamicIndexBuffer(ThirtyTwoBits, ...)` — all 8 throw `std::runtime_error` with the exact expected message. Two findings worth documenting explicitly rather than assuming: (1) `SdlGraphicsBackend::CreateIndexBuffer32` is NOT separately overridden on this backend, so it uses `IGraphicsBackend`'s own default delegation straight to `CreateIndexBuffer16` — meaning a 32-bit `IndexBuffer`/`DynamicIndexBuffer` throws the IDENTICAL `"SDL_Renderer does not support 3D: CreateIndexBuffer16"` message as a 16-bit one, never a distinct 32-bit-specific message; (2) `DynamicVertexBuffer`/`DynamicIndexBuffer`'s `dynamic` constructor parameter is a completely unused/ignored `bool` — both delegate straight to `VertexBuffer`'s/`IndexBuffer`'s own private constructor with zero backend-level distinction from their non-dynamic base types. Also confirmed as an explicit edge case (the kind of thing a lighter pass would skip): the backend throw fires UNCONDITIONALLY before any argument validation — `VertexBuffer(device, 0)`, `VertexBuffer(device, -1)`, and `IndexBuffer(device, -1)` all still throw the SAME backend-unsupported message rather than an `ArgumentOutOfRangeException`, since `device.GetBackend().CreateVertexBuffer(...)` runs in the member-initializer list, before the constructor body (where such validation would live, if it existed) ever executes. New `sdlrenderer_buffer_construction_throws_test.cpp`: 12 checks (8 construction-overload throws + 3 zero/negative-count edge cases + device-functional-after), all pass. **Discriminating power verified**: sabotaged `SdlGraphicsBackend::CreateIndexBuffer16`'s message text (`"CreateIndexBuffer16"` → `"CreateIndexBuffer"`) — rebuilt and reran, and exactly the 6 checks depending on that message failed (6/12) while all 6 `VertexBuffer`-dependent checks (which depend on the separate, untouched `CreateVertexBuffer` message) stayed green, precisely isolating the two independent message paths; restored (confirmed clean `git diff`) and reconfirmed all 12 pass. Since this task shipped zero net production-code change (only a new test file + `CMakeLists.txt` registration; the sabotage-and-revert touched `SdlGraphicsBackend.cpp`, backend-specific code, with zero net diff), no cross-backend spot-check was needed. Full regression (full rebuild first): `ctest` 4329/4342 passed, 2 skipped, 11 failed (exactly the true baseline, zero flaky tests this run, zero new failures). |
| 724 | Verify `VertexDeclaration` construction does **not** throw on SDL_Renderer | ✅ | **No bug found.** `VertexDeclaration` is pure data (stride + element list) — unlike `VertexBuffer`/`IndexBuffer` (whose constructors immediately call `device.GetBackend().CreateVertexBuffer`/`CreateIndexBuffer16`, throwing on this 2D-only backend — Tasks 720-723), `VertexDeclaration` extends `GraphicsResource` but EVERY one of its constructors relies on `GraphicsResource`'s default `device = nullptr` argument — it is never tied to any `GraphicsDevice`, let alone a specific backend, at all. New `sdlrenderer_vertexdeclaration_construction_test.cpp`: confirms all 4 constructor overloads (default; `initializer_list<VertexElement>` with auto-computed stride; explicit-stride + `initializer_list`; explicit-stride + `vector<VertexElement>`) construct with no throw; confirms the constructed declaration reports the exact stride/element count it was given; confirms passing the SAME declaration to an actual `DrawUserPrimitives` draw call (Task 721's raw+`VertexDeclaration` overload) still throws — but only AT THE DRAW, never during declaration/construction — proving the "pure data, no backend touch" claim end to end rather than just asserting it; confirms the device stays fully functional throughout. All 7 checks pass. **Discriminating power verified**: sabotaged the `initializer_list` constructor in `VertexDeclaration.cpp` by adding an unconditional `throw std::runtime_error(...)` at its start (temporarily adding `#include <stdexcept>` for it) — rebuilt and reran, and exactly that one check failed (6/7) while the other 3 constructor-overload checks and the draw-still-throws check all stayed green, precisely isolating which constructor the sabotage affected; restored both the throw and the temporary include (confirmed clean `git diff`) and reconfirmed all 7 pass. Since this task shipped zero net production-code change (only a new test file + `CMakeLists.txt` registration) and the finding — `VertexDeclaration` never touches any backend, for any backend — is inherently backend-agnostic by construction (no backend-specific logic exists anywhere in `VertexDeclaration.cpp`), no cross-backend spot-check was needed. Full regression (full rebuild first): `ctest` 4330/4343 passed, 2 skipped, 11 failed (exactly the true baseline, zero flaky tests this run, zero new failures). |
| 725 | Decide and document `Texture3D`/`TextureCube` construction on SDL_Renderer | ⛔ | **Real architecture decision needed — see question below.** Investigated the actual current state: `SdlGraphicsBackend` does NOT override `CreateTexture3D`/`CreateTextureCube` at all, so both fall through to `IGraphicsBackend`'s own default (`return nullptr;` — no throw). `Texture3D`/`TextureCube` construction on SDL_Renderer therefore currently succeeds silently with a permanently-null `backend_`, and `SetData`/`GetData` both explicitly guard `if (backend_) ... / if (!backend_) return;` — silently no-op-ing rather than throwing. Prototyped the "throw at construction" option (adding `ThrowNo3D("CreateTexture3D")`/`ThrowNo3D("CreateTextureCube")` overrides, mirroring `CreateVertexBuffer`/`CreateIndexBuffer16`'s exact Tasks-720-723 pattern) and discovered a MUCH larger blast radius than those tasks: unlike `VertexBuffer`/`IndexBuffer` (whose construction-throw is exercised only by NEW, purpose-built tests added in Tasks 720-723), `Texture3D`/`TextureCube` are constructed directly, with ZERO backend-conditional guards, by **94 existing shared cross-backend `TEST`/`TEST_F` cases** across 3 files (`Texture3DTests.cpp`: 33, `TextureCubeTests.cpp`: 42, `Texture3DTextureCubeRenderTargetTests.cpp`: 19) — `grep -c "^TEST"` confirms the count, and `grep` for `CNA_BACKEND_SDL_RENDERER`/`GTEST_SKIP` across all 3 files returns zero matches. All of these currently PASS on SDL_Renderer (confirmed via this session's own full-regression logs, e.g. `Texture3DTest.ConstructorSetsWidth/Height/Depth/Format`, `MipMapFalseIsAlwaysOne`, etc., none appear in the true 11-failure baseline) precisely BECAUSE construction silently succeeds today. The OTHER option (throw on `SetData`/sampling instead, keeping construction silent) is not blast-radius-free either: `Texture3DTest.SetDataExactElementCountDoesNotThrow` (and likely several siblings across all 3 files) explicitly asserts `EXPECT_NO_THROW(tex.SetData(...))` with valid arguments — making `SetData` throw when `backend_` is null would break this and any equivalent test too. **Question for the project owner:** (a) throw at construction (matches the `VertexBuffer`/`IndexBuffer`/`CreateVertexBuffer` precedent's "fail loud and immediately" philosophy, but requires auditing and adding `#if defined(CNA_BACKEND_SDL_RENDERER)` guards — or a shared per-backend-aware test helper — across an estimated large majority of these 94 existing tests, a materially larger and riskier change than any single task this stretch); or (b) throw on `SetData`/`GetData`/actual-draw instead (smaller blast radius — likely only the small number of tests that specifically exercise successful `SetData`/`GetData` round-trips on this backend — but still requires the same kind of audit, just against a smaller, less obviously-enumerable set, and leaves construction itself silently misleading in the meantime); or (c) leave the current silent-no-op behavior as an accepted, documented 2D-only-backend limitation (zero test changes needed, matches this task's own "match FNA's behavior model as closely as a 2D-only backend can" instruction least well, since FNA itself never silently constructs a dead-on-arrival texture). Not guessing on this per project's own risky-decision policy — leaving BLOCKED until the project owner picks a direction. Reverted the prototyped `ThrowNo3D` overrides in `SdlGraphicsBackend.hpp`/`.cpp` before closing (confirmed clean `git diff`) — zero net production-code change from this task. |
| 726 | Verify all 5 stock 3D effects' `Apply()`+property setters do **not** throw on SDL_Renderer, but drawing with them does | ✅ | **No bug found.** The 5 stock 3D effects are `BasicEffect`, `AlphaTestEffect`, `DualTextureEffect`, `EnvironmentMapEffect`, `SkinnedEffect` (`SpriteEffect` is the 2D sprite effect, not one of the 5, and is out of scope). For each: construction, a representative set of property setters (`World`/`View`/`Projection` matrices, `DiffuseColor`, `Alpha`, a bool toggle — `LightingEnabled`/`PreferPerPixelLighting`/`VertexColorEnabled` as applicable per effect — `Texture` assignment with a real `Texture2D`, plus each effect's own unique setters: `AlphaFunction`/`ReferenceAlpha` for `AlphaTestEffect`, `Texture2` for `DualTextureEffect`, `EnvironmentMapAmount`/`FresnelFactor` for `EnvironmentMapEffect`, `WeightsPerVertex` for `SkinnedEffect`), and `Apply()` all do NOT throw on SDL_Renderer — matching FNA's "effects are backend-agnostic data objects until actually used to draw" model exactly. Traced why: each effect's `OnApply()` only ever calls `Texture2D::GetBackend()` for an ASSIGNED texture (always guarded by an `if` check), never `GraphicsDevice::GetBackend()` directly — and `Texture2D` itself works fine on SDL_Renderer (only `Texture3D`/`TextureCube`/`VertexBuffer`/`IndexBuffer` are 3D-unsupported, Tasks 720-725), so property setters + `Apply()` never touch anything that could throw here, regardless of whether a `Texture2D` is assigned. Deliberately did NOT assign `EnvironmentMapEffect::EnvironmentMap` (a `TextureCube*`) to stay clear of Task 725's currently-BLOCKED architecture decision — only confirmed the plain-pointer-store setter itself (called with `nullptr`) doesn't throw, which needs no `TextureCube` construction at all. Also confirmed, once per effect, that actually DRAWING with it (via `DrawUserPrimitives` immediately after `Apply()`) still throws — tying back to Task 721's established SDL_Renderer 3D-unsupported throw path and confirming the "until actually used to draw" half of the claim, not just the "doesn't throw before that" half. New `sdlrenderer_stock3deffects_apply_test.cpp`: 11 checks (5 construct+setters+`Apply()` no-throw + 5 draw-still-throws + 1 device-functional), all pass. **Discriminating power verified**: sabotaged `BasicEffect::OnApply()` by adding an unconditional `throw std::runtime_error(...)` (temporarily adding `#include <stdexcept>` for it) — rebuilt and reran, and the `BasicEffect` construct+setters+`Apply()` check correctly failed (printed cleanly thanks to unbuffered `stdout`, added to `main()` matching Task 717's established crash-safety convention), followed by an expected process crash from the SEPARATE, unwrapped `fx2.Apply()` call used for the draw-still-throws check — this is the CORRECT behavior for a genuine `OnApply()` regression (any real game calling `Apply()` without expecting it to throw would crash identically), not a test-design flaw; restored both the throw and the temporary include (confirmed clean `git diff`) and reconfirmed all 11 pass. Since this task shipped zero net production-code change (only a new test file + `CMakeLists.txt` registration), no cross-backend spot-check was needed. Full regression (full rebuild first): `ctest` 4331/4344 passed, 2 skipped, 11 failed (exactly the true baseline, zero flaky tests this run, zero new failures). |
| 727 | Verify `OcclusionQuery::Begin`/`End` throw correctly on SDL_Renderer | ✅ | **Real bug found and fixed — unlike Task 725's BLOCKED `Texture3D`/`TextureCube` decision, this one was safe to fix immediately.** `SdlGraphicsBackend` never overrode `CreateOcclusionQuery`, so it fell through to `IGraphicsBackend`'s own default (`return nullptr;` — no throw): `OcclusionQuery` construction silently succeeded with a permanently-null `backend_`, and `Begin()`/`End()` both guard with `if (backend_) backend_->Begin/End();` — silently no-op-ing instead of ever running a real occlusion query. This was inconsistent with every OTHER 3D-only entry point on this backend (`CreateVertexBuffer`, `CreateIndexBuffer16`, `DrawColoredPrimitives`, etc.), which all throw loudly and immediately via the shared `ThrowNo3D` helper. Confirmed this fix was SAFE, unlike Task 725's Texture3D/TextureCube situation: `OcclusionQuery`'s only existing test coverage is `EasyGL_OcclusionQuery_Cycle` (`examples/occlusion_query_test.cpp`), registered ONLY for the EasyGL backend in `CMakeLists.txt` — zero existing SDL_Renderer-run tests construct an `OcclusionQuery` at all, so zero blast radius. Fixed by adding `SdlGraphicsBackend::CreateOcclusionQuery()` → `ThrowNo3D("CreateOcclusionQuery")`, mirroring `CreateVertexBuffer`/`CreateIndexBuffer16`'s exact Tasks 720-723 pattern. Since construction now throws first, `Begin()`/`End()` can never actually be reached on a valid instance on this backend — the same shape as Task 720's `DrawPrimitives`/`VertexBuffer` finding. New `sdlrenderer_occlusionquery_throws_test.cpp`: 2 checks (construction throws the exact expected message + device-functional-after), both pass. **Discriminating power verified via `git stash`**: stashed `SdlGraphicsBackend.hpp`/`.cpp`, rebuilt, and the construction-throws check failed cleanly (1/2) — construction silently succeeded again, matching the pre-fix behavior exactly; `git stash pop` restored the fix, rebuilt, reconfirmed both checks pass. Since the fix is entirely SDL_Renderer-backend-specific code (other backends have their own separate, already-real `CreateOcclusionQuery` implementations, untouched by this change), no cross-backend spot-check was needed. Full regression (full rebuild first): `ctest` 4331/4345 passed, 3 skipped, 11 failed (exactly the true baseline, zero flaky tests this run, zero new failures). |
| 728 | Verify `Model::Draw` throws correctly on SDL_Renderer | ✅ | **No bug found.** `Model::Draw` requires real 3D primitives to reach any throw at all: `Model::Draw` → `ModelMesh::Draw` → `GraphicsDevice::SetVertexBuffer`/`DrawIndexedPrimitives`, per mesh part with an assigned `Effect` and `PrimitiveCount > 0` (parts with no effect or a non-positive count are silently skipped, matching FNA's own `ModelMesh.Draw`). Confirmed zero existing SDL_Renderer test coverage of `Model::Draw` before this task (`ModelMeshTests.cpp` never calls `Draw()` at all — every test there passes a `nullptr` `GraphicsDevice`), so this was safe new coverage with zero blast radius, unlike Task 725's situation. New `sdlrenderer_model_draw_throws_test.cpp`: 2 checks (exact-message throw + device-functional-after), both pass. **Discriminating power verified**: sabotaged the exact message text in `GraphicsDevice::DrawIndexedPrimitives`'s "no vertex buffer is bound" check (dropped "is") — rebuilt and reran, and the exact-message check failed cleanly (1/2) while the device-functional check stayed green; restored (confirmed clean `git diff`) and reconfirmed both pass. Since this task shipped zero net production-code change (only a new test file + `CMakeLists.txt` registration; the sabotage-and-revert touched already-shipped shared code with zero net diff), no cross-backend spot-check was needed. | 729 | Verify `RasterizerState`/`DepthStencilState` can be constructed and assigned without throwing on SDL_Renderer | ✅ | **No bug found — this closes the entire 720-729 numbered task range (3D-drawing-call exception behavior on this 2D-only backend).** Unlike `VertexBuffer`/`IndexBuffer`/`Texture3D`/`TextureCube`/`OcclusionQuery` (whose backend factory methods either throw — Tasks 720-723, 727 — or are currently BLOCKED pending a decision — Task 725), `GraphicsDevice::setRasterizerStateProperty`/`setDepthStencilStateProperty` call `IGraphicsBackend::ApplyRasterizerState`/`ApplyDepthStencilState`, whose documented DEFAULT (in `IGraphicsBackend.hpp`) is an EXPLICIT no-op — `/// Applies a RasterizerState to the backend. Default: no-op.` / the identical doc comment for `DepthStencilState`. `SdlGraphicsBackend` does not override either, so both are genuine, ALREADY-DECIDED, intentional no-ops on this 2D-only backend — a fundamentally different, unambiguous situation from Task 725's Texture3D/TextureCube dilemma (there, the "silent no-op" was an accidental omission with no documented intent either way; here it's the documented, deliberate default). New `sdlrenderer_rasterizer_depthstencil_construction_test.cpp`: confirms `RasterizerState()`/`DepthStencilState()` default construction, plus property setters (`CullMode`, `FillMode`, `ScissorTestEnable`, `DepthBufferEnable`, `DepthBufferWriteEnable`, `StencilEnable`), never throw; confirms `GraphicsDevice::setRasterizerStateProperty`/`setDepthStencilStateProperty` with several presets (`CullNone`, `CullClockwise`, `None`, `Default`) never throw; confirms the assigned state is genuinely STORED and reported back correctly via `getRasterizerStateProperty`/`getDepthStencilStateProperty` (pure data, not silently dropped) even though the GPU-side application is a no-op on this backend; confirms the device stays fully functional throughout. All 11 checks pass. **Discriminating power verified**: sabotaged `GraphicsDevice::setRasterizerStateProperty` by commenting out its `rasterizerState_ = value;` storage line — rebuilt and reran, and exactly the "reports back the exact RasterizerState just assigned" check failed (10/11) while all other checks (including the equivalent `DepthStencilState` round-trip check, which uses a separate, untouched storage member) stayed green; restored (confirmed clean `git diff`) and reconfirmed all 11 pass. Since this task shipped zero net production-code change (only a new test file + `CMakeLists.txt` registration; the sabotage-and-revert touched already-shipped shared code with zero net diff), no cross-backend spot-check was needed. Full regression (full rebuild first): `ctest` 4334/4347 passed, 2 skipped, 11 failed (exactly the true baseline, zero flaky tests this run, zero new failures). |

### Compatibility proof

| #   | Task | Status | Notes |

| 730 | Port and run 5 minimal 2D-only FNA/XNA samples specifically targeting SDL_Renderer | ✅ | **Qualitatively different from Tasks 720-729's narrow single-Draw()-call API-behavior checks — this is real end-to-end compatibility proof via genuine multi-frame `Update()`+`Draw()` sample programs, not isolated exception-path assertions.** No local FNA-Samples repo exists to literally port files from, so "port 5 samples" was interpreted as: reuse an existing genuinely-2D-only CNA sample where one exists, and author new minimal classic-XNA-idiom samples for the rest. Investigation of `CMakeLists.txt` found a concrete pre-existing gap: `cna_demo_2d` (an existing cross-backend 2D sprite demo using the established `--smoke N`/`SetSmokeFrames(int n)` bounded-headless-run convention from `examples/demo_2d/src/Game1.hpp`) already had `Vulkan_Demo2D_SmokeTest` (Task 88) and `Bgfx_Demo2D_SmokeTest` (Task 89) registered, but had ZERO SDL_Renderer registration, despite the demo running correctly on this backend. **Sample 1/5** closes exactly this gap: `SDL_Renderer_Demo2D_SmokeTest` (`cna_demo_2d --smoke 3`). `cna_demo_input` (uses `SpriteBatch` but has no `--smoke` support) was considered as a second reusable sample and deliberately NOT retrofitted — out of scope for this task, which is about proving compatibility via minimal purpose-built samples, not modifying unrelated existing interactive demos. **Samples 2-5** are 4 brand-new, self-contained, deterministic (fixed per-`Update()` increments, not wall-clock-timed) minimal samples, each a real multi-frame game loop rather than a single API call: (2) `sdlrenderer_sample_bouncing_sprite_test.cpp` — classic bouncing-sprite physics (4x4 sprite, 16x16 backbuffer, 5 `Update()` cycles engineered so a bounce is guaranteed, independent `ComputeExpectedPosition()` mirrors the bounce formula, final pixel readback confirms the sprite renders at the exact expected post-bounce position); (3) `sdlrenderer_sample_keyboard_sprite_test.cpp` — keyboard-driven sprite movement via the established `CNA::Internal::Input::InputManager::SetKeyState(Keys::Right, true)` headless-input-injection seam (same pattern as `tests/Microsoft/Xna/Framework/Input/KeyboardInputTests.cpp`), 4 frames of `Keyboard::GetState().IsKeyDown()`-driven movement, exact final position + pixel readback both checked; (4) `sdlrenderer_sample_spritefont_text_test.cpp` — genuine two-glyph STRING rendering (`SpriteFont::DrawString("HI", ...)` against a hand-built 16x8 two-glyph atlas, following Task 690's established "hand-build a minimal fixture" convention since CNA has no XNB content pipeline), proving multi-glyph kerning-driven horizontal layout, not just one enlarged/overlapping glyph — 5 pixel checks (2 glyph interiors + 3 background points); (5) `sdlrenderer_sample_animated_spritesheet_test.cpp` — `Update()`-driven spritesheet frame-index selection feeding `SpriteBatch::Draw`'s `sourceRectangle` (8x4 two-frame sheet, red frame 0 / green frame 1, `frameIndex = frameCounter_ % 2`, 3 `Update()` cycles land on frame 1), final pixel readback confirms the actually-drawn sprite (not the source texture) shows the correct frame, proving `sourceRectangle` selection genuinely reached the real draw call. All 5 registered as new SDL_Renderer `ctest` entries (`SDL_Renderer_Demo2D_SmokeTest`, `SDL_Renderer_Sample_BouncingSprite`, `SDL_Renderer_Sample_KeyboardSprite`, `SDL_Renderer_Sample_SpriteFontText`, `SDL_Renderer_Sample_AnimatedSpritesheet`) via the standard `cna_sdl_test` macro + `ENVIRONMENT "SDL_VIDEODRIVER=x11;DISPLAY=${CNA_TEST_DISPLAY}"`. Hit one compile error along the way: `constexpr Color kWhite(255,255,255,255);` in the SpriteFont sample failed since `Color` is not a literal type (same root cause as Task 721's `VertexPositionColor` finding — `Color` has a virtual base via `IPackedVectorT<UInt32>`) — fixed by changing `constexpr` to `const`. **No bug found** — this task is a coverage/compatibility-proof task, not a bug-hunting one; the only "fix" is the pre-existing Demo2D SDL_Renderer registration gap, now closed. **Discriminating power verified** via representative sabotage-and-revert on sample 2: commented out the X-axis bounce-reversal line (`if (spriteX_ > maxPos) { spriteX_ = maxPos; velX_ = -velX_; }`) in `Update()`, rebuilt, confirmed the "exact post-bounce position" check correctly failed (1/2 — the "a bounce genuinely occurred" check still passed since it only checks the naive-vs-actual trajectory differs, but the *exact position* check failed since the unclamped position now differs from the independently-computed expected one), restored the line, rebuilt, reconfirmed both checks pass (2/2), confirmed clean `git diff` on the sample file afterward. Manually verified all 4 new samples individually under `SDL_VIDEODRIVER=x11 DISPLAY=:99` before wiring into `ctest` (bouncing sprite 2/2, keyboard sprite 2/2, spritefont text 5/5, animated spritesheet 2/2 — all PASS on first try except the deliberately-sabotaged rerun). Since this task shipped zero net production/library code (only `CMakeLists.txt` registration + 4 new self-contained example files), no cross-backend spot-check was needed. Full regression (full rebuild first, `-k` to skip the known unrelated `cna_demo_xact` Content-copy error): `ctest` 4341/4352 passed, 2 skipped, 11 failed — exactly the true baseline, zero flaky-test appearances this run (no `AudioCategoryTest` flake), zero new failures; all 5 new SDL_Renderer sample/demo tests (`#4346`-`#4350`) pass. |
| 731 | Document `docs/sdl-renderer-2d-completeness.md`: final per-feature support table + decisions made for Tasks 676/681/686–687/700/708–709/725, with rationale | ✅ | **CLOSED — pure documentation task, the final task in the entire SDL_Renderer audit phase (Phase 70, Tasks 666-731).** Wrote `docs/sdl-renderer-2d-completeness.md`: a per-feature support table organized by the same 9 sections `plan_graphics.md` itself uses (SpriteBatch, Texture2D, SpriteFont, BlendState, SamplerState, RenderTarget2D, Viewport/PresentationParameters/GraphicsDeviceManager, GraphicsDevice/resource lifecycle, the systematic 3D-throws sweep) plus the Compatibility proof samples (Task 730), each row marked ✅ (fully supported)/⚠️-emulated (works via a backend-specific accommodation, not byte-identical native behavior)/❌-throws-by-design (intentionally unsupported, throws clearly) with a 1-3 sentence rationale citing the verifying task number(s). Explicit, fully-detailed subsections for all 7 named decision tasks: 676 (custom `Effect` via `Begin` — throws), 681 (mip-level `Texture2D::SetData` — throws for `level>0`, small blast radius so safe to resolve immediately), 686-687 (`TextureAddressMode::Wrap`/`Mirror` — still ⛔ BLOCKED, all 3 options summarized verbatim from `plan_graphics.md`/`NEXT.md`, none picked), 700 (custom `BlendState` — 10/13 `Blend` values + all 5 `BlendFunction` values work, 3 throw, non-Add operations empirically confirmed-working-but-not-universally-guaranteed), 708-709 (`RenderTarget2D` `DepthFormat` — silently ignored rather than thrown; MRT — throws), 725 (`Texture3D`/`TextureCube` construction — still ⛔ BLOCKED, all 3 options summarized, none picked). An explicit "Contrast with Task 681" note explains why the structurally-similar Task 681 (small blast radius) resolved immediately while Task 725 (94-test blast radius) remains BLOCKED — same shape of problem, different outcome. Final summary table tallies 15 real bugs found and fixed across the whole audit (2 SpriteBatch, 1 cross-backend SpriteFont, 2 BlendState, 1 SamplerState, 4 RenderTarget2D, 1 PresentInterval, 3 disposed-resource guards, 1 OcclusionQuery), 1 mip-level decision resolved (681), 2 decisions still BLOCKED (686-687, 725). No code/tests changed — matches Task 725's own docs-only closing pattern, no `ctest` regression needed to close. **This closes the entire SDL_Renderer 2D backend audit phase (Phase 70, Tasks 666-731).** |

## Phase 71 — EasyGL: final gap closure and perfection gate

> Runs second. Deliberately small: EasyGL is already the implicit default target of most
> unlabeled tasks across Phases 34–55 (see the coverage-analysis artifact from this session —
> among 235 pending core tasks, 189 name no backend and default to EasyGL by established
> convention). This phase covers only what genuinely isn't tracked anywhere else yet.

| 732 | Implement real `SurfaceFormat` GPU forwarding on EasyGL | ⛔ | **Real architecture decision needed — see question below.** Investigated the actual current state before writing any code, per this project's own blast-radius-check discipline. Confirmed the task's own premise (`CreateTexture3D`/`CreateTextureCube` hardcode `::metagl::InternalFormat::Rgba8` regardless of the `surfaceFormat` parameter — `EasyGLGraphicsBackend.cpp`'s `EasyGLTexture3DBackend::EasyGLTexture3DBackend(int, int, int, bool, int /*surfaceFormat*/)` and `EasyGLTextureCubeBackend::EasyGLTextureCubeBackend(int, bool, int /*surfaceFormat*/)` both literally comment out the parameter name) — but this dead code is currently **unreachable from any live call path**: the shared, cross-backend `Texture::ValidateFormat(SurfaceFormat)` (`Texture.cpp:109`), called unconditionally by every format-taking `Texture2D`/`Texture3D`/`TextureCube` constructor on ALL FOUR backends, already throws `std::runtime_error` for every `SurfaceFormat` value except `Color` — so none of the 14 formats this task names can ever reach the EasyGL backend's `CreateTexture3D`/`CreateTextureCube` today, regardless of backend. This is not an oversight: it is an existing, ALREADY-SHIPPED, ALREADY-TESTED contract from Task 176 (`examples/easygl_surface_format_throws_test.cpp`), which explicitly asserts `Texture2D`/`Texture3D`/`TextureCube` constructed with `Bgr565`/`HalfVector4`/`HdrBlendable`/`Vector4`/etc. (multiple of the EXACT formats this task names) **must throw** — and is separately, explicitly documented as an accepted, by-design, cross-backend limitation in `NEXT.md` §5 ("`SurfaceFormat` support is Color-only for real GPU formats"). Implementing this task as literally scoped would directly reverse that shipped decision (a regression against Task 176's own test) via a change to SHARED code (`Texture::ValidateFormat`) affecting ALL FOUR backends, not an EasyGL-only fix, despite being filed under an EasyGL-only phase. **Deeper blast radius, found while investigating further**: even if `ValidateFormat` were loosened for these 14 formats, CNA's ENTIRE public `SetData`/`GetData` API surface for `Texture2D`/`TextureCube` is exclusively `Color*`-based (4 bytes/pixel RGBA) — there is no way for any caller to supply correctly-packed non-RGBA8 data (e.g. a 2-byte `Bgr565` texel, a 16-byte float `Vector4` texel) at all today. `Texture3D` alone has one `NOXNA SetDataPointerEXT(..., const void* data, int dataLength)` raw-bytes escape hatch; `Texture2D`/`TextureCube` have none (`Texture2D::SetDataRGBA` is RGBA8-only, not format-agnostic). Genuinely implementing "real GPU forwarding" for these 14 formats therefore needs, at minimum: (a) loosening the shared `ValidateFormat` for exactly these 14 values (reversing Task 176's contract, cross-backend); (b) adding/extending raw-or-typed `SetData`/`GetData` overloads for `Texture2D`/`TextureCube` (mirroring `Texture3D::SetDataPointerEXT`'s precedent) so a caller can actually supply/read non-RGBA8 pixel data; (c) correct bytes-per-pixel math throughout mip-level-size/`GetData`-buffer-size calculations (currently hardcoded to 4 everywhere); (d) the EasyGL-side format-mapping table this task's own note focuses on. (a)-(c) are cross-cutting API/architecture decisions well beyond "map an enum to GL internal formats in one backend file." **Question for the project owner:** (a) do the full job — extend the public `SetData`/`GetData` API surface (a real, generic-or-typed data path, not just `Color*`), loosen `ValidateFormat` for these 14 formats across all 4 backends, then implement EasyGL's GLES 3.2 format table (large, multi-file, cross-backend architecture change, arguably deserving its own dedicated multi-task plan rather than one line item); or (b) implement GPU-allocation-only forwarding — have `CreateTexture3D`/`CreateTextureCube` (and `CreateTexture`, if given format info) allocate the GPU texture with the correct internal format for these 14 values, verified via a backend-level test that bypasses the public `Texture2D`/`Texture3D`/`TextureCube` API (since the public API still can't supply/read the data) — narrower, but produces plumbing the public API can't use yet, and still requires deciding whether `ValidateFormat` moves at all; or (c) leave the current, already-documented, already-tested "Color-only" limitation as-is, and treat this task as superseded/closed-won't-do unless a future, properly-scoped architecture task (generic `SetData<T>`/raw-bytes API design, spanning all 4 backends) is planned separately. Not guessing on this per project's own risky-decision policy — leaving BLOCKED until the project owner picks a direction. Zero code changes made — this was investigation only. |
| 733 | Implement `FillMode::WireFrame` emulation on EasyGL via barycentric-coordinate geometry shader + `fwidth()` edge detection | ✅ | **CLOSED — already implemented, already tested; the task's own premise was stale.** Investigated before writing any code (per this project's own verify-first discipline). Confirmed `FillMode::WireFrame` is ALREADY emulated on EasyGL — not via a geometry shader, but via a simpler, already-shipped technique: `EasyGLGraphicsBackend::ApplyRasterizerState` sets a `wireframe_` flag when `fillMode==1`, and every 3D draw entry point (`DrawColoredPrimitives`/`DrawTexturedPrimitives`/etc.) calls `DrawWireframe()` first when that flag is set, which re-expands the triangle list/strip's index stream into a scratch `GL_LINES` element buffer (one edge-pair per triangle side) and issues a `GL_LINES` draw instead of `GL_TRIANGLES` — correctly handling both `TriangleList`/`TriangleStrip`, indexed and non-indexed, 16-bit and 32-bit indices, and `baseVertex`. This is real, complete, working code (`EasyGLGraphicsBackend.cpp`), not a stub — the task's own "N/A on GLES" framing being corrected was already out of date. **Also already has test coverage**: `EasyGL_FillMode_Solid` (registered in `CMakeLists.txt`, reusing `vulkan_fill_mode_test.cpp` verbatim per this project's own established backend-agnostic-test-reuse convention, e.g. Task 326) already exercises exactly this — a real triangle drawn Solid (centre must be RED), then WireFrame (centre must be BLACK, interior not rasterized), then reset to Solid (centre RED again) — and already passes 3/3 on a fresh run this session (confirmed directly, not assumed from a stale log). The test's own name is a slight misnomer (it validates all 3 sub-tests, not just Solid) but its actual assertions are exactly what this task needs. **Discriminating power independently verified**: sabotaged `ApplyRasterizerState` to force `wireframe_ = false` unconditionally — rebuilt and reran, and exactly the WireFrame sub-test failed as predicted (centre read RED instead of BLACK, 2/3 pass) while the two Solid sub-tests stayed correct; restored (confirmed clean `git diff`) and reconfirmed 3/3 pass. **Why the proposed barycentric-coordinate/geometry-shader technique was NOT implemented instead**: replacing a working, correct, already-tested implementation with a fundamentally different, more complex one for no behavioral gain would violate this project's own "don't add complexity beyond what's required" and "match XNA/FNA behavior over personal C++ preference" conventions. Real hardware `glPolygonMode(GL_LINE)` (what FNA/XNA's `FillMode::WireFrame` actually models) produces thin, non-anti-aliased edge lines — exactly what the existing `GL_LINES` re-expansion already produces — whereas a `fwidth()`-based barycentric-distance technique is the standard idiom for a THICK, anti-aliased stylized wireframe-overlay effect, a deliberately different, non-XNA-faithful visual. That technique remains a viable FUTURE option if a stylized overlay effect is ever explicitly wanted, but implementing it now would be speculative, unrequested scope, not a fix for any real gap. Wrote and then deleted a duplicate EasyGL-specific test file after discovering the existing reused-Vulkan-source test already covers this fully (per this project's reuse-over-duplication convention) — zero net files added. No code changes shipped (the sabotage-and-revert touched already-shipped code with zero net diff); no separate `ctest` regression needed since no production code changed net. |
| 734 | Add a non-geometry-shader barycentric fallback for GLES 3.2 drivers with unreliable geometry-shader support | ✅ | **CLOSED — moot, depended entirely on Task 733's premise, which Task 733 itself found to be stale.** This task exists solely as a fallback path for the geometry-shader-based `FillMode::WireFrame` implementation Task 733 was supposed to add. Task 733 investigated first and found `FillMode::WireFrame` is ALREADY correctly emulated on EasyGL via a completely different, simpler, already-shipped technique (`DrawWireframe`'s `GL_LINES` index re-expansion) that involves no geometry shader at all — and, per Task 733's own write-up, implementing the geometry-shader/barycentric approach was explicitly declined as unrequested scope replacing a working, XNA-faithful implementation. With no geometry-shader-based WireFrame implementation in existence (by design, not by omission), there is nothing for this task's "non-geometry-shader fallback" to fall back FROM — the existing `GL_LINES` technique already IS the universally-portable, driver-independent, no-geometry-shader-required implementation this task was trying to provide as a fallback. No code changes needed; no `docs/easygl_bugs.md` note needed since there is no unreliable-geometry-shader-driver risk to document (no geometry shader is used for wireframe at all). |

| 737 | Add explicit `TextureAddressMode::Mirror` regression test for the Task 269 SpriteBatch SamplerState fix | ✅ | **CLOSED — real, previously-unverified gap in test coverage closed; no production bug found (the underlying fix already covers Mirror correctly).** Task 269 fixed `SpriteBatch::Begin()`'s `SamplerState` having no effect on EasyGL by adding `EasyGLSpriteBatchBackend::SetSamplerFilter`/`SetSamplerAddressMode` overrides, and its own test (`easygl_texture_address_mode_test.cpp`) independently pixel-verified `Wrap` and `Clamp` at `U=1.25` — but never `Mirror`, even though `Mirror` is threaded through the exact same `pendingAddressU_`/`pendingAddressV_` fields and the exact same `SetSamplerAddressMode` call. Distinct from Task 296's existing `easygl_texture_address_mode_mirror_effect_test.cpp`, which verifies `Mirror` via `DrawUserPrimitives`+`DualTextureEffect` and `GraphicsDevice.SamplerStates[0]` directly — a completely different code path that never touches `ISpriteBatchBackend::SetSamplerAddressMode` at all. FNA has no `PointMirror` static preset (only `*Clamp`/`*Wrap` for `Point`/`Linear`/`Anisotropic`), so the new test builds a custom `SamplerState` (`Filter=Point`, `AddressU=AddressV=Mirror`), mirroring Task 296's own approach. Reused Task 269's exact methodology (2×1 Red\|Blue texture, `SpriteBatch::Draw` with a `sourceRectangle` 2× the texture width so sampled `U` spans `[0,2]`) but sampled at `U=1.6` instead of Task 269's `U=1.25` — deliberately, since `Mirror` and `Clamp` coincidentally agree at `U=1.25` (both land on the texture's last texel), which would make the check non-discriminating; at `U=1.6`, `Wrap`→fract(1.6)=0.6→Blue, `Clamp`→clamps to last texel→Blue, `Mirror`→reflects 2.0-1.6=0.4→Red — all three modes cleanly separated (matches Task 296's own `U=1.6` choice for the identical reason). New `examples/easygl_texture_address_mode_mirror_test.cpp`, registered as `EasyGL_TextureAddressMode_Mirror`: asserts `Mirror`→Red as the actual subject, and additionally asserts both `Wrap`/`Clamp`→Blue at the same sample point purely to confirm the point genuinely discriminates (not part of the pass/fail gate's headline claim, but still checked, unlike Task 685's SDL_Renderer test which only prints its context checks). Passes on the first attempt with the exact predicted RGB values. **Discriminating power independently verified via targeted sabotage-and-revert**: temporarily reduced `EasyGLSpriteBatchBackend::SetSamplerAddressMode` to a no-op (simulating Task 269's pre-fix silent-no-op state) — rebuilt and reran, and the test failed exactly as predicted (`Mirror` sample read Blue instead of Red, the same wrong answer a broken/reverted fix would produce); restored the two-line assignment (confirmed clean `git diff` — zero net production-code change from this task) and reconfirmed pass. Full regression (full rebuild first, `-k` to skip the known unrelated `cna_demo_xact` Content-copy error): `ctest` 4431/4434 passed, 2 skipped, 3 failed — exactly the established, already-documented EasyGL baseline (`EasyGL_MRT_TwoAttachments`, `EasyGL_GraphicsDevice_ReferenceStencil`, `easy-gl-resource-smoke-tests`, first documented at Task 902), exact name match, zero new failures; the new `EasyGL_TextureAddressMode_Mirror` test itself passes (the +1 test/+1 pass vs. the Task 902 baseline's 4430/4433). |

| 750 | Wire a Task-269-equivalent `SpriteBatch` `SamplerState` fix into Bgfx's `ISpriteBatchBackend` | ✅ | **Real bug confirmed and fixed — identical shape to Task 269 (EasyGL) and Task 665 (Vulkan).** `BgfxSpriteBatchBackend` never overrode `SetSamplerFilter`/`SetSamplerAddressMode` at all (silent no-op via `ISpriteBatchBackend`'s default empty bodies), so `SpriteBatch::Begin()`'s `SamplerState` had zero effect — every draw used whatever `samplerFlags_[0]` happened to already hold from a previous 3D draw (or the zero-initialized default), never the sampler actually requested. Unlike EasyGL/Vulkan's deferred-batch architecture, Bgfx's `SpriteBatchBackend::Draw()` submits immediately per call (no separate flush step) via `BgfxGraphicsBackend::SubmitSprite()`, which already read `samplerFlags_[0]` via `bgfx::setTexture(0, textureSampler, textureHandle, samplerFlags_[0])` — the per-slot sampler-flags plumbing (`ApplySamplerState()`, already used by the 3D draw paths) was already fully in place; it just was never invoked from the sprite path. Fixed by adding `SetSamplerFilter`/`SetSamplerAddressMode` overrides storing `pendingFilter_`/`pendingAddressU_`/`pendingAddressV_` (mirroring EasyGL's exact field names/defaults — `TextureFilter::Linear`/`TextureAddressMode::Clamp`, `SpriteBatch`'s own real default), and calling `graphicsBackend.ApplySamplerState(0, pendingFilter_, pendingAddressU_, pendingAddressV_, 1)` immediately before each `SubmitSprite()` call. **New test** `examples/bgfx_texture_address_mode_test.cpp` — a direct port of Task 269/665's identical 2×1 Red/Blue-texture, `sourceRectangle` 2× width, `U≈1.25`-readback, `PointWrap`-vs-`PointClamp` methodology (retrying the backbuffer read per this project's established Bgfx first-read-per-frame convention): both checks pass with the exact predicted colors on the first attempt. **Discriminating power independently verified**: `git stash`-reverted both changed files and rebuilt — the pre-fix code produced the exact predicted symptom, both `PointWrap` and `PointClamp` reading the **identical** wrong value `(254,0,1)` (proving `Wrap`/`Clamp` addressing genuinely never took effect either way, matching Task 665's exact pre-fix Vulkan symptom shape); restored and reconfirmed both checks pass. Full regression: `CnaTests` (Bgfx) 4276/4278 passed, 2 skipped, 0 failed (exact baseline match); `ctest -R Bgfx` 52/54 (2 failures are the already-documented pre-existing `Bgfx_RenderTarget2D_MsaaResolve` Xvfb limitation and `Bgfx_RenderTarget2D_MipChain`, reconfirmed genuinely flaky in this sandbox via 3 direct isolated reruns — 2/3 passed, unrelated to this fix). |

## Phase 73 — Vulkan: full 2D+3D pixel-verified parity (gap closure)

> Runs fourth (last of the four backend-focused phases; WebGPU remains parked after this).
> Vulkan already has substantially more infrastructure than Bgfx — per-slot samplers (Task 118),
> custom Effect/SPIR-V (119), instancing (111), wireframe (112), MSAA (147), RenderTargetCube
> (142), Texture3D/Cube backends (143), scissor (329), depth bias (328), and stock-effect SPIR-V
> shaders for all 5 effects (102–109) — so this phase is sized as gap-closure, not full
> replication, plus the two SpriteBatch bugs found this session.

## Phase 74 — XNA compiled effect bytecode (`.fx`) support via MojoShader

Opened by Task 352's full-support decision. Full research/reasoning behind this phase lives in
`docs/fx-bytecode-support-plan.md` — read that first. Dedicated task-number block (`10200`+),
mirroring the WebGPU backend's `10000`+ convention (Phases 56–69), since this is comparable in
scope: a new vendored native dependency, two independent per-backend GPU-shader-translation
paths, an unknown-feasibility third backend, and real test-fixture sourcing with no XNA Content
Pipeline tooling available in this project.

| #     | Task                                                                                                   | Status | Notes                                                                                                    |
| ----- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| 10200 | Vendor MojoShader (`third_party/mojoshader` submodule) + `cmake/ThirdPartyMojoShader.cmake` + `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md` entry | ⬜ | Source confirmed available/readable locally at `/rv/data/library/github.com/u3d-community/U3D/Source/ThirdParty/MojoShader` (zlib-licensed); needs its own proper upstream submodule remote, not a copy from that path |
| 10201 | C++ wrapper around `mojoshader_effects.c`'s parser: compiled-effect byte blob → CNA-native technique/pass/parameter reflection data | ⬜ | The container-parsing half of "full support" — independent of any GPU shader translation |
| 10202 | EasyGL path: compile MojoShader `GLSL`/`GLSL120` profile output via EasyGL's existing shader-program creation path | ⬜ | Closest to free — MojoShader's own primary supported output target |
| 10203 | Vulkan path — vendoring/feasibility: decide and vendor a GLSL→SPIR-V compiler (glslang is the default candidate; only present locally as Android NDK/Flatpak build tooling today, not a repo-vendorable checkout) | ⬜ | Blocking prerequisite for Task 10204; MojoShader has no SPIR-V output profile |
| 10204 | Vulkan path implementation: MojoShader GLSL output → SPIR-V (via Task 10203's compiler) → Vulkan pipeline creation | ⬜ | Depends on Task 10203 |
